# Grocy compatibility

The baseline is Grocy 4.5.0, commit `21aa5eab218c8466f90729154cb293fffbe10c21`. The OpenAPI file and its checksum are in `contracts/`.

## Release target

The release uses Python. Keep `GROCY_USE_NATIVE_PHP=false`. This applies to the API, files, input filtering, printing, barcode lookup, and all business rules. The release image does not install PHP.

PHP is only a test reference. Original `config.php` files and PHP plugins are reference inputs. The Python application uses `GROCY_` environment variables or `.env`. It does not execute those PHP files. Legacy features must have Python implementations.

The old native adapter remains available for test investigation. A test that passes with `GROCY_USE_NATIVE_PHP=true` does not establish Python parity. The older [backend audit](backend-parity-audit.md) records many results from that adapter. Those results are historical evidence about the reference behavior.

## Current coverage

The Python API provides the 86 contract operations and reads for the 33 listable entities. Route coverage does not prove full behavior parity. The separate `/api/v2` routes provide application features such as nutrition, API key management, and meal completion.

The database uses the pinned Grocy views, triggers, permission hierarchy, and allocation counters. Imports preserve source data and counters. See [migration](migration.md) for upgrade and recovery limits.

The current repair work covers these confirmed differences:

- Stock undo reads booking arrays and refreshes the entry list.
- Stock forms save price, store, purchase date, note, location, and open state.
- Shopping rows store stock-unit quantities. The web response and paper output convert them to the selected display unit. The Python add extension converts incoming display quantities before storage. Shopping purchases use the stored stock quantity and one database transaction per item. A retry does not purchase a removed item again.
- Calendar export downloads the authenticated calendar document.
- Chore and battery forms send the legacy date and time format.
- Stock list filters use the same query rules as generic lists.
- Compatibility recipe routes use the legacy serving rules. The recipe card saves desired servings and uses these routes for consumption and shopping writes.
- Input filtering runs in Python. It preserves the checked HTML, entity, null, boolean, and numeric value rules.
- Permission replacement preserves the legacy delete and batch failure order.
- Custom field writes preserve earlier successful values when a later field fails. Field reads preserve numeric name and object-key comparison rules.
- Numeric settings keys retain the legacy distinction between a full settings response and a single-setting response.
- Recipe copy, consumption, and shopping writes preserve the checked transaction and partial failure rules.
- Barcode lookup supports [Python plugins](python-barcode-plugins.md), image downloads, and the legacy write order.
- File responses use content-based MIME types and preserve the checked stream and header rules.
- API key and session checks preserve the checked expiry, byte value, credential order, and use-record rules.
- HEAD, OPTIONS, method errors, and CORS headers use the legacy API response rules.
- A configured API base path supports the checked route, authentication, error page, and encoded file rules. Python extension routes keep their JSON errors and CORS rules under the prefix.
- Demo and prerelease modes use their own database files. The tests check reads, writes, missing schemas, return to production data, and initialization at startup. File access, image caches, and barcode image downloads also use the selected mode directory. The directory uses the default locale or explicit demo suffix. An explicit empty suffix retains the native shared directory rule.
- The API specification includes current custom entities and uses the configured URL rules for server and key management links.
- Locale selection uses the saved locale, request language preferences, and configured default. Translation output preserves empty entries and plural forms. Recipe copies use the selected translation.
- User profile errors and self-edit ID checks follow the checked legacy rules. The web profile form retains the saved photo.
- `GROCY_DEFAULT_USER_SETTINGS` supplies Python configuration defaults. Saved values have priority, and recipe views use the same defaults.
- Python authentication modes support API keys, sessions, and reverse proxy identities. Session mode also works with web login and logout.
- Python LDAP login checks the directory password, uses the returned user ID attribute, and creates users with the configured default permissions. Real directory tests compare these rules with legacy login. TLS tests check LDAPS, StartTLS, and rejection of an untrusted certificate.
- Picture responses use libgd from Python. Reference checks compare exact bytes for PNG, JPEG, GIF, WebP, and BMP, including palette images, EXIF rotation, and fractional resize values. The release image includes libgd and does not include PHP.
- Recipe printer labels expose the native fields. Empty printer paths return the checked legacy error.
- The web app applies server feature flags to navigation, page controls, additional modern routes, quick actions, Today requests, household choices, and data management choices. Native direct pages authenticate independently of menu visibility. String values retain PHP truth rules: `"0"` disables a feature, but `"off"` enables it.
- Stock forms, stock tables, shopping tools, and label choices use the related tracking and printer flags. Disabled entry fields retain the legacy submission rules, including price `0`, location `1`, and a closed entry.
- The product editor includes freezing warnings, opened-stock treatment, stock label defaults, automatic label reprinting, and purchase price type. Product fields use the checked legacy hidden defaults when tracking is disabled.
- Web builds, page links, API requests, and authenticated downloads support a configured path prefix. The release web server and Vite proxy use the same prefix as the Python API.
- Product and recipe editors support picture upload, preview, replacement, and removal. They save the record and custom fields before upload. An upload retry reuses a new record. File deletion failure does not cancel the record update. Picture requests use authentication headers.
- Python configuration exposes the configured energy unit, code type, and calendar week-number setting. The product energy field uses the configured unit without a value conversion.
- Barcode forms apply opening, location, and price tracking flags. Scan defaults accept numeric values and the legacy browser boolean forms, including `"on"`.
- Opening the web root uses the configured `ENTRY_PAGE`. Native page names map to the related web routes. Today remains available at `/today`.
- Stock shortages use the server result. Minimum gauges exclude opened stock when required, and parent product displays and consumption forms use the aggregated stock amounts.
- Stock forms select the default purchase or consume unit and use the resolved product conversions. Amounts and prices use the checked legacy rounding rules. The forms support unit prices, total prices, gross tare weights, and exact consumption amounts. Tare success messages show the booked stock change.
- The barcode page and barcode editor support camera scans with the six legacy formats: EAN-8, EAN-13, Code 39, Code 128, Data Matrix, and QR. Camera selection uses the legacy storage key. The scanner applies camera and torch flags, stops after the first result, and releases the stream on close, camera change, or page change. Browser checks decoded official ZXing images through a virtual video stream. Physical camera and torch checks remain open.
- Legacy routes decode the URI path before matching, then decode each captured argument. Barcode comparisons check escaped percent values, product selection, and all five stock actions. Real HTTP checks also confirm that these values select the expected product. Directly encoded slashes retain the legacy route rejection.
- Stock preferences expose price input and display precision, quick consume amounts, and purchase dates. Preference checkboxes read the legacy browser boolean forms. Purchase and inventory forms omit the purchase date when it is disabled, and price history uses the saved display precision.
- Calendar export retains the legacy error when a time-zone transition falls outside UTC years 0000 through 9999. Boundary comparisons cover the historical Bucharest offset and valid year-zero exports. Date-list tests remove their year-zero tasks so later calendar checks use their own data.
- The web app provides the 33 legacy languages. It reads the saved language, loads the pinned translation catalog, and applies its plural rules and text direction. Navigation, screen labels, form labels, and action messages use the translation helper. User names, product names, custom captions, and saved field values retain their original text. Quantity units support saved plural forms. Text without a legacy translation keeps its English fallback; remaining wording and date displays still need review.
- The saved night-mode settings control all web pages. The appearance form supports system, on, off, automatic time ranges, and ranges across midnight. It retains the native exclusive time limits, strict `HH:mm` parser, `24:00` value, and invalid-input rules. Checks run every 60 seconds in production and every four seconds in other modes. The internal enabled flag is saved when it changes. Before login, the app uses the system color scheme. Twenty recorded results from the pinned legacy JavaScript check the schedule rules.
- New-product presets expose location, group, quantity unit, due days, opened-stock treatment, and stock label type. The editor applies them only to new records and uses the preset unit for all four unit fields. A stock-unit choice fills other empty unit choices and retains an existing choice. Existing records keep their saved values, and disabled tracking fields retain their submission rules.
- The header clock follows its saved setting, updates each second, and uses the legacy long and short formats. The short format is shown below 576 pixels. It uses the catalog's `moment_locale` value, including its English fallback. Clock code loads when the setting is enabled. Its interval stops when the clock is disabled or removed.
- Product descriptions, recipe preparation, and equipment descriptions display formatted text, lists, tables, and images. Python prepares a display copy through the authenticated content endpoint. Stored descriptions and compatibility API responses retain their values. The display copy removes active scripts and adds an iframe sandbox without same-origin access.
- Recipe and equipment views support full-screen cards. Recipe links retain the `#fullscreen` form. The controls remain visible during scrolling. Keyboard focus stays in the card, Escape closes it, and closing restores page access and prior focus. Automatic data refresh waits while a card is in full-screen mode. Equipment manuals display as PDFs and retain authenticated downloads.
- The appearance form exposes both screen-on settings. The first user action enables the saved preference. Always-on has priority over the conditional setting. The app releases its lock when the page is hidden, the setting is disabled, or the conditional card closes. It can acquire a new lock when the page becomes visible. A late request also releases its lock after close or removal. Browsers without the native API use the legacy NoSleep.js 0.12.0 method.
- Recipe cards include every nested recipe once and sort the included cards by name without case sensitivity. Python reads the pinned recipe views and keeps separate ingredient rows, nesting amounts, unit conversions, groups, notes, variable amounts, stock status, prices, and calories. The web reader supports keyboard tab selection and the saved ingredient checkbox and product-group settings. Check marks remain local to the page and reset on reload. Desired servings start with the saved value. Changes are saved in order before stock or shopping actions can run. Consumption can proceed when stock is insufficient, as in the legacy version.
- The Python recipe overview returns normal recipes in name order without case sensitivity. It includes ingredient names, due scores, missing ingredient counts, and all three stock states. Internal meal-plan recipes remain available to their API users but do not appear in this overview. The web list and gallery support ingredient search and stock filters. The list supports sorting and an optional desired-servings column. Gallery pictures use authenticated requests.
- Recipe preferences expose the legacy side-by-side setting, with its enabled default. The page selects the first normal recipe and supports the legacy `recipe`, `search`, `status`, `tab`, and `embedded` query fields. It saves the list or gallery choice under `recipes_last_tab_id`. When side-by-side display is disabled, selection opens a full-screen dialog. Closing through the Recipes link preserves filters and restores focus. Closing during loading also prevents a late response from opening the dialog again. On phones, an explicit recipe selection waits for the recipe data before it scrolls and moves focus.
- Recipe consumption requires confirmation and shows the legacy partial-stock and single-unit-check rules. Shopping confirmation lists missing ingredient positions from Python, including nested and repeated rows. Each row starts checked. Unchecked rows supply `excludedProductIds` to the compatibility shopping route. Cancellation makes no stock or shopping write. Escape closes the confirmation, keeps an expanded recipe open, and returns focus to the action button.
- Recipe printing includes every nested card, with each included recipe starting on a separate page. It prints pictures, ingredient amounts, notes, costs, and preparation while it hides navigation, lists, editors, and stock-status text. Long preparation text can span pages. Printing leaves normal full-screen mode before it calls the browser printer. A recipe opened in a dialog stays open after printing. Print colors remain readable with a saved dark theme.
- Recipe copy reads the new ID from the Python response and selects it at `/recipes?recipe=ID`. It clears prior filters, closes an open recipe dialog, and keeps the configured path prefix. The side-by-side view displays the copied recipe; list-only mode selects its row. This also works when the source is an included recipe.
- Recipes can add meal-plan entries from the list, gallery, or reader. The form starts with the local date, the selected normal recipe, one serving, and the first section in the saved sort order. It permits a different recipe, date, section, and fractional serving count. Servings and section choices remain available when the form is reopened on the page. The date and recipe reset on reopening. The minimum and step use the saved amount precision. Successful writes use the Python compatibility route and refresh meal data. Failed saves retain input for retry. The meal-plan feature flag controls these actions.

Recipe code images use Python translations of the pinned Data Matrix and Code 128 encoders. The recipe download routes preserve the checked size rules, image geometry, PNG output, cache headers, download name, and query value rules. The legacy GUI route retains its login redirect and HEAD response. The application API checks recipe access. Release images use libgd to retain the native PNG bytes; local environments without libgd use Pillow with the same checked pixels. The source package includes the original license texts and attribution.

The recipe list, gallery, and reader provide code downloads. Included recipe cards use their own IDs. Label controls use the printer feature flag and the configured browser or server mode. Browser requests support JSON and the pinned jQuery form encoding, including nested fields. Printer parameters override payload fields. The browser does not send the Grocy API key to the printer webhook. Server mode uses the existing Python label route without a second browser print request.

Python also provides the legacy product, chore, battery, and stock-entry code image routes. Stock-entry codes read the saved product ID and stock ID. Missing entries retain the checked empty-field code. GUI routes require authentication; the application routes also require their own domain permission. The same code menu serves product lists, product details, stock tables, stock entries, the stock journal, chores, batteries, and recipes. The separate labels page uses these printer actions and lists normal recipes. Its tab panels refer to the correct tab labels.

The release web server and development proxy forward all five legacy code image routes and stock-entry paper label routes to Python. They retain query values, binary output, download headers, HEAD responses, authentication, and error responses. Resource routing uses the configured path prefix literally, including dots and plus signs. Product form routes still open the web app. The web image builds its escaped resource pattern from `VITE_BASE_PATH`.

Stock-entry paper labels use the native product name, due-date text, code size, font sizes, and image placement. The document embeds its PNG and the pinned Roboto fonts. It can open and print without further authenticated asset requests. It retains the missing-entry output and remains available when its code image fails. The GUI route retains login and HEAD behavior; the application route requires stock access. The stock-entry menu can open this document with the label-printer flag disabled.

Thermal shopping actions send a print request for the selected list. Their dialog reads and saves the header preference, shows connection status, reports printer errors, and permits retry with the retained options. Closing a pending dialog does not permit a second job before the first request finishes. Successful output closes the dialog and reports that the request was sent. The labels page also permits thermal printing when a label printer is disabled. Printer JSON responses remain inside the application.

Shopping paper printing supports table and list layouts. Its dialog saves the header, product-group, and layout settings. Python prepares the selected list, including completed items, notes, saved quantity units, and displayed custom fields. It applies the saved round-up rule. The browser applies the native number, unit-plural, and print-time formats. The table retains the native product-and-note order and repeated group headings. The document uses the pinned print styles, logo, and embedded fonts. The options dialog closes before printing. Closing during document loading cancels the request. Print completion removes the temporary document and returns focus to the Print button.

Shopping notes use the pinned formatted editor, including its text styles, lists, links, images, tables, video, HTML source view, and full-screen controls. Python serves the editor document and retains the stored description. Save and Clear use the normal object API. Clear stores the native empty paragraph. Source edits enter the notes value when source view closes. Printing uses the current draft without saving it. It retains the native rule that hides printed notes when the page initially had none. The editor follows the selected language, writing direction, and night mode. Its content cannot read the parent page. Only messages from the active editor frame can change the notes controls.

Shopping lists now have create, edit, and delete controls. The selector shows all lists in the native name order, with counts that include completed items. Selection is stored in the URL. Legacy `/shoppinglist?list=...` links open the selected list. The default list has no delete action. Confirmed deletion returns to the default list. Clear done items retains the open rows.

The list form includes the fourteen native custom field types. It retains the native blank defaults, local date initialization, required checkbox acknowledgement, dirty-field writes, file names, and save order. Creation saves the list before its custom fields. Editing saves custom fields before the name. A failed upload can be repeated on the created list. File and picture uploads also work on local HTTP pages. Python paper output can resolve custom image files.

Python serves legacy named file links for all five native file groups. The first encoded name selects the stored file. The second sets the displayed name. GET and HEAD retain the native content type, inline file name, cache, and image-size rules. File links from printed shopping lists pass through the release web server and the development proxy, including a configured path prefix. Session cookies and API keys can authorize them. Missing files use the translated page message and five-second return. File preflight requests need no login and retain the native cross-origin headers.

Shopping rows have an edit action in Plan mode. A detailed create form supports products or note-only rows, a destination list, selected quantity units, multiline notes, and all existing custom field controls. It converts displayed quantities to stock units at the saved amount precision. The form saves the row before its custom fields and retains completed status. A failed field save keeps the draft; retry reuses a newly created row. Successful moves refresh both lists and select the destination. The single-list flag forces list `1`. Legacy `/shoppinglistitem/{id}` and `/shoppinglistitem/new` links open the form.

Item links support `product`, `product-name`, `list`, and `amount`. Name prefill checks barcode text before product names. A saved product takes priority over a query product. An edit amount overrides the stock quantity before display conversion. The presence of `updateexistingproduct`, including the value `false`, adds the stock amount to the first matching row in the selected list. It retains that row's unit and completed state and replaces its note. New prefilled merge links retain the checked native load rule: the form starts with one purchase unit, including when the link supplies another amount.

The `InplaceAddBarcodeToExistingProduct` link starts the barcode and shopping row saves independently. A barcode failure permits the row save. A row failure retains a saved barcode. The form waits for both requests before it permits retry. Retry repeats only unfinished steps. After a successful merge, retry cannot add the amount again, including when the row lookup or a custom field failed. The form reads the affected row ID before saving custom fields because the native merge API returns no ID. The compatibility response remains 204. The Python form uses a valid purchase unit for a plain numeric-product prefill; the checked native form left its unit empty and its hidden stock amount invalid. The native return from creation can also leave an invalid hidden amount for a product with one unit, then save zero while the visible amount is one. The Python form retains the valid displayed quantity. Embedded window messages still need work.

The detailed shopping form can select products by name, barcode, or product Grocycode. Barcode input is case-insensitive and retains the checked native suffix matching behavior. Enter and blur resolve the input and prepare the purchase unit. The camera control uses the existing scanner and its feature flags. A result stops the stream and moves focus to Amount after the camera closes. Escape from the camera or unknown-product dialog retains the shopping draft.

Unknown codes have the native create, assign, create-with-barcode, external lookup, and scan-again actions, with the P, B, A, E, and C keys. A code or name that belongs to an unavailable product retains the native stock error. External lookup reads the display name from Python, including custom Python plugins. Empty and unknown plugin settings hide the action. No result and plugin errors permit further input or retry. The application form retains its list and draft during barcode assignment; the checked native B action reloaded the form and selected list `1` after starting on list `2`.

Legacy `/product/new` and `/product/{id}` links open the current catalog editor. Its workflow return path stays inside the application. Name creation fills the name. Creation with a barcode leaves the name empty and saves the barcode when the shopping item is submitted. External lookup uses `add=true`, then opens the created product for review before it returns to shopping. Return paths carry the selected list. Product editing also retains the checked native number-picker default: a null energy value becomes one. New products keep the zero energy default.

Product reference choices come from Python. Locations, stores, groups, and parent candidates must be active. Parent candidates must have no parent. An edit excludes the current product. Any child, including an inactive child, disables the parent control. A disabled parent control is omitted from the save. New location lists use SQLite name order; edit location lists and other reference lists use name order without case sensitivity.

New products can select any stock unit, including an inactive unit. Their other unit controls contain active units. For existing products, purchase, consume, and price units must also have a resolved conversion to the product. Stock units use that conversion restriction after any stock log entry, including an entry that was undone. Consume and price units are required. Unavailable saved choices appear empty. Valid stock unit IDs fill empty unit controls. Empty optional product references are sent as empty strings, as in the native form.

The displayed purchase conversion factor follows the resolved unit pair. A unit selection alone does not write that factor again. An explicit factor change retains the existing conversion save behavior. Product option loading blocks saves and has a Retry control. Retry retains the form draft. The options read checks the product ID without parsing unrelated numeric fields. This permits repair of text values accepted by the legacy compatibility API.

Product forms read the selected parent before they apply its minimum-stock rule. A parent that sums child minimums disables the child's Minimum stock control. Its visible draft value stays in the form, but the save omits that field and retains the stored value. Changing to a parent without this setting, or clearing the parent, enables the control. A failed parent read retains the current control state and draft. Retry reads the parent again. Responses from a previous selection cannot change the current selection's control state.

Copy retains another native initialization rule. Setting the copied parent does not disable Minimum stock. The first copy save includes its copied minimum. A later edit reads the parent and disables the control when required. An explicit parent selection in the copy form also applies the parent's rule.

Product number controls retain the native required values and limits. Due days use whole numbers. Purchase due days and freezing due days permit minus one. Opening and thawing due days start at zero. Minimum stock, container weight, energy, and quick amounts use the saved amount precision. Quick amounts must be at least one step. The native number fields require a value except for Energy. Disabled controls retain their save rules.

Editing rounds stored amount fields for display with the native browser number format. Copy fills its source numbers after that initial formatting step and retains their original precision. Invalid copied values must be corrected before saving. Empty required source numbers stay empty in a copy. Arrow Up and Arrow Down change a product number by one, including fractional amounts. From an empty field, Up starts at one and Down starts at zero.

An empty Energy control submits empty text, including on creation. SQLite retains that text value. The compatibility read converts empty text to null, as the native PDO connection does. The native number picker therefore shows one when that product is reopened. A new product keeps the normal zero Energy default.

Product number fields have Increase and Decrease buttons. Each button changes the value by one and uses the same empty-field rules as the keyboard. Values are not clamped at the field limit; an invalid result blocks saving. The buttons retain the native rule for disabled inputs: they can change the visible draft, but the save still omits that input. Enabling the input permits the changed value to be saved. The buttons have accessible names and support Tab, Enter, and Space without submitting the form. Native browser spin controls are hidden, as in the legacy page.

The product form has a stock journal dialog. It retains the product draft while loading, filtering, retrying, and undoing bookings. Closing restores focus to the journal button. The stock feature flag controls the action. Both `/journal` and the legacy `/stockjournal` route use the same reader. The presence of `embedded` hides the product choice, including `embedded=false`.

Python reads all journal rows within the selected date range from the pinned journal view. It uses the native six-month default and strict date boundary. Valid month text retains the native SQLite modifier rules, including signed values. Active products appear in the filter; inactive products remain readable by ID. Locations include inactive choices. Rows retain user display names, stock units, notes, spoiled state, linked booking IDs, and stock custom values.

The journal supports text, transaction type, location, and user filters, column sorting, date ranges, and product selection. Location and user filters use the selected display text, as in the native table. Their words can match in either order. Global search supports straight and curly quoted phrases. Clear resets the date range and retains the product only in embedded views. Amounts use the saved display precision and unit plural rules. Amount sorting uses the formatted text and unit, as in the native journal. A successful undo changes the linked rows on screen and disables their undo controls. A failed read or undo retains the current inputs for retry. Product code actions use the existing download and printer controls.

The product form also has a stock entry dialog. The stock overview links to the legacy `/stockentries` page. The dialog and page share the Python entry read, product and location filters, sorting, and custom product and stock columns. Embedded views retain their product on Clear. The active lookup lists supply names and choices. Inactive references keep their stock rows with empty display names. Entry prices start in the configured price unit. After a booking, the affected product rows use the purchase unit, as in the native refresh code. The special due date displays as Never. Timestamp is hidden in the default table.

The entry location filter also matches words in either order. Entry amount and price sorting remain numeric. Text sorting uses the browser locale. The native Note sorter compares the cell HTML, with its stock row ID before the note. Entry Note sorting retains that rule. Shared stock tables keep their cell text on one line, with internal scrolling when required.

The entry, journal, and summary tables share saved layout controls. They use the native `datatables_state_{tableId}` and `datatables_rowGroup_{tableId}` settings. Layouts retain original column indexes, the `ColReorder` permutation, visibility, and multi-column sort order. Hidden technical columns and disabled feature columns keep their native positions. A changed column count discards the old layout, as in DataTables. Timestamp is hidden by default in entries and can be shown from Table options. Search text is cleared before the layout is saved.

Table options changes apply immediately. Headers support dragging. Earlier and Later buttons also change column order with the keyboard or on phones. Grouping has a fixed ascending group order before the saved sort order. Group headers can collapse their rows. Group state accepts native text column indexes and numeric indexes. The saved group position is interpreted with the current column order on reload. A failed setting write retains the changed controls and offers Retry. Reset requires confirmation and removes group and layout settings before it restores the defaults. Retry after a partial reset does not repeat a completed group delete. A successful reset also clears an earlier save error. Escape closes only the options dialog and restores focus.

A column move retains the displayed groups and row order until the next table draw. Table options shows the column now at the saved group position. Sorting, filtering, a search key event, or a group toggle draws groups from that position. Changing column visibility retains the displayed groups. Moving a column also restores the original cached cells and their action controls. Journal Undo display changes and entry refresh display changes therefore disappear after a move. Entry row colors and hidden consumed rows retain their current state. A later read restores the saved record values.

Entry actions can consume the complete selected entry, consume it as spoiled, or mark it as open. Consume uses the saved stock ID, amount, location, and exact-amount flag. Opening is disabled for opened entries, tare products, and products that disable opening. Successful actions refresh stock and related recipe and shopping data. Undo remains available in the dialog and success message. Failed writes retain their controls for retry. Undo returns focus to the refreshed table. Closing retains the product form draft and restores focus to the entry button.

Entry editing uses one shared form in the stock entry table, product detail ledger, and legacy `/stockentry/{id}` page. Each opening reads the saved entry, active locations and stores, and stock custom values. The form waits for saved precision settings before it prepares number values. Amount must be positive. Purchase date, due date, and location are required when their controls are visible. Price is optional. An empty price saves as null. Number buttons change values by one. Due-date changes select label reprint. The Never overdue control sets the special date and makes its input read-only.

The form retains the native hidden submissions: price zero, location one, and a closed entry. Hidden due dates retain their saved values. The purchase date remains visible and required. Custom values and files save under the successful edit transaction ID. A failed record save keeps the draft. After the record is saved, its controls stay disabled while a failed custom field or file can retry. Retry does not repeat the stock booking or a completed custom field. Label requests start after the record save and do not cancel custom field work if printing fails. A custom field retry does not repeat that label request.

Closing a nested entry editor keeps the product draft. Escape closes only that level and returns focus to its Edit button. A second Escape closes the stock entry table and returns to the product form. The direct entry page reloads saved values after a save or undo. Each reload uses a separate read so that an older response cannot replace the current saved state.

Opening dates retain their stored date or timestamp precision in stock entries and bookings. New opening records use a date. Editing an already opened entry retains an imported timestamp, including midnight. Closing clears the opening date. Undo restores the previous value and its precision.

The legacy `/stockjournal/summary` page reads all dates and groups non-undone bookings by product, transaction type, and user. It uses the pinned summary view, including its product, unit, and user joins. Inactive products can have totals but do not appear in the product choices. The product, user, and transaction type URL limits apply before local table filters. Empty limits match no rows. Clearing local filters keeps those URL limits. Embedded mode hides the product control. The main journal links to the unrestricted summary.

The summary displays saved amount precision and unit names. Its four columns sort with the browser locale. Amount uses the native text sort with formatted numbers and units. Product filtering uses the complete displayed name. User, transaction type, and global search use native smart search rules, including straight and curly quoted phrases. The table retains its headings on phones and supports keyboard scrolling. Opening the summary again reads current totals.

Stock compaction keeps a common stock ID on split rows at other locations. A later purchase can change that ID when it combines matching entries. Transfer undo still resolves the moved row after that change.

The core stock table cells now use separate native search, sort, and group values. Journal and summary search includes their number markup. Journal search also includes product, undone, spoiled, and relative date markup. Native entity decoding removes markup from a cell only when the cell contains an HTML entity. Entry amounts and prices use numeric comparison. An empty raw price retains the native non-number comparison. Entry dates use HTML text comparison. Other core cells use the native locale comparison. Group identity uses the complete cell value. Equal due dates can therefore have separate groups because their cells contain different entry IDs. Purchased-date groups retain their separate plain date source. Undone product groups retain their own identity, strike-through, timestamp, and relative label.

Entry and journal dates now show the native relative text. Date-only values use 23:59:59. Never, Unknown, Today, invalid dates, and empty dates retain the checked source rules. Labels use the selected Moment locale and its digit rules. Opening the table and successful reads provide the time used for these labels. Numeric custom fields keep empty values blank and parse a numeric prefix before formatting. Currency values use the saved display precision.

All three stock tables now retain their previous complete row order when new sort values are equal. Sorting runs before local filters, including rows that the filters hide. Reset clears that order history. A route reload starts with the saved sort and the new source order. The first order waits for imported table settings. Summary row identity uses the saved product, user, and transaction type, before translation.

Revision 0024 restores the native stock index on product, open state, due date, and amount. This also restores the source order used by the entry view before client sorting. The index is present on new databases and added to saved databases. The API reader retains its original display-name joins.

Revision 0025 restores both native stock-log indexes. One covers stock ID, transaction type, and amount. The other covers product, due date, purchase date, transaction type, stock ID, and undone state. A journal read for one product now retains the native index order when creation timestamps are equal. An unrestricted read retains its native table scan order. The journal query keeps its existing timestamp order without an added row-ID order. New and saved databases receive the same indexes.

Revision 0026 restores the six remaining native performance indexes. These cover parent products, the native parent-ID expression and active state, recipe names and types, chore and battery IDs and active states, and the resolved unit-conversion cache keys. The product form conversion reader now retains the native cache order. The migration keeps saved cache rows, their IDs, and their allocation counter. The existing barcode unique constraint has the same binary key and uniqueness as the native named barcode index. It does not need a second index.

Journal Undo now retains the original table values for search, sorting, and groups. Only the row display changes. The displayed Undo timestamp uses the browser clock and selected Moment locale. Only the clicked row receives relative date text; linked rows retain an empty relative label. Clear filter reads saved rows even when the date and product parameters stay the same. Table Reset and journal reopening also read saved rows. A failed reload keeps the current display and offers Retry. Initial values wait for the read, translations, custom fields, and saved table settings.

Stock entry actions now also retain the original table values. A refresh changes the displayed amount, dates, location, store, price, note, and open control. Product names, unit labels, custom values, sort values, filters, and groups retain their original source. Action amounts and stock IDs also retain their original values. Refresh reads only the product rows present under the current table filters. A fully consumed row stays hidden in the table. Refresh of a previously hidden row reloads the table, including a restored entry with a new row ID. Table Reset reads saved rows and clears prior action errors. Entry Clear filter changes the local filters without a read. A failed refresh retains completed display changes and offers a separate read Retry without repeating the booking.

The native refresh assigns each returned location to every Consume control. Python retains this behavior. A later Consume can therefore send the location from another refreshed row. Both runtimes now reject the checked wrong-location request with the native missing-transaction message.

The Consume compatibility path now checks total product stock, including child stock, before it applies the location and stock-entry limits. It rounds both amounts to two decimal places for this check. The selected entries can supply less than the requested amount. The response then contains only the actual bookings. Child substitution retains the native conversion direction and missing-conversion behavior. Tare products retain the native gross and exact-amount checks. A zero stock change produces no transaction rows.

Compatibility Consume reads the native `stock_next_use` view and writes its original booking fields. It uses floating-point arithmetic and native PDO parameter text. Booking and transaction reads retain the saved fractional values. Each booking write commits before the related entry update or delete. A failed later statement therefore keeps an earlier booking. Automatic shopping updates run after the stock writes and before the API reads the result transaction. They can remain saved even when the API reports a missing transaction. A failed shopping-list check also keeps earlier stock writes. Recipes use the same rules within their existing transaction. Their ingredient failure still rolls back the complete ingredient change. Chore consumption uses the native separate commits. Other application callers retain the existing stock service rules.

The remaining entry workflows, external embedded window messages, and remaining journal row actions still need work. Formatted custom-field search, sorting, and groups require further comparison. Further row color and partial-read cases also need comparison. Other legacy query flags and array parameters need comparison. Names with markup in conversion descriptions, more locale cases, and external embedded-page layout also need comparison. Other product-card placements need comparison. Native table paging is disabled.

Product names in stock entries and the journal now open the product overview dialog. The dialog reads fresh saved product values. It shows the native stock and opened amounts, child totals, stock value, location, purchase and use dates, prices, shelf life, and spoil rate. Products with no own stock hide their own amount. Feature flags control the price, location, shelf-life, and shopping controls. The description uses the Python display filter and has Show more and Show less. Its expansion state stays set when the overview reopens on the same page. Pictures use the authenticated picture reader.

The overview opens stock entries, the journal, and the shopping form as child dialogs. The shopping action selects the product, list one, and merge mode. Closing a child returns focus to its control. Closing the overview keeps the parent draft and filters. Embedded Edit product opens a separate saved-product form. Save and Cancel return to the stock table and reload its values. They keep an existing parent product draft. The return clears local filters and returns focus to the table. On a full stock page, Edit product opens the legacy product route with a return URL. Read errors have Retry. A successful retry returns focus to Close.

The Python compatibility product reader now reads `uihelper_product_details` and `stock_current`. It retains saved price precision and returns a numeric spoil rate. Related product, unit, location, and barcode rows retain their stored fields and values. Price history reads `products_price_history` in native date order. It keeps full price precision and all saved store fields, including inactive state. The web chart uses the pinned Chart.js 2.9.4 and trend-line scripts with their licenses. Store series, price-unit factors, currency labels, the hidden trend series, and the visible trend line follow the legacy chart. A data table makes its values available to screen readers. The chart has enough height for the phone labels. The `/house/vendor/` path serves the copied scripts directly.

The product editor now has barcode and product conversion tables. Barcode forms retain optional amounts, units, stores, notes, camera input, and custom fields. The table includes configured custom columns. New barcode units start empty. Available units follow the native conversion order relative to the purchase unit and include connected inactive units. Empty optional barcode values are sent as empty strings. This retains the native stored values and prevents an omitted unit from taking the database default.

Product conversion forms start with the stock unit and factor one. They require different source and destination units and retain the configured amount precision. Forward and inverse descriptions use the selected units. The editor can show the computed conversion table without edit controls. A product Grocycode preview uses the current product ID and the existing download and label actions.

Resolved conversions now use the saved native cache through a Python reader. The viewer supports one product and global rows. Empty or unknown product queries use the global rows. Only active units supply names and filter choices; inactive units retain their conversion rows with empty labels. The reader keeps saved factor text at full precision. The web table applies amount precision, full unit names, and plural labels in its descriptions. Its unit filter searches the From column, including after column moves. Factor sorting uses the native formatted text comparison.

The five-column viewer uses the native `qu-conversions-resolved-table` settings. It supports column order, visibility, sorting, grouping, collapse, and Reset. The three visibility controls are From, To, and Factor. The empty description heading retains an accessible sorting control. The legacy `/quantityunitconversionsresolved` route keeps native numeric product bookmarks. The product editor and Manage unit lists can open the same viewer in a dialog. Each opening reads current cache values. Closing keeps the parent draft or selection and returns focus. A failed read offers Retry; a successful retry returns focus to the table and keeps the selected unit.

The web router now reads and writes plain query text. It does not parse query values as JSON. Each route still validates its own fields. Stock product IDs, journal date ranges, summary filters, and embedded values keep their native text without added quotes. Product names retain exponent text, long decimals, quotes, and JSON-like text. The application and its full-route tests use the same router setup. Shopping list checks now inspect the visible URL instead of the router's internal number type.

The product form has Save & continue and Save & return to products. Enter continues after creation and returns after editing. A successful barcode or conversion save reloads the saved product values, including when the parent form had an unsaved draft. Cancel retains that draft. Confirmed deletion removes the related row before it saves the current product draft. A failed product save can retry without another deletion or conversion creation. Barcode editing starts custom field and row writes independently. Python waits for both requests and retains failed work for retry.

Product, recipe, and equipment forms use the pinned formatted editor. Python serves its scripts, styles, fonts, and translations. The editor has the legacy toolbar, image and video controls, source view, and fullscreen mode. A description stays in the form draft until the record save. Source changes enter that draft only when source view closes. Opening the editor retains the original stored value. Save controls wait for the active document to load; a failed document has a Retry control. Failed record saves keep the description draft. Successful reloads use the saved value. Product lists and detail views use the Python display filter.

Catalog rows and the catalog editor have Copy links. Legacy `/product/new?copy-of=ID` links open the same new-product form. Copy reads the current source record before it fills the form. A failed source read has Retry and Cancel controls. Copy bypasses product presets and retains the checked native field list. The name starts empty. Custom controls use their new-record values. The picture and barcodes stay separate, and new-product conversion factors keep their normal defaults. Source description text uses the native paste operation, including its paragraph and line-break rules. The first name entry fills empty consume and price units from the stock unit. A successful save clears the copy query and uses the selected continue or return action.

The container-weight control follows the tare checkbox. A disabled control is omitted from the record save and keeps its stored value. Copy retains the native programmatic-checkbox rule: a copied true flag does not enable the weight control. Its copied weight stays visible, but a new product keeps the database weight default until the user enables that control.

The native stock-unit picker copies a selected row position to the purchase, consume, and price pickers. Inactive units can change those positions. The checked native form then selected an unrelated unit. Python continues to copy the selected unit ID so that its defaults stay valid. Stored-value comparisons use explicit matching unit choices when this native defect applies.

The shared record editor now uses the fourteen native custom field controls. This includes products, recipes, household records, and custom records. New controls retain blank defaults, except for local date and time initialization with `now`. Required checkboxes need a choice on creation and permit an explicit false value. Existing values retain numeric field names, file display names, image previews, titled links, and selected lists. Only changed fields are sent. Field display order uses the native sort number and SQLite name order, including Unicode names.

The editor saves product and other record data before custom fields and pictures. Editing a shopping list in Manage retains the separate native order: custom fields before the list name. A field or file error keeps the draft and any new record ID. A retry sends unfinished field writes and uploads. Changing a custom record type uses its own field namespace and retains shared field edits. The shopping forms use the same controls and file functions.

The Python input filter also retains the native closing form for breaks and images. It supplies missing image text, checks the supported image data types, and corrects the declared type when the native reference does so. Encoded literal tag text retains the separate decoding rule.

Full Python parity is not complete. Open work includes remaining configuration and preference effects, translated web text, legacy forms and web error cases, and the behavior and release gates below. The later verification entries describe implemented native recipe tables, shopping messages and meal-plan workflows; those increments still need broader browser and release verification. Physical camera, torch, and printer checks remain open. Test results must identify the runtime and selected cases.

## Verification

On 2026-10-27, the full reference comparison passed on Python commit `b91d908` in `Europe/Bucharest`: 1,850 tests passed and 56 were skipped. The release image also passed HTTP checks for startup, catalog, stock, undo, settings, custom fields, files, calendar, the API specification, and profile access. The image had no PHP executable. These results cover the checked cases; they do not prove complete web feature coverage.

On 2026-10-28, the same commit passed the full reference comparison in `UTC`: 1,850 passed and 56 skipped. The normal Python test run passed 1,008 tests and skipped 1,062 cases that required other test environments. The disabled-feature comparison also passed both selected cases.

The latest full comparisons on Python commit `e9a1b62` passed in both `Europe/Bucharest` and `UTC`: 1,862 tests passed and 56 were skipped in each run. The release image passed the HTTP checks, including escaped barcode product selection and the new web language endpoint. It had no PHP executable. The stock aggregation case stays two days from the date boundary; separate cases check the date-boundary rules.

Language checks passed 70 frontend tests and five Python endpoint tests. Formatting, lint, type checks, and the web build passed. Browser checks covered German and Hebrew settings, saved numeric values, desktop text direction, and the Hebrew mobile menu. These checks do not establish complete translation coverage.

The appearance changes passed 93 frontend tests. Browser checks covered saved light and dark modes, the `24:00` schedule value, and a 390-pixel phone view. The language check also passed in that phone view. The menu stays outside the closed view after a direction change and opens from the correct side. Test settings were restored after these checks.

The product-preset changes passed 95 frontend tests, formatting, lint, type checks, and the web build. Browser checks saved all six presets, created a product, and checked the values in the Python database. The saved due-days value remained `-1`. The preset form also fit a 390-pixel phone view. The test product was removed and the prior settings were restored.

The production web image at `95bbe28` also passed a browser check with the `/house` path prefix. Nginx served the built assets and forwarded requests to the Python API image at `e9a1b62`. Saved product presets survived a reload. German text and the saved night mode worked through that prefix. Neither image had a PHP executable.

The header clock passed 66 recorded format cases for the 33 legacy languages. An additional comparison checked 11,088 combinations of language, month, weekday, and hour against the pinned Moment 2.30.1 output; all matched. The app uses Moment 2.31.0 and retains the older Catalan and Ukrainian apostrophes in clock text. The package audit reported no vulnerabilities. All 162 frontend tests, lint, formatting, type checks, and the web build passed. Browser checks covered live updates, saving and disabling the setting, and English and German at desktop and phone sizes. Test settings were restored.

The formatted-content and full-screen changes passed 14 selected Python tests and all 170 frontend tests. Formatting, lint, type checks, and the web build passed. The package audit reported no vulnerabilities. The refresh regression failed with the earlier refresh code at the expected changed stock value and passed with the correction. Content tests check domain access, formatted output, script removal, encoded literal text, iframe isolation, missing records, and preservation of saved values. Screen-on tests check visibility, setting changes, rejected and late requests, removal, and the actual legacy video method.

Browser checks covered direct full-screen links, formatted recipe and equipment text, keyboard exit, desktop and phone PDF views, and controls during long-page scrolling. The browser's native screen-lock API acquired a lock in full-screen mode and released it on Escape. The saved always-on setting also acquired a lock outside a card. These checks confirm browser requests and lock release; they do not check physical display sleep.

Both release images built. The Python image passed the HTTP checks, including both formatted-content routes and saved-description preservation. The production web image passed the recipe, clock, scroll, and native screen-lock checks through `/house`. Checked API and asset requests used that prefix. Neither release image had a PHP executable. Browser fixtures were removed, test settings were restored, and the temporary release containers and network were removed.

The included-recipe repair passed nine selected Python integration tests and all 172 frontend tests. Formatting, lint, type checks, and the web build passed. The Python tests cover nested card order, shared child recipes, separate ingredient rows, converted amounts, single-unit checks, groups, notes, stock and shopping amounts, access checks, and safe preparation output. The frontend tests cover full unit names, keyboard selection, temporary check marks, saved servings, and ordered saves before native consumption. The saved-serving regression failed with the earlier page: it showed `1` when the saved value was `4`. It passed after the correction.

The recipe release images built and passed their checks. The Python image passed HTTP checks for nested recipe cards and safe preparation output. The production web check covered desktop and phone views through `/house`, included preparation, and saving the ingredient checkbox setting through the preference form. The browser then cooked a recipe that needed 200 grams with 50 grams in stock. Python recorded consumption of 50 grams and the remaining stock was zero. Neither release image had a PHP executable. Browser fixtures were removed, test settings were restored, and the temporary release containers and network were removed.

The recipe overview changes passed ten selected Python integration tests and all 177 frontend tests. Formatting, lint, type checks, and the web build passed. Tests cover normal recipe selection, all three stock states, ingredient search, sorting, saved gallery selection, authenticated pictures, legacy query fields, embedded views, and dialog closure during loading. The overview test failed with the previous page because the selected recipe was absent beside the list. The dialog close test also failed before its correction. Both passed after the changes.

The Python release image passed HTTP checks for the overview, all three stock states, nested cards, safe preparation output, and the earlier release checks. The production web image passed checks through `/house` for list and gallery display, side-by-side columns, saved preferences, authenticated gallery pictures, and dialog closure with filter and focus preservation. Checked API and asset requests used the prefix. Neither final image had a PHP executable.

Visual checks covered the 1280-pixel desktop view and the 390-pixel phone list and gallery. The phone table now preserves short words. A separate phone check found that an explicit recipe link scrolled before its data loaded. After the correction, the loaded recipe started at the top of the view, held focus, and caused no page overflow. All seven recipe tests passed again, as did formatting, lint, type checks, and the release build. Final visual captures used an isolated Chromium container. The scrolled-page capture did not render correctly, so the selection position was checked through the browser DOM.

The shared picture fixture was replaced with a valid PNG. The old fixture had an invalid checksum, which release libgd rejected. Both Python test APIs then returned the valid image successfully, and the release gallery displayed it. This was a test fixture repair. Browser test records were removed, prior settings were restored, and the temporary release containers and network were removed.

The recipe action changes passed ten selected Python tests and all 178 frontend tests. Formatting, lint, type checks, and the web build passed. The two action tests failed with the previous reader because its confirmation dialogs were absent. The Python presentation test failed with the previous response because it had no missing-ingredient list. The repaired tests check confirmation before a write, cancellation, fresh choices after reopening, exclusions from repeated rows, nested ingredient positions, and ordered serving saves. A final run of all eight recipe tests also passed.

Both action release images built. The Python image passed the release HTTP checks, including empty and populated missing-ingredient lists. Neither image had a PHP executable. Production web checks through `/house` covered both confirmations inside a full-screen recipe. Escape closed only the confirmation and restored action-button focus. Cancellation left stock and shopping data unchanged. With Flour unchecked, the shopping request saved only three units of the nested Pepper ingredient. Confirmed consumption then booked the two available Flour units and retained all five units of the check-only ingredient. Desktop and 390-pixel phone captures showed the confirmations and their buttons within the screen. The phone page had no horizontal overflow. The consumption capture used isolated Linux Chromium after the first browser capture failed. Both browser sessions, all three temporary containers, and their network were removed. The local Python test server was restarted and its new recipe response was checked.

The print and copy changes passed all 181 frontend tests, formatting, lint, type checks, and the web build. The three new cases failed against an isolated copy of the previous code: its reader had no Print button, and copying did not select the returned ID in either display mode. The print test checks that full-screen state, scroll locking, and page isolation are cleared before the browser print call. Copy tests check the returned ID, selected row, new card, filter reset, and closure of an open dialog.

The final production web image passed browser checks through `/house` with the Python action release image. A direct recipe and a recipe dialog each produced a four-page PDF. Both documents retained all 22 preparation paragraphs, the final step, the authenticated picture, ingredient amounts, notes, and the included sauce on its own page. PDF text checks excluded list and editing controls. Visual checks covered the first and included-recipe pages. The saved inline dark color scheme initially made page margins dark; the final print rule overrides it, and the final page margin was white. Printing a direct full-screen recipe cleared its hash and scroll lock. Printing a dialog kept that dialog open. The 390-pixel phone controls fit the page.

The release browser copied the included sauce in list-only mode and selected its saved copy. It then copied the main recipe in side-by-side mode and displayed the new card. The copied preparation, ingredient, and nested recipe were retained in Python. Checked API requests kept the `/house/api/` prefix, and neither release image had a PHP executable. The browser session, three temporary containers, and their network were removed.

The recipe meal-plan changes passed all 184 frontend tests and eight selected Python tests. Formatting, lint, type checks, and the web build passed. Both positive meal-plan cases failed against an isolated copy of the previous reader because its controls were absent. The new tests check the local date, selected recipe, ordered sections, initial focus, retained choices, required date, fractional servings, the compatibility request fields, save errors, retry, and the feature flag. A lifecycle test also found that removing the page with a form open could retain a full-screen scroll lock. It failed with `overflow: hidden` before the shared dialog cleanup correction. All 22 recipe, camera, and full-screen cases passed after the correction. The final 14 recipe cases also passed after the payload checks were made independent of JSON field order.

The production web image passed meal-plan checks through `/house` with the Python action image. The form used the selected recipe, today's local date, one serving, and the first section by sort number. With amount precision set to two places, its minimum and step were `0.01`. Desktop and 390-pixel phone captures showed all fields and both action buttons within the dialog. After entry through the native date control, Python saved `2027-04-07`, the changed recipe, `0.5` servings, section `2`, and type `recipe`. Reopening retained servings and section and reset the date and recipe. Escape preserved the underlying full-screen recipe and restored focus to its action button. Neither release image had a PHP executable. The browser session, three temporary containers with their test records, and their network were removed.

The recipe code changes passed 50 selected Python tests and all 192 frontend tests. Formatting, lint, and type checks passed. Both release images built. The HTTP tests use image hashes from the pinned PHP reference and cover 40 image and error cases. They also check download query values, cache headers, the GUI login redirect, HEAD, and recipe access. In the Linux release image, all 35 successful PNG files matched the saved PHP output byte for byte. The other five cases returned the same error messages. Separate reference comparisons matched 93 Data Matrix outputs and 93 Code 128 bar sequences. The selected baseline test returned 404 with the previous Python code. The new reader download test failed with the previous page because its code controls were absent. The repaired tests passed.

The production web checks used the `/house` prefix. Desktop and 390-pixel phone menus fit the screen. Opening a menu moved focus to Download. Escape closed the menu, returned focus to its button, and retained the full-screen recipe. The browser saved a 474-byte, 100-by-100 PNG as `Grocycode.png`. The browser tool's file transfer was canceled; a page download directory inside the isolated Chromium container allowed the file to save. Browser JSON, browser form, and Python server printing each sent one request to an isolated test receiver. None sent the Grocy API key to that receiver.

The meal-plan camera check decoded both Python image formats through virtual video streams: Data Matrix on desktop and Code 128 on a phone-sized view. Both selected the included recipe and stopped the stream. The Data Matrix check then saved that recipe with `0.5` servings in Python. These checks did not use a physical camera or printer. Neither release image had a PHP executable. The browser session, three temporary containers, their network, and their test database and files were removed.

The remaining code route changes passed 63 selected Python tests and all 203 frontend tests. Formatting, lint, and type checks passed. Both release images built. New reference fixtures cover product, chore, battery, saved stock-entry, and missing stock-entry codes in both image formats. The Python HTTP tests check the GUI and application routes, all five GUI login redirects and HEAD responses, and domain access. The selected baseline returned 404 before the route was added. All 11 new web cases failed with the previous controls; that baseline included the tab-label correction so the checks reached the missing controls. The repaired cases passed.

The Python release returned 20 HTTP responses whose PNG bytes matched the saved native reference hashes. These checks used both Data Matrix and Code 128 through `/house`. The release browser then sent one label request from each of the four label panels, one from a stock entry, and one from the stock table. The receiver recorded six requests with the selected IDs. The stock-entry request retained its saved stock ID. No request sent the Grocy API key to the receiver.

The phone check found that a table's no-wrap rule placed the two menu buttons beside each other and clipped the second action. After each button became a block-level flex box, both actions fitted inside the stock and chore menus. Final captures covered the 390-pixel stock, chore, and labels pages and the stock-entry menu. The labels page also passed a desktop check. All 203 frontend tests passed again after the layout correction, as did formatting, lint, type checks, and the final web release build. Neither release image had a PHP executable. The browser session, three temporary containers with their test records, and their network were removed.

The paper-label and thermal-action changes passed 71 selected Python tests and all 207 frontend tests. Formatting, lint, type checks, and both release builds passed. The Python fixtures came from the pinned PHP application's paper-label route. They cover both code formats, the due-date flag, German text, escaped names, empty dates, missing entries, and a failed image. Access and HEAD checks use the real HTTP routes. The original baseline returned 404 for the label. The new web cases failed with the previous controls because its paper-label action and print-options dialog were absent. A further regression failed with status 500 before the label renderer was corrected to retain the document when its image fails.

The final Python release passed ten paper-label HTTP checks through `/house`. Eight retained the native text and PNG bytes. Two retained the label text while an oversized code returned 500 from its image route. Four thermal cases matched the native printer bytes exactly, including notes, unit names, fractional amounts, and completed entries. Neither release image had a PHP executable.

The release browser opened a stock-entry label in a new window with the label-printer flag disabled. Both required Roboto weights loaded. Its 25-pixel code image, product-name bounds, and full screenshot matched the native view. Both versions produced a one-page PDF with identical extracted text and printed pixels. The release PDF embedded Roboto Regular and Bold.

The phone check selected shopping list `7`, disabled its header, and saved that choice. A real missing output directory caused the expected file-connector error. After that test directory was created, Retry wrote 112 bytes that matched the native receipt. Reopening retained the disabled header, and the saved API value was `0`. Print options and error controls fitted the 390-pixel view. These checks used files in disposable containers, without a physical printer. The browser session, four temporary containers with their test records, and their network were removed.

The shopping-paper changes passed 74 selected Python tests and all 208 frontend tests. Formatting, lint, type checks, and both release builds passed. The new HTTP cases check both layouts, completed items, note markup, custom-field columns, empty cells, the PHP round-up value rules, shopping access, and missing lists. The new web case checks saved options, the selected list, API-key forwarding, and document errors. With the previous source, the HTTP checks failed with 404 and the web check failed because the print-options dialog was absent.

The final Python and web release images passed browser checks through `/house`. The table PDF, with header, group headings, and custom columns, matched the pinned native PDF pixel for pixel at 928 by 1200 pixels. The list PDF, without a header, also matched pixel for pixel. Both checks used the same English data; the table used a fixed print time. The data included fractional amounts, note-only items, completed items, formatted notes, escaped names, decimal and currency fields, a checkbox, a titled link, and group fields. The PDFs used embedded Roboto fonts; the table also used the pinned icon font.

All three print settings survived a release reload. The options fit a 390-pixel phone view. Closing during a delayed paper load cancelled the request and left no print frame. The print dialog closed before the browser print request. A browser `afterprint` event removed the frame and restored keyboard focus. The isolated headless browser does not open a physical print dialog; printer hardware remains unchecked. Neither release image had PHP. Test records, settings, containers, and the network were confined to the temporary test environment.

The notes changes passed all 209 frontend tests, formatting, lint, type checks, and both release builds. The full Python run passed 1,103 tests and skipped 1,072 tests. Three image lookup cases failed because the test process could not open a local socket. All three passed on repeat with socket access. A further 20 selected HTML comparisons passed on Python and the pinned PHP reference. The final notes and HTML owners passed 16 tests and skipped the two cases that require the optional PHP worker.

Nine captured native HTTP results cover empty paragraphs, break syntax, image attributes, valid and invalid image data, corrected image types, and encoded literal tags. Eight failed before the shared filter correction. The editor HTTP checks cover the stored description, current print drafts, the initial empty-note rule, shopping access, and script authorization. They also retain imported markup as data. The new web case checks the editor message source, selected list, API key, failed save, and retry. With the previous source, the notes endpoint returned 404, the list response lacked its description, and the web case could not find the notes controls.

The release browser saved formatted notes and uploaded a real PNG through `/house`. Both stored values matched the native API output. Clear stored `<p><br /></p>` in both versions. The PDF with an unsaved formatted draft matched the native PDF text and all pixels at 928 by 1200 pixels, with a fixed print time. Printing retained the prior stored value. Source-view checks retained the native save timing. The editor link control opened an owned test page in a new tab. Full-screen checks covered Tab, Escape, page locking, and focus return. The installed Python image passed all 58 pinned editor asset hashes.

The 390-pixel checks covered German labels, Hebrew text direction, native editor fonts, and saved night colors. The shopping controls and rows fitted the phone view. At the end of the page, the editor remained clear of the basket bar. The final phone page had no horizontal overflow. Neither release image had a PHP executable. Browser sessions, test records, containers, and their network remained confined to the temporary test environment.

The shopping list control changes passed all 213 frontend tests and 19 selected Python tests. Formatting, lint, type checks, and both release builds passed. The new checks cover list summaries, custom field metadata, numeric field names, shopping access, list deletion, completed-item removal, and printed custom images. The web checks cover legacy links, browser history, all custom field types, save order, cancellation, and upload retry on one created list. Both new Python cases and all three new list-form web cases failed with the previous source.

The release browser checks used `/house` on a local HTTP origin. List names, order, notes, and counts matched the native controller query. Clear done items left the same three open rows in both databases. The native and release forms saved the same text, explicit false checkbox, selected choice, and empty date values. Both uploaded files had the same displayed names and bytes. The HTTP check found that secure-page-only file name generation blocked uploads. The corrected file name code passed the browser check and the web retry test without that browser function.

The 390-pixel checks covered the list form, saved files, rename, delete confirmation, cancellation, and the default list delete rule. The form scrolled within the screen. Escape closed the confirmation and returned focus to List actions. Confirmed deletion selected list `1`. The phone page had no horizontal overflow. These checks used an isolated browser and disposable databases. Neither release image had PHP. The task browser, four test containers, and their network were removed.

The named-file changes passed 78 selected comparisons on Python and the pinned PHP server. A further 85 Python integration cases covered file storage, access, shopping paper, and code images. Formatting, lint, type checks, and both release builds passed. The 13 initial local cases failed on the previous source because the named file route was absent. The added preflight case also failed on the previous source.

The final release checks used `/house`. Both links from a generated shopping document returned their files through the web server. The `/transfer` page and the entry and journal row actions share one transfer form. Entry actions fill the product, source location, and stock ID. The transfer form supports barcode amount and unit values, unknown-code assignment, failed-read retry, failed-save retry, freezing messages, and transaction Undo. Barcode assignment occurs after the transfer write. A failed assignment reports the error and retains the assignment for retry. Embedded frames send the native resize, product change, success, and close messages. A successful embedded form stays disabled until its parent closes it. Full-page saves reset the picker and refresh the product card. Nested forms retain the parent product draft and restore focus. Notification controls remain inside the active modal dialog, so Undo stays available after a child dialog closes.

Stock Grocycodes retain the native source-selection order. The native stock lookup changes the source value without sending a source-change event. Its independent location response can then replace that value. Python now sends the location, barcode, and stock reads together. If the location response finishes last, the default source remains. If the stock response finishes last, its source value remains, while the stock choices and amount limit still belong to the default source. An explicit source change then refreshes the choices and selects the forced stock ID. Python keeps the controls disabled until these selection reads finish. Intermediate native loading states still need comparison. Other legacy booking forms, row actions, and parent message handling also remain incomplete.

The regression failed on changed saved rows before the repair. Imports without a source cache continue to calculate their rows.
