PHP was disabled and absent from the release image. The two owned containers and data volume were removed. The consume page controls, stock-item selection, recipe booking, opening, and scan workflows still need implementation and comparison. Full legacy compliance remains incomplete.

The `/consume` page and stock overview Consume dialog now use the Python reader. The overview header opens the full page. The row action requires consume permission and positive aggregate stock. The configured `consume` entry page opens this route under the web base path. The form supports product names, barcodes, product and stock Grocycodes, camera input, barcode assignment, recipe names and recipe Grocycodes, spoiled stock, specific stock items, tare weights, and opening. New-product workflows stay unavailable, as in the native consume view.

Consume reads locations and stock items with child products included. It selects the default consume location when that location has stock, or the first available location. Unit choices use the saved conversion rows and names. Quick amounts use the selected conversion factor. Stock amounts use the native hidden-field rounding. Normal amount limits use the selected location. Parents with child products have no form amount ceiling. Tare limits use the container weight unless exact consumption is selected. A manually selected stock item uses its raw stock amount as the display limit. Switching that item off retains the limit. Repeated stock codes retain separate choices. A stock-code query clicks the native checkbox once per matching row; two matching rows leave the checkbox off while retaining the selected code.

Consume and Open read fresh product details before writing and permit child-product substitution. Consume sends the checked exact, spoiled, location, recipe, and stock-item values. Open sends amount and the optional stock code. A full-page Consume clears product, unit, location choices, and recipe. Spoiled and exact choices remain. Open clears the product and stock-item controls but retains unit, location, and recipe. Opening a tare product, a product that disables opening, or a product whose own stock is already all open remains unavailable. The open control also requires open permission. Read and booking errors retain the draft and permit retry. Barcode assignment sends only barcode and product ID. A failed assignment still permits normal consumption completion.

Full-page scan mode saves its consume preference. Plain product selection uses one display unit for automatic consumption. Barcode amounts retain their saved value. Embedded forms force scan mode off without changing the saved preference. Successful embedded consumption retains locked controls and sends the native product-change, success, and close messages. Embedded Open keeps the form available. Plain full-page product bookmarks retain the native empty-unit initialization behavior until the product is selected again. Embedded links initialize the fields. A `locationId` query does not replace the later default-location read in the checked native sequence.

The consume form passed all 413 frontend tests in 45 files. Lint, formatting, all three frontend type checks, and root and `/house` release builds passed. The new route and entry-page checks failed against the previous route definitions. The location-reset check failed before the empty-list correction. The repeated-stock-code case failed before the checkbox correction. The cases also cover saved alternate-unit labels, recipes, spoiled and exact choices, tare weights, parent stock, opening, read and write retry, feature flags, permissions, barcode assignment failure, scan settings, and stock codes. No backend source changed in this batch.

Independently allocated stock IDs were compared by their corresponding saved records; their later use and retention matched. The final image also matched the repeated-code checkbox state, booking, and Undo. Both embedded forms retained amount `0.5`, hid scan mode, and left the saved scan setting enabled. That setting was then restored to disabled in both test copies.

Consume now retains the native card values after Open and Undo. A successful full-page consume refreshes the card. Selecting a product after the form clears also refreshes it, including the same product. Undo no longer refreshes the stock queries. Open leaves those queries unchanged. Returning from the product editor still refreshes the card and related stock data.

A stock-code location change now reads the current stock entry before reading its location contents. Clearing Location also runs this lookup. The returned stock location replaces the selected value. The display amount changes to the saved entry amount. The hidden API amount and conversion hint retain their previous conversion until an amount-picker event updates them. Amount and unit edits update the conversion. If a focused Amount changes, leaving that input also updates the conversion. The Amount input remains available during a location read so that this focus behavior can occur. Shared number controls forward optional focus and blur events; the consume form uses them to retain this native distinction.

The form also retains the checked native control restoration. Open, successful full-page consumption, and a booking error enable the unit and stock-item controls. This includes a unit list with one choice and an unchecked stock-item control. Reloading product units restores the one-unit disabled state. Checking and clearing the stock-item checkbox restores its own disabled state. Selecting a stock item while its checkbox is off changes the amount limit but does not add a stock code to the API request.

### Stock booking row references

New purchase, Open, transfer, consumption, and inventory bookings now leave `stock_row_id` null. Grocy 4.5.0 sets this field only for stock edit records. Open and consumption records receive their opened date directly from the stock entry. Stock edit records keep the original row reference when matching stock entries are combined. Combining entries still updates their stock IDs and the associated booking stock IDs.

The public Open comparison checks complete booking records for partial and full opening, with and without transfer on Open. It checks the original expiry in the Open record, the shorter expiry in stock and transfer records, saved metadata, correlated transfer records, booking reads, object reads, and transaction Undo. Existing inventory tests now check their row references. The stock edit tests check Undo and retained references after entries are combined. These are current API and saved-record contracts.

All four Open cases and both inventory cases failed on Python before the repair and passed on PHP. The stock edit reference case also failed with the old entry-combination code. The final selection passed all 38 comparisons with the local Python app and PHP. It also passed all 38 comparisons with the Linux Python release and PHP. All 285 integration tests passed, with five optional tests skipped. Ruff lint and formatting checks and the Python type check passed. The release matched all 177 Python source files. PHP was disabled and its executable was absent. All 62 stock rows and 129 booking rows remained unchanged after restart. The database integrity check passed.

The Open receipt difference recorded above is corrected. Initial consume location callbacks, further response-order cases, scan sounds, recipe text, embedded host behavior, purchase gaps, and inventory page behavior still need work. Full legacy compliance remains incomplete. Python remains the release runtime. PHP remains only a test reference.

### Legacy API cache headers

Legacy API responses now retain the native HTTP cache policy. The common response middleware no longer adds `Cache-Control: no-store` to these routes. Explicit cache headers, such as the file download lifetime, remain in effect. Application routes under `/api/v2/` and the health route retain their default `no-store` policy. The extension authentication test checks this policy for both successful and denied prefixed requests.

The public comparison covers GET, HEAD, and OPTIONS for product details, stock entries, stock locations, location objects, and system information. The file comparison also checks its explicit cache lifetime. Both the local Python app and the Linux release passed all 32 selected comparisons with PHP. Nine stock read cases failed on Python before the repair and passed on PHP. All 285 integration tests passed, with five optional tests skipped. Ruff lint, formatting, and Python types passed. Python remains the release runtime; PHP is only the test reference.

### Initial consume location callbacks

The consume form now retains each initial location event. A stock code starts a stock-entry lookup for each event, including an event before the default location is added. Each returned lookup resets the shared stock sum. Its entry callback appends choices, adds their amounts, and toggles the specific-item checkbox once per matching row. The initial callbacks share their current amount, unit factor, sum, and checkbox state. They are no longer replaced by one final location read.

The stock-code reads start before the barcode lookup, as on the native page. Consume reads now use the browser's default HTTP cache mode. Together with the legacy cache-header correction above, this retains native handling of repeated requests. The visible amount and hidden booking amount still retain their separate native behavior.

Three new cases failed on the previous form. The one-location control passed. The corrected form passed all 422 frontend tests in 45 files. The new cases check one, two, and three initial callbacks, a delayed stock reply, retained duplicate choices, amount limits, checkbox state, and the complete consumption request. Existing focus, cleared-location, barcode, scan-mode, purchase, transfer, and shared number-control cases also passed. Lint, formatting, all three type checks, and root and `/house` builds passed.

The native page and Linux release matched with stock in one, two, and three locations. Their initial choice counts were one, two, and three. Their specific-item checkbox states were on, off, and on. Consumption and visible Undo passed on both runtimes. All stable booking and stock fields matched, including retained stock identities. A fresh three-location case also passed booking and Undo on the final release images.

A disposable HTTP proxy held selected replies without changing their bodies. With all stock lookups completed before entry replies, both runtimes retained a six-unit maximum. Releasing the final stock lookup after one entry callback gave four units on both. Releasing it after two entry callbacks gave two units on both. Their three choices and checked state also matched. The normal three-location browser run showed the same four-unit maximum on both runtimes after the cache correction.

The 1440-pixel and 390-pixel views retained the existing layout. The phone form had no horizontal overflow, and both action buttons remained reachable after scrolling. All 153 frontend source files matched the build snapshot. All 87 web build files matched the final image. All 177 Python source files matched the API image. ### Consume scan feedback and first booking

Scan mode now captures a valid booking from the first stock reply. It retains the scanned stock ID instead of waiting for later replies to toggle the specific-item checkbox. The form keeps the current product, location, amount, and unit factor for this capture. A scan setting change applies at once. It remains active if the setting write fails. The scan button also remains available while a booking reply is pending. Turning scan mode off before that reply suppresses the consumption success sound.

The four original MP3 files are included unchanged from the pinned Grocy release, with its MIT license. Enabling scan mode plays the permission clip. Barcode and stock-code commits retain the two native scan beeps. Successful consumption plays its success clip when scan mode is still active. Invalid scan amounts play the error clip. Open and Undo do not play the consumption success clip. Embedded consume forms keep scan mode off.

Exact-name typing in scan mode now waits for confirmation. The tested Enter path matches the native page: no consumption occurs while typing, and Enter starts one booking. This batch left browser-list selection waiting for Enter or blur. The next batch corrects that difference.

All 428 frontend tests passed in 45 files. Seven local checks failed with the previous form. Two focused checks also failed before the name-confirmation and pending-toggle corrections. Lint, formatting, all three type checks, and root and `/house` builds passed. No backend application source changed.

Their sound sequences, successful booking fields, and saved stock fields matched. A proxy held a booking reply while scan mode was turned off; both pages retained the booking and suppressed its success sound. Name entry, Open, corrected invalid amounts, and visible Undo also passed. All stable saved records and Undo booking fields matched.

The first release check found that prefixed sound links returned the application HTML. The web server now serves `/uisounds/` under its configured prefix. The public resource checker verifies GET and HEAD status, MPEG content type, exact source bytes, and content length for all four clips. It failed before the route correction. The corrected release passed 37 resource and form responses under `/house`, plus eight root-path sound responses. All four sound types reached playback in the native and release browsers.

PHP was disabled and absent from the release images. Saved stock and booking records remained unchanged after restart. The database integrity check passed.

### Confirmed product-list choices

A confirmed browser-list choice now starts a scan booking at once. Typing still waits for Enter or blur. The choice retains the two scan beeps observed with a pointer on the native page. The shared picker keeps the existing behavior when deferred selection is off.

The input distinction comes from Chromium's [datalist selection handler](https://github.com/chromium/chromium/blob/main/components/autofill/content/renderer/autofill_agent.cc) and its form-control event path. A list choice dispatches a plain input event. Text edits use `InputEvent`. The consume request check covers both this list event and typed-name confirmation. The list case failed before the correction; the Enter control passed.

All 429 frontend tests passed in 45 files. The 29 consume checks include both confirmation paths. Lint, formatting, all three type checks, and root and `/house` builds passed. The separate browser probe confirmed the typed event but could not select the browser popup through automation. This batch therefore does not claim a completed browser check of the popup or full parity across browsers. No backend source or release-runtime setting changed.

### Consume recipe selection

The consume form now has an explicit recipe list. It retains the native matching order: name prefixes first, then matches with the same letter case, then other matches. Arrow keys move through the list and wrap at each end. Pointer selection, Tab, the clear action, and the list toggle retain their native results. Text that is not confirmed stays visible with an empty recipe ID. Editing a selected name clears its ID. Recipe Grocycodes resolve on blur. Invalid code types clear the input.

The browser check found a native interaction between Enter and form validity. If the consume form is valid, Enter clicks OK and blurs the recipe input before its keyup handler can select a recipe. The recipe stays unselected, and that keypress makes no booking. If the form has no product, Enter can select the highlighted recipe. Tab selects it in both cases. The Python control retains these observed results.

An open recipe list blocks manual booking and automatic scan booking. The blur path retains the native 200-millisecond list delay. Scan callbacks read the current menu flag. They cannot book from an earlier render while that flag is still set. The focused scan regression failed before this guard was added.

The recipe field also exposes the shared camera action. Its accessible name identifies the recipe field. A camera error retains the selected recipe, returns focus to the camera button, and makes no booking. The existing camera-disable flag applies. Physical camera decoding through this field remains unverified.

All 440 frontend tests passed in 45 files, including 40 consume checks. Seven focused cases failed with the previous form, while four controls passed. Lint, formatting, all three type checks, and root and `/house` builds passed. No backend source changed.

Its 1440-pixel and 390-pixel views were inspected. All recipe choices and both action buttons were reachable on the phone, with no horizontal overflow. Restart retained all 64 stock rows and 135 booking rows, and SQLite integrity passed.

### Booking date keyboard controls

Purchase and inventory now share a text date control with the native keyboard rules. It accepts `x` and `X` for Never overdue, `MMDD`, `YYYYMMDD`, `YYYYMMe`, `YYYYMM+`, and relative day, month, and year values. Arrow keys change days or weeks. Shift with an arrow changes months or years. Month and year changes retain valid month-end dates. An empty field starts at today when an arrow is pressed.

The control retains the native focus rules. Purchase due-date shortcuts move focus to Price. Inventory due dates and purchased dates keep focus in the field. Tab moves from Due date to Price without extra calendar or checkbox stops. Never overdue makes the due field read-only. Clearing its checkbox clears and focuses the field. The number-pad feature flag controls the input mode. Invalid required dates retain the native custom validity. Removing the required setting does not clear an earlier custom error. A valid date clears it. On blur, the calendar parser can restore its last accepted value, as in the native purchase form.

The primary keyboard checks use 27 values captured from Grocy 4.5.0. All 489 frontend tests passed in 47 files. Two routed checks failed with the previous date inputs. A separate Tab check failed before the calendar and checkbox stops were removed. Lint, formatting, all three type checks, and root and `/house` builds passed. No Python backend source changed.

The Linux browser replay matched all 108 native states across purchase and inventory, for both due and purchased dates. It compared values, required and read-only settings, custom validity, Never state, and focus. Live bookings in both runtimes used compact dates, month-end dates, and the Never checkbox. Request values, saved entries, booking values, and stock custom fields matched after generated IDs and timestamps were excluded. Every visible Undo link restored empty stock. These booking checks used active units; the separate native inactive-unit initialization defect remains open.

The browser calendar selected the next day and set the field to `2026-10-31`. The 1440-pixel desktop and 390-pixel phone views were inspected. The phone view had no horizontal overflow. All 96 web build files and 177 Python source files matched their release images. All 39 public resource and form responses passed. SQLite integrity passed. PHP remained disabled and absent from the API image. The owned browser, four containers, and private network were removed.

Today changes the field and keeps the menu open. A typed key closes the menu and retains the native focus result. The final calendar month retains the checked disabled dates and navigation limits.

Its width stays within the field or the available dialog gutter. This corrects the right-edge clipping found in the phone stock dialog.

### Stock entry date controls

The shared stock entry editor now uses the booking date control. This applies to the direct `/stockentry/{id}` page, the stock entry table, and the product ledger. Both dates support the native keyboard shortcuts and calendar menu. Purchased-date shortcuts move focus to OK. Due-date shortcuts retain focus in the date field. The due-date number-pad flag, calendar week settings, and contextual date text apply.

Saved dates retain the native initial state. Date help starts empty. A saved Never date does not select its checkbox or make the input read-only until it is validated. Clearing Never clears and focuses the input. Closing the due-date calendar selects label reprint, including when the selected date is unchanged. Editing the purchased date does not select label reprint. A typed due date selects reprint when it loses focus. The empty Store request value is now the native empty string.

The second native date input does not parse typed text on blur. It retains invalid text and its validation error until further input or a calendar action. Purchase, inventory, and stock entry purchased dates now retain this rule. Due dates keep the primary parser and its cached-value restoration rule.

Existing nested-editor retry, parent-draft, disabled-tracking, and Undo checks also passed. Lint, formatting, all three type checks, and root and `/house` builds passed. ### Spending report

The web application now provides `/stockreports/spendings`, with a Spendings link on the stock overview. Stock and price tracking settings control the link and page. The Python reader at `/api/v2/stock/reports/spendings` requires authentication and permits the native read-only user.

The report reads the pinned `products_price_history` view. It groups by product, product group, or store. Product-group limits apply only to product grouping. Two valid date bounds select an inclusive purchase-date range. Without both valid bounds, the report starts at the first day of the current month and has no upper date bound. ### Location sheet quantity text and unit formats

### Stock overview quick actions and related workflows

On 2026-11-06, the overview quick controls were compared with `views/stockoverview.blade.php` and `public/viewjs/stockoverview.js` from the checksum-verified Grocy 4.5.0 release. The overview now offers the saved quick consume amount, Consume all, and the saved quick open amount. The generic one-unit shortcut remains on Today; it no longer supplies the overview controls.

The entries and journal actions reuse their existing dialogs. The summary opens with the selected product filter. Dialogs return focus to the row's More button.

Its popover activation is simulated because jsdom does not implement the native browser interaction. The broader Python integration and unit run passed 301 tests and skipped five optional checks, with one failing image-cache assertion. That assertion required a three-channel RGB pixel even when the release's libgd encoder returned opaque RGBA. It now converts the decoded pixel to RGBA and still requires opaque blue; source bytes, mode paths, cached bytes, deletion, and production-file preservation remain checked. All eight mode-file tests then passed with libgd, and the affected cache test also passed with the Pillow fallback. Changed Python lint, formatting, and type checks passed. The complete Python suite was not repeated after this test-only correction. The local runs required libmagic and libgd on the process library path; no PHP runtime was used.

### Stock overview statistics and move-on-open feedback

The overview now reads its statistics from the same compatibility APIs as the pinned page: `/api/stock` and `/api/stock/volatile?due_soon_days=...`. The summary counts API stock products rather than displayed overview rows. Hidden products do not count, but their stock value remains part of the total. The currency summary uses the browser's normal currency precision, independently of the saved table price precision. Disabling price tracking removes the total value. Due, overdue, expired, and shortage counts exclude hidden products using the native browser boolean rules. Overdue and expired counts retain their API overlap, even when the corresponding table filters select different products. The due count also exposes the saved day window in its tooltip.

Statistics queries share stock invalidation with purchases, consumption, opening, and undo. A failed read has a dedicated retry that retains the table search and filters. Quick open now reads product details before its success receipt, retains the native singular stock-unit name in that receipt, and reports the configured consume location when move-on-open is enabled. A failed post-booking details read reports its error without replaying the successful write or creating a success receipt.

After the final due-window tooltip change, the 21 focused statistics and overview-action cases also passed. The new summary regression failed before the implementation because the stock summary was absent. Checks cover hidden stock value, separate API counts, overlapping statuses, tracking flags, currency precision, summary refresh after consume and undo, read recovery, move destinations, and post-booking read failure. Existing due-window and minimum-stock fixtures now provide complete volatile-stock responses. No backend production code changed in this batch. No new real-browser, PHP runtime, or release-image comparison was performed.

### Native partial stock overview refresh

The overview now captures its initial table model and patches individual products through the existing `/api/stock/products/{id}` reader. Quick actions, purchase, consumption, inventory, transfers, stock entry actions, and entry edits supply the affected product ID to stock invalidation. The overview refreshes that product and its parent while other stock, recipe, and shopping queries retain their invalidation behavior. Product editing also refreshes the independent statistics.

Displayed own and aggregate amounts, opened amounts, stock value, due text, and row colors update through the native cell targets. Names, locations, group markers, custom values, search strings, and sorting values retain their initial table data. An emptied product with no minimum stock is hidden in place when the saved browser setting requires it. Undo can restore that same row. A booking does not add newly eligible products to the table. An explicit table reset captures a fresh overview model.

Quick actions retain their original quick amounts, but apply the pinned post-refresh enable rules. Those rules can enable quick consume below its original threshold and can enable opening even when all stock is open; the Python compatibility action still decides whether the resulting write is valid. Consume all receives the refreshed own amount, including for a tare product. Refreshed unit text uses the browser's number formatting and quantity-unit plural forms. Inactive units retain their English fallback. The aggregate unit text stays as originally rendered when only its amount changes.

All 541 frontend tests passed in 53 files. Lint, formatting, type checks, and the production build passed. The new table regression failed before the change because a complete overview read changed names, row membership, and order. Passing cases now exercise parent totals, cached search, a later explicit sort, original quick amounts, row hiding, undo, fractional unit text, and active versus inactive unit forms. The final implementation adds no backend production route or runtime dependency. These are source-comparison and automated-test results; no new real-browser or release-image comparison was performed.

Further comparison is still required for grouped rows and refreshed colors, callbacks that overlap a reset or another request, unit-format failures, complete product-dialog behavior, and external embedded host messages. The remaining application forms, preferences, translations, device checks, and broader parity queue remain open. Full legacy compliance remains incomplete.

### Product messages and grouped refresh completion order

The overview now receives same-origin `ProductChanged` messages, including the `BroadcastMessage` envelope sent by embedded stock forms. Numeric product IDs and digit strings select a targeted product read, parent refresh, and both statistics reads. Invalid IDs, malformed envelopes, and foreign-origin messages do not issue authenticated product reads. Leaving the overview removes its message listener. This is the overview's product-change receiver; the remaining global embedded modal protocol is not implemented by this change.

The shared table state now distinguishes a draw from a partial product update. Native refresh removes a row's collapsed-group display style without changing the group's collapsed flag. The refreshed row therefore becomes visible until the next search, sort, group toggle, or other explicit draw reapplies that flag. The overview retains this behavior. The refresh records the committed table draw when its response completes, so a reply arriving after a newer draw can still reveal that row. Other table consumers keep their existing group behavior.

All 548 frontend tests passed in 53 files. Frontend lint, formatting, type checks, the production build, and diff whitespace checks passed. New regressions failed before the message receiver and grouped refresh fixes. Passing cases cover direct and broadcast bags, foreign and malformed input, parent and summary refresh, listener cleanup, collapsed rows, a draw while a reply is pending, and two product reads completing in reverse order. Reverse completion intentionally permits the older request's response to become the final displayed value, matching the native independent callbacks. These checks use controlled HTTP replies without timing sleeps. No backend production code changed, and no new real-browser or release-image comparison was performed.

Full table reset and page-departure behavior for already pending reads still require work. The remaining embedded modal messages, success-message rendering and undo, cross-frame broadcast propagation, complete product-dialog behavior, unit-format failures, and rendered color and device comparisons also remain open. Full legacy compliance remains incomplete.

### Pending overview reads and embedded receipt commands

Pending targeted overview reads now belong to a page/reset request scope. A table reset or departure aborts that scope. Replies from a source that ignores abort cannot patch the replacement table or start follow-up parent reads. Cancellation does not create an error notice. A normal search, sort, or overlapping product refresh does not cancel the scope, so the previously checked native completion-order behavior remains intact. The pinned table reset deletes its saved state and reloads the page; the React reset retains its client-side implementation while reproducing the old page's read cancellation.

Authenticated application pages now receive same-origin `ShowSuccessMessage` and `CloseLastModal` bags. Success receipts retain supported inline formatting and ordinary HTTP(S) links. Active elements and supplied event handlers are not mounted. Recognized single- or double-quoted `UndoStockTransaction` links become typed Undo buttons instead of executable inline JavaScript. Stock undo posts once, reports success, reads the transaction's first booking, and broadcasts its product change so an active overview can refresh that product and its parent. Remaining stock, recipe, and shopping readers are invalidated. Modal close commands select the last ordinary open dialog and invoke its existing cancel/close handling, restoring focus through the dialog lifecycle.

All 554 frontend tests passed in 54 files. Lint, formatting, type checks, the production build, and diff whitespace checks passed. The reset regression first demonstrated a late response replacing the fresh amount `4` with old amount `2`; it now retains `4` and verifies cancellation and absence of a parent follow-up. The receipt regression first failed because the host did not show the message. Passing integration cases cover both native quote forms, formatted text, removal of active markup and arbitrary inline code, stock undo and targeted refresh, foreign senders, nested modal order, and focus restoration. jsdom's own-window message delivery is given the real browser sender origin in the undo integration check. No backend production change, new real-browser check, or release-image comparison is claimed.

The remaining host protocol includes iframe modal creation, responsive embed sizing, propagation of broadcasts to child frames, and reload handling. Other domain undo commands, pending-form close behavior, richer receipt formats, complete product-dialog behavior, callback and formatter failure combinations, and rendered/device checks also require comparison. Full legacy compliance remains incomplete.

### Iframe host dialogs, responsive embeds, and broadcast delivery

The authenticated host now accepts `IframeModal` bags and maintains a stack of embedded-page dialogs. Supplied HTTP(S) page URLs, query fields, and dialog classes are retained. The ordinary form, wider, and table layouts use the established dialog styling. Active URL schemes and foreign-origin commands cannot open frames. Closing nested frames restores focus in stack order, and changing the parent route removes its frames.

`ResizeResponsiveEmbeds` now sizes responsive iframe content from the child document. Iframe load, window resize, and dialog opening also trigger sizing. Full-screen mode uses the parent body's height. Responsive PDF embeds retain the pinned available-height calculation and `resize-done` marker. Cross-origin documents are not read when the browser denies access. A native `Reload` bag calls the browser reload operation; this dispatch is implemented but its navigation has not been exercised in a real browser.

The host now performs the native broadcast delivery: it posts the original inner message to its own window and forwards the original envelope to every live responsive child frame. Page-specific product receivers consume the local message once. Closing a frame removes it from later delivery. The host tests supply the sender origin that real browsers provide, since jsdom omits it for own-window `postMessage` delivery.

All 560 frontend tests passed in 54 files, with lint, formatting, type checks, the production build, and diff whitespace checks passing. The iframe regression first failed because no host dialog opened. New checks cover URLs and layout classes, nested close/focus order, frame load and resize messages, full-screen sizing, PDF resize markers, foreign-origin rejection, once-only local delivery, child delivery, and removed frames. A separate navigation regression exposed a shared modal scroll-lock defect: removing two nested dialogs together left body scrolling disabled. The dialog hook now retains a shared lock until the final owner closes, independent of cleanup order, and that regression passes.

This evidence covers DOM and message integration rather than real frame network loading, physical devices, or release-image rendering. Reload navigation, cross-origin and nested-host browser behavior, pending-form close rules, richer receipt formats and other domain undo actions, complete product-dialog behavior, remaining application forms and preferences, translations, and the broader parity queue still require work or comparison. Full legacy compliance remains incomplete.

### Chore and battery receipt undo

Success receipts now recognize the pinned `UndoChoreExecution(id)` and `UndoChargeCycle(id)` links. Each becomes a typed Undo button that posts to the existing chore-execution or battery-charge undo endpoint and uses the corresponding native success message. Successful actions invalidate the current household state, its history, the relevant overview readers, and calendar events. Receipt commands are matched in full. Household IDs must be positive safe integers; appended JavaScript, negative or fractional IDs, zero, and unsafe integers cannot become actions. Supplied event handlers remain absent from mounted receipt content. A rejected undo shows the API error without claiming success or automatically repeating the write.

### Stock booking receipts and closing busy dialogs

The receipt host now recognizes `UndoStockBookingEntry(bookingId, stockRowId, productId)` and its quoted two-argument form. It posts one booking undo, reports `Booking successfully undone`, and broadcasts only a supplied product ID. The pinned function does not use `stockRowId`. With no supplied product, the host invalidates other stock readers without guessing a product for the overview's cached rows. Every numeric argument must be a positive safe integer. The pinned entry editor accesses `result.id` on its booking array and can generate an undefined booking argument; that malformed receipt cannot become an actionable API write.

The pinned body Escape handler sends `CloseLastModal`, and the host hides its last ordinary modal even while a form is busy. React dialogs now allow both host close and native keyboard cancellation during a pending operation. Input buttons retain their pending state. The unresolved-product workflow carries the pinned `custom-escape-key-handling` marker, so the host's modal selection skips it. Closing a dialog releases its focus and scrolling ownership through the existing lifecycle.

Submitted writes continue without replay after a dialog is dismissed. Shopping-item success callbacks now check that their owning dialog is still open before notifying the parent. Stock booking-dialog completion callbacks similarly check their dialog before requesting close, and the stock-entry editor checks before invoking its owner's saved callback. The shopping regression covers closing a pending form, opening a second form and entering a new draft, then letting the old write and its saved-item lookup complete without closing the new draft.

All 568 frontend tests passed in 54 files. The 18 receipt/host integration cases also passed after strengthening the invalid-command checks to inspect every receipt before toast expiry or eviction could conceal an erroneous action. Lint, formatting, type checks, the production build, and diff whitespace checks passed. The initial booking regression lacked an Undo button; the initial pending-close regression retained its busy dialog. A subsequent late-save regression exposed the old form closing the second draft, and now retains that draft for both host close and keyboard cancellation. Modal selection also retains a custom-close dialog while closing the ordinary one below it. These checks use DOM integration and simulated native cancel events; no real-browser navigation, backend production change, or release-image comparison is claimed.

Further product-card comparison confirmed that hiding the own-stock wrapper while retaining the separate opened amount already matches the pinned source. JavaScript quantity-unit interpolation and inactive-unit plural selection still differ and remain implementation work. Other remaining comparisons include callbacks already inside asynchronous parent work when a dialog closes, nested custom workflow removal, richer receipt formats, complete product-dialog behavior, other application forms and preferences, translations, rendered browser comparisons, and physical devices and printers. Full legacy compliance remains incomplete.

### Product-card quantity formats and callback ownership

Product-card stock quantities now use the pinned browser quantity domain. Active units select the locale's plural form; inactive units use the untranslated English fallback. Unit placeholders receive the absolute count formatted with the browser's default precision, independently of the saved precision for the visible amount. Zero, negative, singular, fractional, and aggregate quantities retain these rules. The overview's partial row refresh uses the same quantity helper.

The card now projects responses in native callback order. A malformed own-stock unit writes the new name, description, and raw own amount, then retains the prior unit and later fields. A malformed aggregate unit additionally retains the new raw stock value, dates, location, spoil rate, own unit, and aggregate amount, but stops before later visibility, opened amounts, action targets, and price/history changes. A first-read failure leaves later values blank and actions disabled. Successful completion applies number/date formatting and refreshes price history; a later valid response recovers normally. A null location retains the prior location. The pinned average-price empty branch's retained tooltip is also preserved, while an empty last price clears its tooltip.

Dialog openings now have individual identities. Completion callbacks from overview shopping, product-card shopping, shopping-list actions, and product relations can close only their own opening. Shopping-list completion also checks its identity before changing the selected list. This extends the earlier mounted-dialog guard to callbacks already inside an awaited parent refresh. Closing a product overview also guards its later callback against closing a replacement overview.

All 579 frontend tests passed in 55 files. Lint, formatting, type checks, the production build, and diff whitespace checks passed. Nine product-card boundary cases cover unit domains, own/aggregate formatter failures, first-read failure, recovery, and retained location/tooltip behavior. The old component showed uninterpolated unit placeholders, replaced later fields after a bad format, and discarded the retained average-price tooltip. The aggregate regression was also checked against the old component after correcting its asynchronous wait. The dialog regressions first showed a new shopping draft disappearing when an older post-save refresh completed; both host close and simulated keyboard cancellation now retain that draft for held writes and held refresh reads. The shared booking-screen run passed all 122 tests before the full frontend run.

This evidence covers React/DOM integration and comparison with the pinned source. No new backend production change, real-browser run, physical device check, or release-image comparison is claimed. Remaining work includes card state across close/reopen, overlapping card and history requests, nested custom workflow removal, further editor/navigation callback lifetimes, richer receipt formats, other application forms and preferences, translations, rendered browser comparisons, and physical camera, torch, and printer behavior. Full legacy compliance remains incomplete.

### Retained product cards and overlapping replies

Each stock overview, entries view, and journal now owns its product-card memory. Closing its overview dialog retains the projected fields rather than reconstructing them from the last raw response. Inline booking cards own their own memory. This preserves prior fields after closing and reopening a card whose most recent response stopped partway through a malformed unit format. Card queries have owner-specific keys beneath their existing product prefix, so existing product invalidations still refresh the applicable cards without sharing a displayed snapshot between separate owners.

Product and price-history requests follow the owner's page lifetime. Query bookkeeping can cancel when a dialog closes or a selection changes, while its underlying native callback continues under the owner's request scope. A new opening can start another read of the same product. Replies update the retained card in arrival order, including an older selection arriving after a newer one. Leaving the owner aborts that scope; a transport that ignores abort cannot update its card or start another history read afterward.

History is retained alongside the card. A pending refresh keeps the preceding prices visible. Each history reply applies the conversion factor captured by its own product callback, even if another product callback or history reply has completed since. An empty response replaces the prior history with the native empty hint. A failed refresh retains prior prices and exposes retry. Closing and reopening the dialog can display history that completed while hidden.

All 584 frontend tests passed in 55 files, with lint, formatting, type checks, the production build, and diff whitespace checks passing. New regressions first showed lost partial card fields after repeated reopen, a late product reply being discarded, and a late history reply being discarded. Passing checks also cover two overlapping same-product openings, reverse completion, page-departure cancellation without history follow-ups, and history failure/recovery. The product-card fixture now runs in React Strict Mode, and its mutable reference data is isolated from the shared stock-entry fixture. Canvas library execution is not mocked; these DOM tests verify the accessible history table and request lifecycle, not rendered chart pixels or external script loading.

No backend production change, new real-browser run, physical device check, or release-image comparison is claimed. Remaining work includes resetting retained card memory with native table-option page resets, nested custom workflow removal, further editor/navigation callback lifetimes, richer receipt formats, other application forms and preferences, translations, rendered browser comparisons, and physical camera, torch, and printer behavior. Full legacy compliance remains incomplete.

### Table-reset card cleanup and external barcode lookup

A completed table-option reset now replaces the retained product-card memory in stock overview, entries, and journal views. Its old request scope is disposed, its projected fields are discarded, and description expansion returns to its default. Ordinary close/reopen continues to retain memory. A transport that ignores abort cannot repopulate the replacement card or start a history read from an old reply. This reproduces the card cleanup caused by the pinned table reset's page reload within the existing client-side reset implementation.

The native external-barcode button closes its workflow chooser as it begins the lookup and disables the owning form's inputs. The shared picker now dismisses its chooser at mutation start and reports external-lookup form locking separately from ordinary product resolution. Shopping, purchase, consume, inventory, and transfer forms apply that lock to the complete form, including custom fields and action buttons. Existing field layouts retain their display and spacing through the lock fieldset. A no-result response unlocks the form and restores product-input focus/selection. Lookup failures retain the API error outside the dismissed chooser; successful lookup still continues through the product editor.

The nested custom-workflow removal question was checked against the embedded source: destroying the owning iframe also destroys its product-picker dialog. The React portal already follows the form owner's lifetime. An application message-boundary check now confirms that a foreign close command leaves both dialogs open, while an accepted host close removes the owning shopping form and its custom-close workflow and restores scrolling. No extra modal-detachment behavior was introduced.

All 587 frontend tests passed in 55 files. Lint, formatting, type checks, the production build, and diff whitespace checks passed. The reset regression first observed an old hidden card request remaining un-aborted; it now checks cancellation, absence of follow-up history, empty later fields after a new malformed first read, and reset expansion after recovery. The external-lookup regression first found the chooser still open during a held request. Passing cases check whole-form disabling, chooser dismissal, no-result recovery, error display, and the existing successful editor return flow. The nested-workflow removal check passed before and after these changes and confirms the existing ownership behavior.

No backend production change, new real-browser run, physical device check, or release-image comparison is claimed. Remaining work includes reset callback/error ordering, further editor/navigation lifetimes, native generic-error notification/detail behavior, richer receipt formats, other application forms and preferences, translations, rendered browser comparisons, and physical camera, torch, and printer behavior. Full legacy compliance remains incomplete.

### Generic error notifications and technical details

External barcode lookup and primary master-data record saves now use the pinned generic-error flow: a translated error notice offers `Click to show technical details`, which dismisses that notice and opens an `Error details` dialog. Its OK action and native cancel event close only the detail dialog. The form and its draft remain available. Detail dialogs have their own lifetime rather than depending on the originating notification timer. Error reporting is scoped to its caller, so an external lookup failing after its form has closed cannot produce a notice on the replacement page.

API errors retain their original response text alongside the existing parsed body. The pinned XMLHttpRequest API uses its default text response type, and `ShowGenericError` stringifies that response; JSON error responses therefore normally appear as quoted strings with escaped content. The helper also preserves the direct-object `error_message` branch, indented JSON fallback, null, arrays, and undefined. Technical content is rendered as literal text rather than executable markup. The shared wider-dialog rule now retains the source's 800-pixel maximum for detail dialogs and product workflows.

Notifications now use the pinned 20-second timeout and five-second hover extension. Hovering pauses expiry. Focused notification actions also remain available during keyboard interaction. An open detail dialog is unaffected by notification expiry. Existing Undo actions and migration of the notification host into the active dialog retain their previous behavior.

All 595 frontend tests passed in 55 files. After the final typing, formatting, and wider-dialog adjustments, all 45 affected notification, shopping, and product-relation tests passed again. Lint, formatting, type checks, the production build, and diff whitespace checks passed. The application regression first failed because the lookup showed only its inline API error. It now checks the generic notice, exact raw response text, detail opening/closing, and retained list selection before continuing through the existing successful editor flow. Product-save failure cases check that inspecting details causes no extra record write or deletion before retry. Other cases cover literal hostile markup, the exception formatting branches, keyboard activation, timeout/hover/focus behavior, detail lifetime, and suppression of a departed form's late error. The old notification component also failed the native timeout regression by expiring before 20 seconds.

No backend production change, new real-browser run, physical device check, or release-image comparison is claimed. Further comparison and integration remain for other native generic-error callers, including dedicated list/user forms, merge and webhook failures, and secondary write failures. Reset callback/error ordering, further editor/navigation lifetimes, richer receipts, other application forms and preferences, translations, rendered browser comparisons, and physical camera, torch, and printer behavior remain open. Full legacy compliance remains incomplete.

### Household tracking and meal-plan bookmarks

Dedicated `/choretracking` and `/batterytracking` pages now provide active-record selection, Grocycode entry and camera dispatch, editable tracking times, chore date-only mode, done-by assignments, manual-chore skip restrictions, custom log fields, receipts and Undo. The tracking pages are linked from the existing chore and battery pages and obey their feature flags. Submission also observes the current actor’s tracking permissions. Battery timestamps reject future dates; minute-only browser datetime values are normalized to seconds. Exact names take precedence over case-insensitive search so case-distinct records keep their identities.

The custom-field write follows the record write. A failed secondary write retains the saved record ID and offers retry for its remaining fields, avoiding a duplicate booking. This retry recovery is an application extension; the underlying compatibility endpoints retain their existing partial-write behavior. Dirty custom values remain available for the next booking, as in the native form. Selected files receive a fresh filename for each booking so replacing or clearing a later draft cannot delete an earlier booking’s attachment. Chore writes and Undo invalidate stock, recipe and shopping availability as well as household views because chores may consume stock.

Chore assignees are read through `/api/v2/chores/tracking/users`, which accepts chore-domain or tracking permission and returns only `id`, `username` and `display_name`. This avoids requiring user-management directory permission just to use the native tracking workflow. The native `/api/users` permission boundary is unchanged. Its backend regression first failed with a missing route and now exercises allowed/denied actors, the safe projection and execution on another user’s behalf.

At this checkpoint, `/mealplan` was an alias for the additional modern planner. The native calendar increment below replaces that alias; `/meal-plan` retains its separate in-memory navigation and atomic Complete workflow. The pinned planner ignores the upstream `week` parameter, and that defect remains unchanged: native bookmarks consume `start`.

The frontend now registers GET entry points for `/login` and `/logout`, including subdirectory hosting. Direct login uses the existing v2 session API and then follows `/` to the configured entry page. Only `invalid=true` displays the native invalid-credentials hint; auth `returnto` is ignored as in the pinned controller. Logout shares a session helper with the application shell. A server failure retains the authenticated session and exposes retry rather than silently leaving a cookie authenticated. Native POST `/login` wire compatibility, remember-me behavior and broader auth-mode comparisons are not established by these frontend routes.

The tracking, meal-plan and authentication increment passed all 623 frontend tests in 58 files, frontend lint/format/type checks and the production build. The tracking-assignee backend owner and sibling tests also passed. Native card edit links, additional household editor/navigation behavior, browser rendering, physical devices and release-image checks remain open. Filtered journals are covered by the next increment below. These routes do not establish full Grocy parity.

### Household journals

The native `/choresjournal` and `/batteriesjournal` routes now display filtered log history, active-record choices, skipped/late/undone states, chore assignees and log custom fields. Their v2 presentation endpoints preserve the pinned controllers’ strict tracking-time cutoff and SQLite calendar arithmetic. Chores default to 12 months and batteries to 24. PHP integer validation selects valid filters while the accepted month text remains unchanged inside a bound SQLite date modifier, including the native empty-result behavior for signed or whitespace-bearing modifiers. Invalid record filters are ignored. Inactive records remain absent from choices and do not acquire a name through a broader join.

The journals support saved column visibility, reorder, grouping and sort through the existing native table-state helpers. Tracking time sorts descending initially; chore creation time is hidden by default. Local Undo updates the row and native receipt without replacing the table’s cached initial cells. The native chore button’s incorrect selector leaves a newly undone execution usable until reload, whereas the battery button disables after Undo. Initially undone rows are disabled in both. Clearing an embedded chore journal keeps its chore selection; clearing a battery journal removes its battery selection. Unrelated query text is preserved. Tracking cards open the selected journal in the shared table dialog, and overview pages expose direct journal links.

Undo completion after journal departure invalidates affected caches without writing visual state or showing a receipt on the replacement page. Tracking-page Undo retains its preceding card values, as in the pinned source; successful tracking still explicitly refreshes its card. The regression for retained card values was shown to fail for both domains when the incorrect global card invalidation was reintroduced, then pass with that invalidation removed.

The 12 journal frontend owner tests pass, including a held Undo response after navigation. Both direct journal routes first failed their workflow tests because they were absent. Eight new backend journal cases and the two tracking/schedule siblings pass, covering permission grants, date boundaries, custom fields, undone state, raw filter spellings and SQLite month overflow. No separate preimplementation red run was captured for the backend journal cases. All 635 frontend tests in 59 files pass after integration, with frontend lint, formatting, application/node/test TypeScript checks, the production build, backend Ruff/Ty checks and diff whitespace checks passing. The full backend run completed with 1,216 passed and 1,137 skipped in 18m53s with `GROCY_USE_NATIVE_PHP=false`. The eight journal cases added after that run collected its tests passed separately on the final source, giving 1,224 distinct passing backend cases across the two runs. Reference-server and environment-dependent skips remain skips; no live PHP differential comparison, browser/device check or release-image check is claimed.

At that checkpoint, mobile filter collapse and native named editors still required subsequent work. The following increment implements them; further scheduling/assignment comparisons, rendered browser checks and physical-device/release checks remain open. This increment does not establish full Grocy parity.

### Household master lists, editors and native pickers

`/chores` and `/batteries` now serve their native master-data purpose; operational pages have separate `/choresoverview` and `/batteriesoverview` routes. Navigation and configured entry-page mappings distinguish those destinations. Master lists use active records by default and include inactive records when `include_disabled` is present, including a value of `0`. They expose native Add/Edit destinations, configured custom columns, saved column visibility/reorder/group/sort, literal search, delete confirmation and chore merge. The interval column sorts numerically. Clear resets search and the checkbox without unloading already included inactive records, matching the pinned list handlers. Battery and task-category list editors open embedded forms; chore list editors navigate directly. Tracking-card editors navigate directly for both domains. Selected row journals continue to open in table dialogs.

Named editors now exist at `/chore/{id}`, `/battery/{id}`, `/task/{id}` and `/taskcategory/{id}`, with `new` create routes and `/taskcategories` master data. They load the selected record rather than redirecting into an unselected generic editor. Chore controls cover all seven period types, retained hidden schedule validation, weekly configuration, assignment order and selection, start-date history locking, date-only/rollover/active flags and stock consumption. Existing consumption amounts round to configured precision as in the native number picker. Save order is object, custom fields, assignment recalculation, then `/chores`, including the native chore form’s embedded navigation behavior. Battery/task/category saves write the object before custom fields, then navigate or send native parent Reload. New tasks support Save & close and Save & add another, with current-user assignment, active categories and date-only due values. Completed state is preserved rather than exposed as an unrelated form checkbox.

`/api/v2/chores/{id}/editor` and `/api/v2/tasks/form-context` supply limited form context without requiring the user-management directory permission. Chore form users retain native DTO order; task users retain native username ordering. Form reads accept the relevant domain/action or master-data grant; native writes still require `MASTER_DATA_EDIT`. ISO start dates normalize to server time, and malformed legacy date text follows the epoch fallback. Arbitrary PHP relative-date strings remain outside that parser’s established behavior.

The shared user picker now follows the pinned display-text matcher, ranking, ID prefills, duplicate-label mapping, typing/clearing/blur rules and keyboard/mouse selection. It replaces assignee selects in task editing and chore tracking. A visible-menu guard prevents premature form submission. Chore consumption uses the shared barcode/Grocycode/name/camera picker with all creation, assignment and external workflows disabled. Unknown optional text remains unselected and cannot open one of those workflows. Product unit text and amount focus follow selection; exact product spelling takes precedence over folded matches.

Custom-field saving accepts a per-owner abort signal and checks it between sequential writes, including file deletion/upload. The new editor owners and tracking form cancel it on departure. A completed primary write retains its ID across secondary failure/retry, so recovery does not create another object. Late callbacks cannot start later writes or assignment recalculation, emit replacement-page notices or trigger embedded Reload after departure. Primary save failures retain the native generic-error detail flow. Code previews/downloads use authenticated native resource URLs so a master-data editor does not also need the domain-specific v2 image permission. Label actions retain the existing printer path.

The mobile journal filter control is now present, collapsed below the native medium breakpoint with an accessible expanded-state toggle. The table and forms retain the existing project’s styling. This is DOM/route and code-level verification; browser layout, physical camera/torch/printer behavior and the release image have not been rechecked.

All 693 frontend tests passed in 64 files after integration. Frontend lint, formatting, application/node/test TypeScript checks and the production build passed. The 14 selected backend household/context/journal/scheduling/permission cases passed with native PHP disabled; full backend Ruff, formatting and Ty checks passed. The earlier full backend run remains the preceding increment’s evidence and was not rerun in this increment. The first full frontend attempt found two Grocycode checks still targeting the old overview URLs; those checks were moved to the restored overview destinations with their selected-code download assertions preserved, then all cases passed. Initial route tests demonstrated the missing editor/overview and incorrect master-list behavior. Additional cases cover hidden schedule validation, amount rounding, literal picker input and save cancellation. Explicit pre-fix failures were captured for the legacy start-date parser, deferred amount-focus behavior and the old assignee control. Several other refinement cases were authored after their fixes; their passing results are not a substitute for pre-fix failure evidence.

The [household master source notes](household-master-parity.md) identify the narrower comparison at that checkpoint. The following increment implements operational overview/task-table controls, household settings and raw flag checkbox handling. Arbitrary PHP relative-date parsing, further custom picker/editor interactions, broader settings and translation effects, real-browser comparisons and device/release checks remain open. Restoring these route families does not prove complete household or whole-application parity.

### Native household overviews, tasks and preferences

The operational chores and batteries pages now use the native columns, due-state messages and filters, custom columns, saved layouts, sorting/grouping and selected-row menus. Chores support next-schedule/now tracking, skip, user assignments, card details and rescheduling. Batteries support quick charging, cards, selected journals/editors and native authenticated code downloads. Overview quick actions retain their native success notices without adding the tracking forms’ Undo receipt. Description formatting for chore cards is prepared by Python. All three new presentation reads are authenticated-only, matching their native page controllers; they return limited labels instead of raw user records. Native mutation permissions remain unchanged, including the chore reschedule form’s tracking-grant/object-write-grant mismatch.

Partial refreshes preserve the pinned table behavior. A battery charge updates visible dates and row classes without invalidating cached search, status or sort values. A chore update refreshes visible fields immediately while retaining the hidden assigned-user token. The initial fixed 500-millisecond table-cache delay is corrected to the native startup-dependent delay by the later Chore action increment below. Initial today rows also match due-soon, whereas a refreshed chore today token no longer does. Battery highlighting retains an existing today class when adding its new warning/danger state. Untracked batteries can be initially overdue while their null dates are excluded from browser statistics. The alternate chore tracking notice preserves its native missing-name result. Cards retain page-owned fields across hide/reopen and apply overlapping replies in completion order; their action targets follow the displayed response.

The tasks page now exposes the native due/category/assigned/custom columns, Search, Status, Category and Show done controls, due statistics, saved layouts, selected editors and completion/Undo/delete actions. Complete with Show done checked strikes and mutes the row and disables its Complete action; initially completed rows instead offer Undo. Undo reloads page state. Completion without Show done and deletion remove a visible row while retaining its cached table node, so another draw can restore it until reload. Delete retains the preceding statistics. Clear leaves the Show done checkbox unchanged, matching the source’s ineffective event. Extra user filters, bulk completion clearing and task code controls are absent from this native page.

Native settings routes now exist at `/choressettings`, `/batteriessettings` and `/taskssettings`, with change-only autosave, validation, defaults and their native OK destinations. Chore tracking-button swapping uses the source PHP boolean rules in the overview and browser boolean rules in its settings checkbox. The chore and battery due-soon inputs deliberately write the source’s keys with leading spaces, so those controls leave the canonical overview thresholds unchanged. Task due-soon settings use their effective canonical key. Bare `include_done`/`include_disabled` flags check their controls; explicitly empty values include records but leave the controls unchecked. A value of `0` checks them. Master list toggles now preserve bare flags during navigation, including task categories.

Busy handling now follows the native cursor-only rule, so pending tracking/completion requests do not lock other rows or filters. Independent statistics replies apply in completion order. Successful table resets replace action/card lifetimes, clear queued chore cache updates and old mutation errors, and close page-owned dialogs. Held reads cannot begin later writes or printer webhooks after reset; held completed writes cannot change the replacement table or issue an old success notice. The reload itself has a separate page lifetime, preventing a delayed replacement read from leaving the table permanently loading.

All 767 frontend tests passed in 68 files on the final source. Frontend lint, formatting, application/node/test TypeScript checks and the production build passed. The 14 selected Python household overview/form/scheduling/permission checks passed with `GROCY_USE_NATIVE_PHP=false`; owned backend Ruff, formatting and Ty checks and diff whitespace checks passed. The preceding full backend run was not repeated. Frontend baseline failures demonstrated the absent settings pages/native columns, incorrect raw flag handling, lost hidden card replies, delayed-reset loading, stale aborted-request errors, blocked overlapping actions, reset callbacks beginning later writes/webhooks, and discarded late statistics replies. The new backend context cases were not separately run against a preimplementation baseline. No new live reference-server differential, real-browser/device or release-image comparison is claimed.

The [household overview source notes](household-overview-parity.md) record the settings keys and native controller/template boundaries. The suites exercise actual application routes with HTTP fixtures and disposable Python databases. This establishes the checked DOM, persistence and lifecycle behavior; real-browser layout, translated rendering, physical devices/printers and release-image behavior remain separate open gates. Other native web families and broader settings still require implementation and comparison. Full Grocy parity remains incomplete.

### Stock master lists and selected reference forms

`/locations` now serves the native master list. The existing stock-contents tool remains available at `/stockbylocation`, with its own navigation link. `/productgroups`, `/shoppinglocations` and `/quantityunits` provide their native lists and selected Add/Edit destinations. Reference lists include names, descriptions, configured custom columns, active/inactive rows, saved table layouts and selected deletion. Product-group counts include only active products, and their links select the real catalog’s product-group filter. Catalog filtering follows the selected group’s displayed name, including case-insensitive equal names, and excludes disabled products unless the query includes them. Clear retains the group bookmark while clearing the visible filter.

The stock reference lists’ Clear action clears search without changing Show disabled, unlike the earlier household list handlers. Server inclusion and browser checkbox initialization remain separate: PHP-style parameter presence normalizes dots and spaces in keys and accepts valid bracket arrays, while the browser matcher tests the raw flag spelling and value. An explicitly empty flag includes disabled records without checking the box. An unclosed first bracket registers a different key instead of matching a valid array. The shared presence helper is also used by quantity-unit and household master-list inclusion. Product-group completion messages refresh both records and active product counts; successful deletion returns to the active list. Table resets and delayed reloads have separate ownership from preceding actions.

Selected forms now exist at `/location/{id}`, `/productgroup/{id}`, `/shoppinglocation/{id}`, `/quantityunit/{id}`, `/quantityunitconversion/{id}` and `/productbarcodes/{id}`, including their `new` conventions. Location/store saves write the object before custom fields and then either send native Reload or return to their master list. Product-group saves always send CloseLastModal, even standalone. With freezing disabled, location saves submit the native hidden freezer value zero. These native reference forms show editable controls to authenticated readers even when a subsequent API write is denied; the backend permission checks are unchanged. Custom-field recovery retains a newly created ID and departure guards prevent later writes or replacement-page completion messages.

Quantity units provide singular/plural names, language-specific plural forms, active state, descriptions, custom values, Save & continue/return and default conversion management. Conversion forms preserve unit/product context and native completion destinations/messages. `/quantityunitpluraltesting` accepts its selected unit query and uses the current locale’s plural rule. The Test action preserves the source’s ineffective save trigger: its referenced save-button ID does not exist, so it opens an existing unit’s stored values without saving the draft; a new unit has no ID and neither saves nor opens the tester. The pinned unit form’s ineffective Enter action remains ineffective; conversion forms retain their working Enter submission.

The barcode path selects a barcode record, while `product` supplies its product context. Its unit choices use the saved conversion cache rather than recomputing from the derived view, retain inactive unit metadata, and exclude unrelated global rows. The native form includes barcode text, optional amount/unit/store/note, custom fields and scanning, with feature-dependent hidden defaults and resolved quantity-unit choices. Editing starts dirty custom-field requests independently before the primary PUT; creation starts them after POST. Native primary success posts ProductBarcodesChanged and CloseLastModal without waiting for those requests. A successful standalone form remains disabled and busy. Plain custom-field metadata failures are silent, including file metadata; only subsequent file deletion/upload failures use the native generic-error notice. Conversion hints apply the browser quantity-unit formatter, including unit placeholders and inactive-unit fallback. The native product editor now opens selected barcode/conversion/resolved-conversion iframe links and follows its source completion reload. The existing catalog editor retains its local dialog extension and draft/retry behavior. Actual browser reload execution remains outside DOM-test evidence.
