### Native custom-data configuration and objects

`/userfields`, `/userentities` and `/userobjects/{name}` now provide dedicated native lists, selected Add/Edit destinations, embedded forms, configured custom columns, search and saved table layouts. Field filtering uses the native partial entity match. Its first raw `entity` parameter controls initialization, including bare, duplicate, invalid and empty values. Clear resets the visible filter while retaining the preceding Add target, matching the source handler. Delete confirmations close when their request starts; a completion after page departure cannot redirect the replacement page. Sidebar entries use flagged userentities, literal captions, binary name order and configured Font Awesome classes. The pinned font assets and their license are bundled locally.

Selected editors exist at `/userfield/{id}`, `/userentity/{id}` and `/userobject/{name}/{id}`, including `new`. Definition controls cover all 14 native types, preset configuration, date defaults, table visibility and mandatory input. Hidden configuration/default values remain in the write. Userentity edits preserve their internal name and omit disabled icon values. Userobjects contain namespace custom fields without invented name or description controls. Their namespace is selected by the URL; as in the native controller, fetching an existing object ID is unscoped and saving it assigns the URL's userentity. These forms expose native editable controls to authenticated readers; mutation grants remain enforced by the existing APIs.

Custom-object writes retain the source's independent dirty-field requests and completion on the last dirty field's callback, rather than waiting for every earlier request. Metadata failures remain silent, including file metadata; later file deletion/upload failures show the generic-error flow. Required stored file/image inputs require a new selection in these native object forms. Tests also cover numeric custom-field names returned as PHP-style arrays, file payloads, hidden values, retained failure state and departed owners. The editor waits for its first fresh namespace context before initializing defaults, then preserves the draft through later refetches.

Authenticated v2 presentation reads supply the native entity/type catalog, namespaced objects and ordered definitions. The shared custom-field reader now receives SQL `sort_number,name COLLATE NOCASE` order. SQLite sorts an empty TEXT sort value differently from SQL NULL, although the compatibility serializer emits both as null; client sorting after serialization cannot recover that distinction. The new read preserves storage ordering before serialization. Context tests cover exact case-sensitive namespaces, native loose object-ID matching, permission boundaries, deletion effects and this ordering case. No compatibility write endpoint, database schema or permission grant changed.

All 929 frontend tests passed in 78 files on the final source, along with frontend lint, formatting, application/node/test TypeScript checks, the production build and diff whitespace checks. Four new backend context cases and three existing siblings passed with Python execution; targeted backend Ruff, formatting and Ty checks passed. The full backend suite was not rerun. Pre-fix regressions covered missing list/editor routes, pending delete confirmation, raw entity/embedded parameters, busy cursor handling, required stored-file behavior, numeric custom-field names, fresh-context defaults and SQL ordering before serialization. The first broad frontend run caught the two encoded embedded-key regressions while their fixes were in progress; the final 929-case run passed after integration. No live PHP differential, real-browser/device or production-image comparison was performed in this increment.

Sources are the pinned Grocy 4.5.0 `GenericEntityController`, `UserfieldsService`, `BaseController` sidebar query, the three list/form templates, their `public/viewjs` scripts and the shared `userfieldsform` partial/script. Raw encoded-key coverage applies at the default frontend path; configured-base-path router canonicalization can normalize encoded keys before the form reads them, and preserving their original spelling there remains open. Further translated rendering, browser navigation/dialog/file validation, overlapping callbacks, physical-device and release-image checks remain open. These workflows do not establish complete custom-data or whole-application parity.

### Native shopping-list and equipment forms

Selected forms now exist at `/shoppinglist/{id}` and `/equipment/{id}`, including `new`. The shopping-list index keeps its existing redirect to the modern shopping page, while child routes render their selected editor. Shopping actions open real native iframe links and accept same-origin `ShoppingListChanged` receipts with string create IDs or numeric edit IDs. The receipt refreshes the selected list and its notes while retaining the frame until the following `CloseLastModal`; `Ready` does not close it. This avoids consuming the close message against another dialog after an early unmount. Invalid or foreign-origin receipts are ignored. Deletion closes confirmation when its request starts, returns to the default list without retaining the old query, and cannot navigate after its page has left.

Shopping-list creation saves the name before custom fields; editing saves custom fields before the name. Dirty custom fields start independently, and the last dirty field's callback controls completion. Success always posts `ShoppingListChanged`, `Ready` and `CloseLastModal`, with native payload types and order, including standalone use. The successful standalone form stays busy if no host closes it. Primary failures unlock the retained draft; an edit retry repeats dirty custom writes and generates fresh selected-file metadata. Field metadata failures remain silent, while subsequent file-stage failures show technical details without completing the form.

These selected forms and the native custom-object form use the shared control's native file behavior. Delete captures the initially stored file without clearing a newly selected replacement: stored A → select B → Delete still deletes A and uploads B. Delete is inert when the field had no initially stored file. Selecting or deleting hides the stored image/Show control, and Delete resets the label while retaining the browser selection. Required stored file/image fields still require a fresh selection. Other generic forms retain their existing file-control behavior.

The equipment master page now uses native Name/custom columns, selection, saved table controls, search, deletion and direct selected Add/Edit links. File fields are excluded from the table and have detail tabs regardless of their column flag. The initial selected row follows the table's current order. Manual presence chooses the manual or Notes tab on each detail completion. The source's completion-order selection behavior and stale file tabs when a later record has null userfields are retained. Notes, manual previews and downloads use authenticated document helpers. Generic equipment editing is also available through Manage's selected `entity=equipment` entry.

Equipment saves the primary record, then custom fields, then an optional manual upload before returning to `/equipment`; embedded presence does not change that destination. Unchanged manual metadata is omitted. Manual controls remain outside the busy form: the filename is generated at submission, but the upload reads the current file selection after the final field callback. Old-manual deletion starts independently before the record PUT. The pinned wrong reset flag means deletion still wins after selecting a replacement, and its incorrect preview selector leaves the old PDF visible on a normal replacement selection. A later Delete can suppress an upload already described by submitted metadata. Upload failures unlock the form, and retry repeats dirty custom writes.

The existing shopping-list form context and equipment Notes rendering/editor reads now allow authenticated readers, matching the native direct pages. Product/recipe content grants and all record mutation grants are unchanged. Backend regressions prove unauthenticated rejection, reader access, denied equipment and shopping-list writes, retained saved HTML, and the localized Notes label. No schema or compatibility write API changed.

All 982 frontend tests passed in 81 files on the final source. Frontend lint, formatting, application/node/test TypeScript checks, the production build, targeted backend Ruff/format/Ty checks and diff whitespace checks passed. Ten selected backend content/shopping cases passed with `GROCY_USE_NATIVE_PHP=false`; the full backend suite was not rerun. A temporary `nix shell nixpkgs#file` supplied the local MIME detection dependency needed by the existing image-print sibling, which had failed without it. Pre-fix regressions covered missing selected routes and equipment columns, index redirects swallowing child forms, native links and host receipts, pending delete/return query behavior, three-message modal lifetime, native file deletion sequences, and reader context/content grants. The first broad run exposed four older shopping fixtures that lacked router context; they now exercise their original unit, purchase, Notes and clear-list assertions through the real application routes. Required-file rejection and optional-file transport are covered; positive required-file browser validity remains unverified because JSDOM's internal file-list validation does not follow its simulated selected files. No live PHP differential, real-browser/device or production-image check was performed in this increment.

Sources are the pinned `StockController::ShoppingListEditForm`, `EquipmentController`, list/form templates and their `public/viewjs` scripts, plus the shared `UserfieldsForm` behavior. Real-browser frame/navigation/file validation, translated layout, physical devices and production-image checks remain open; these restored workflows do not establish full Grocy parity.

### Native recipe and ingredient forms

The singular `/recipe/{id}` and `/recipe/{id}/pos/{posId}` routes now provide selected forms, including `new`. Parent layouts render the ingredient child without loading the recipe editor alongside it. Overview Add/Edit links, gallery links and the reader's selected-card edit link retain the native record identity. The singular route family follows the Recipes feature flag. The existing generic recipe management remains available as an additional workflow.

The recipe form edits Name, base Servings, shopping-list checking, an optional produced product, custom fields and rich Preparation; desired servings remain a reader control. Initial drafts wait for fresh settings and context before rounding stored values. Native Enter remains ineffective. Primary creation failure stays silently busy, while an edit failure unlocks and logs without a generic notice. Successful record writes precede independent dirty custom fields, gated by the last field callback, and then an optional picture upload. Picture deletion starts independently before an edit PUT. Its filename is fixed at submission while the upload reads the live picker. Continue reloads the selected editor, and Return selects the saved recipe in `/recipes`, regardless of embedded presence. Selected recipes include the native 60px Grocycode preview alongside download/label actions. Authenticated preview loading retires pending responses and releases image URLs on departure.

Ingredient Add/Edit links open native iframe routes, with the original product query on edit. `IngredientsChanged` performs a current serialized parent PUT before reload, excluding unsaved custom fields and picture selections. Disabled parent inputs serialize to an empty object while busy. Included-recipe Add/Edit first performs that same partial parent save before opening its modal; selected servings persist through subsequent Add openings. The native choices include the current recipe. Nesting writes emit `IngredientsChanged` without a close message, and a completion after Cancel still affects its living parent page. Page departure and reset retire the old callbacks.

The ingredient form preserves native product prefill, barcode flows, variable amount, group, plain Note, price factor and stock/rounding flags. A direct edit without its `product` query leaves Product blank. Create choices are active products; edit choices and quantity units include inactive records. Conversions come from the saved cache, and the displayed quantity is converted into the submitted stock amount. A single available unit initially disables its selector and omits `qu_id`; a primary error reenables the selector, so retry includes it. The native first create-product reload clears an unmatched initial amount. Only-check-any-unit mode and its zero-minimum reset follow the pinned handlers. Barcode writes start independently, retain flow data on retry, and preserve the wrong-form busy-cursor error behavior. Successful ingredient saves always post `IngredientsChanged` followed by `CloseLastModal`.

Authenticated presentation reads provide native selected records, ordered collections and namespace fields without granting recipe mutation permissions. Ingredient ID lookup is independent of its URL parent; saving under that parent reassigns `recipe_id`. The native authenticated Copy exception remains available while normal create/update requires Recipes permission. Recipe Preparation documents use their translated label. Ingredient notes have a safe formatted display projection alongside their unchanged stored text. The ingredient table starts grouped, honors an explicitly saved ungrouped state, and uses native locale amount display.

All 1,030 frontend tests passed in 83 files. Frontend lint, formatting, application/node/test TypeScript checks, the production build, scoped backend Ruff/format/Ty checks and diff whitespace checks passed. Thirteen selected backend form/content/recipe cases passed with `GROCY_USE_NATIVE_PHP=false`; the full backend suite was not rerun. The existing image-print sibling used the temporary `nix shell nixpkgs#file` MIME dependency. Pre-fix regressions covered missing selected routes and links, singular feature gating, stale cached precision, empty product-name prefill, missing formatted notes, barcode retry and busy behavior, and Grocycode preview loading/cleanup. The 46 new form owner cases also cover primary/secondary save staging, live picture selections, embedded receipts, nesting add/edit/delete, table grouping/reset, record identity and departure cleanup. No live PHP differential, real-browser/device or production-image check was performed in this increment.

Sources are the pinned `RecipesController`, recipe/ingredient templates and scripts, product/amount/recipe picker scripts, shared custom-field form and DataTables overrides. Further raw-picker and overlapping callback cases remain open. Browser frames, required-file validation, translated rendering, hardware and production-image checks remain unverified. These selected workflows do not establish whole-family or whole-application parity.

### Recipe overview, preferences and live custom files

The recipe list now uses native saved table controls: the nine base column indexes include the initially hidden Desired servings and the numeric/status/product/group shadow columns, followed by configured recipe custom columns. Requirements grouping uses its separate status label. Custom values keep their recipe namespace, PHP loose object identity, saved null/empty behavior and blank unsaved defaults. List search includes hidden ingredient product names and formatted custom values. Saved column order, visibility, sorting and grouping survive navigation; reset reloads the native defaults. Initial table selection follows the saved table order while the initial recipe card follows the controller's alphabetical default. The explicit selected query overrides both. Clicking a selected native table cell deselects it; explicit name links retain their additional reader navigation.

Recipe rows now provide native copy and confirmed deletion alongside existing meal-plan and code actions. Copy selects the returned recipe and refreshes the list as well as the reader; successful deletion returns to the unfiltered native overview. Gallery mode preserves the stored-tab/query rule and independent search/status handlers: the last filter handler wins rather than combining both filters. Gallery selection borders follow the explicit recipe query. Native PHP layout truthiness and JavaScript click parsing remain separate, including their different interpretation of the string `false`.

`/recipessettings` provides the three native checkbox controls and an OK link back to recipes. Changes update in-memory settings immediately and persist independently, with no busy lock, rollback or visible autosave error; failed requests log to the console. Returning to recipes rereads saved settings. The side-by-side layout, temporary ingredient marks and product-group headings have workflow coverage. The reader's two ingredient flags now follow PHP truthiness while the settings controls follow JavaScript `BoolVal`. Ingredient marks reset on page departure.

Native custom-file uploads now read the current input when upload actually starts, after metadata and optional old-file deletion. The filename and delete/upload branch remain fixed by that save attempt. This applies to the recipe, equipment, shopping-list and custom-object selected forms; the additional generic editors retain their existing file behavior. Regressions change the real file input after an independent picture-delete failure unlocks a pending recipe form, at both metadata and deletion waits, and inspect the actual uploaded bytes. Departure aborts subsequent stages before reading the retired input.

Recipe custom table values preserve stored-row presence: an absent row omits its key, while imported empty text retains a key whose PDO-style read value is null. Normal API clearing removes the stored row through the existing native triggers. This distinction matters for native blank link/number markup and grouping; configured defaults are not inserted into missing list cells. Image, file and link projections retain native HTML group identity and Chinese-string ordering, including different files or destinations with the same displayed caption. Interactive group headers use authenticated document controls alongside an independent collapse button, and retain the native bubbling collapse behavior. Missing cells, stored-empty cells and the PHP-empty string `0` keep their distinct template behavior.

### Meal-plan section management

The native `/mealplansections` list and `/mealplansection/{id}` selected form, including literal `new`, now have dedicated authenticated presentation contexts. The list excludes reserved/nonpositive rows and retains SQLite's `sort_number` ordering before serialization; a direct selected lookup can still read an existing reserved or negative ID. Create does not invent a record or stored defaults. Existing compatibility writes continue to require Master Data editing, including for an actor with meal-plan permission alone.

The list has native Name, Sort number and Time columns, saved table controls, delayed smart search, `all` clearing and embedded Add/Edit links. Its inherited `chinese-string` ordering compares sort numbers as text, so `10` sorts before `2`; forcing a numeric comparator would change the reference behavior. Confirmed deletion closes the confirmation immediately, reloads the unfiltered list on success and logs failures without a generic error notice. The selected form requires Name, permits an empty nonnegative integral Sort number, and uses the browser's Time control. Stored zero sort numbers start blank, as in the PHP template. Input Enter preserves the native incorrect plural button target. Save failure restores controls and shows the native generic error; success returns to the section list or posts `Reload` when the raw embedded flag is present. The planner's Configure sections link reaches this list.

### Native meal-plan calendar

`/mealplan` now renders the native calendar, and application navigation points to it. The additional modern planner remains at `/meal-plan`. The native page provides responsive day/week views, configured and locale first-day rules, previous/Today/next navigation, used-section ordering, recipe/product/note cards, selected entry dialogs, copy-day and a print action. The first render normalizes `start` and `days` in the URL while retaining the initial controller context; subsequent range or view changes reload context and retire earlier page callbacks. Day view writes `days=0`; week view removes it. Unrelated parameters remain, including the ignored upstream `week` parameter. `MEAL_PLAN_FIRST_DAY_OF_WEEK=-1` starts the week on today’s weekday.

Day and week summaries read persisted internal recipes and resolved costs/calories, with native number, currency, energy-unit and feature rules. A day summary requires an actual rendered event and an existing day recipe. Entry actions keep separate mark-done, Undo, delete, stock consumption, shopping and recipe-reader sequences. Recipe consumption books the persisted shadow recipe before the separate done PUT; product consumption retains its stock/detail/done sequence and possible partial writes. The source's weekly consumption defect, which attempts the subsequent done PUT with an undefined entry ID, remains. The modern planner's atomic Complete action is an additional workflow.

Invalid raw calendar dates produce a controlled Invalid date error. The pinned FullCalendar implementation instead throws before rendering for those inputs; this is a documented error-presentation difference, not a repaired date or a `week`-to-`start` conversion. DOM tests and offline pinned-source probes cover calendar navigation and callback boundaries, but do not establish rendered browser, print or mobile equivalence.

Dispatched calendar writes invalidate related calendar, stock, shopping, recipe and generic-manager caches when they settle, including responses that arrive after departure. Retired callbacks do not reload or report errors on a replacement page. Undo remains available after a failed transaction reversal so it can be retried. Dialog save/copy uses the same invalidation boundary while retaining the native overlapping-save and copy-counter behavior.

The native page reads `/api/v2/meal-plan/calendar`. Its authenticated context supplies raw entry/normal-recipe data and stock details, normal recipe choices, products/units, saved conversions, all/used sections, persisted internal recipes and resolved aggregates, and the independently selected week recipe. The controller's default `days=6` selects entries within `start ±6 days`, not six displayed days. Native integer validation preserves accepted raw `days` text: signed or space-padded values can produce invalid SQLite date modifiers and an empty event range while the week recipe remains selected. Week names use the source's SQLite `%Y-%W` expression, including week zero, rather than ISO week numbering. The read does not repair missing persisted internal recipes or broaden write permissions. Events also expose sanitized `note_html` for display, preserving raw notes in the entry and compatibility responses and retaining newlines without inserting breaks. Four real-API owner cases cover these range, aggregate, validation, authentication and note-projection boundaries.

Before native calendar integration, all 1,079 frontend tests passed in 86 files, alongside frontend lint, formatting, application/node/test TypeScript checks and the production build. The 22 overview, eight settings and 17 section owner cases cover saved layouts, reader/generic-editor refresh, interactive custom groups, native flag parsing, autosave completion order, selected identities, errors, reset and departure behavior. Two additional file regressions inspect changed upload bytes at separate callback boundaries. Expected pre-fix failures were captured for missing routes/contexts, stale table/editor snapshots, cached settings, image/interactive grouping, lost stored-row presence and snapshot file bytes; not every workflow case has a separate preimplementation run. The integrated native calendar suite passed all 1,131 frontend tests in 90 files. The final 67 focused calendar, dialog, action, summary, modern-planner and notification cases passed after the generic-manager cache correction. Frontend lint, formatting, application/node/test TypeScript checks and the production build passed on the final source. Calendar cases cover the frame, summaries, dialogs and actions; observed pre-fix failures include missing native controls, active-view reloads, offset-date conversion, scrollbar breakpoints, omitted-card totals, lost Undo retry and settled-write cache invalidation.

The completed Python backend suite passed 1,251 tests, with 1,137 skipped and three warnings (`/tmp/root-native-backend-final-suite.log`). It includes the corrected authenticated shopping-form read boundary, recipe overview presence cases and new section/calendar contexts. This full run precedes the narrow `note_html` projection and the users/access, native-preferences and native-system increments below; all four calendar owner cases and scoped Ruff/format/Ty checks passed after the note change. These results overlap and must not be added together. The full run used `GROCY_USE_NATIVE_PHP=false` and the temporary `nix shell nixpkgs#file` MIME dependency. No new live PHP differential, real-browser/device or production-image check is claimed. Remaining work includes malformed/raw query and field cases, overlapping callback comparisons, translated and configured-path rendering, print layouts, files/frames and the release gates below. Native calendar implementation does not establish full Grocy parity.

### Native users, permissions and API keys

The `/users`, `/user/{id}`, `/user/{id}/permissions`, `/manageapikeys` and `/manageapikeys/new` routes now have dedicated native workflows. The user list and selected create/edit form provide profile controls, password/change-password controls, pictures and user custom fields. The list retains a complete native snapshot of rows, custom-field definitions and values until reload/reset, with delayed search, saved table controls and native file/image/link group identity. Selected forms preserve current custom edits and file bytes at their native callback stages. Raw selected IDs reach SQLite unchanged, so scientific and leading-zero identities resolve while hexadecimal/binary spellings cannot open a different record. The permissions page uses the native hierarchy: initial inherited grants are checked and disabled, changing a parent applies its checked/disabled state to every descendant, and Save submits only checked, enabled permission IDs. Removing one's own Admin selection asks for confirmation; No restores the hierarchy. The page read requires `USERS_READ`, while replacement through the compatibility API still requires `ADMIN`. Saving stays on the page, permits overlapping requests and retains the draft after a rejected replacement. The additional AccessManager remains available.

Five Python extensions supply the native pages: `GET /api/v2/users/overview`, `GET /api/v2/users/{id}/form-context` (including literal `new`), `GET /api/v2/users/{id}/permissions-context`, `GET /api/v2/access/api-keys-context`, and `GET /api/v2/access/api-keys/new`. User profiles omit password hashes. The overview retains native username ordering; field definitions retain sort-number/name ordering and resolved values retain their users namespace. Selected user contexts bind raw IDs to SQLite, preserving scientific and leading-zero identities, and use PHP loose equality for the self-edit permission check. New forms require `USERS_CREATE`; selected self forms require `USERS_EDIT_SELF`, and other-user forms require `USERS_EDIT`. Permission contexts expose the flat native `uihelper_user_permissions` rows for the recursive controls. Missing or nonmatching selected records return a controlled 404.

Authentication flags preserve the native password branches. LDAP and reverse-proxy authentication are externally managed; the list hides Add and the form omits password controls. With authentication disabled, the form instead supplies hidden password/confirmation values of `x`, and the list hides permission links. Otherwise password controls are present, with edit inputs initially disabled until Change password is selected. There is no backend embedded-install setting; embedded-install behavior is not implied by these flags.

Native user creation still responds with 204 and no created ID. The form does not invent an ID lookup: dirty custom fields can therefore target the native undefined identity, and a failed last-field callback can leave creation busy without reaching picture upload or navigation. Dirty fields start independently and the last DOM field's successful callback gates the next stage. Picture deletion starts independently before an edit PUT; selecting a replacement changes the source's misspelled deletion flag, so it does not clear the actual removal flag. Submission fixes the generated filename, while upload reads the current file selection later. These partial-write, retry and picture-selection quirks remain source behavior rather than recovered atomic saves.

The native key list exposes full key rows to the authenticated owner, or all keys to Admin, with sanitized user profiles for labels. Creation is available to any authenticated user and always creates that user's `default` key: 50 alphanumeric characters with expiry `2999-12-31 23:59:59`. The context and creation reader preserve PHP query registration, duplicate parameters and integer-filter behavior; a valid selected key retains its raw query spelling, zero/invalid selections become `-1`, and an array description raises the native typed argument error without inserting a key. The frontend provides native creation, selection, QR display and confirmed deletion controls. Actual route activation creates one key; preloading does not create one. The QR symbol uses the existing ZXing encoder and carries the absolute API URL plus the secret, or the native calendar-ical URL for that key type. Independent decoding verifies both payloads; SVG rendering replaces the source PNG renderer.

The Python compatibility API now follows the pinned generic controller's key grants. `DELETE /api/objects/api_keys/{id}` requires authentication but has no ownership or current-key restriction; `ExposedEntityEditRequiresAdmin` is empty in the pinned specification. Native POST/PUT first require `MASTER_DATA_EDIT`, then reject API keys through the existing NoEdit guard. Generic reads remain rejected by NoListing. The modern `/api/v2/api-keys` endpoints retain their Admin requirement and current-request-key deletion guard. Deleting the sole usable key makes subsequent authentication fail; an independent valid native session can still authenticate through the existing fallback. A successful native deletion refreshes the current-user query before returning to the full key page. If the sole credential was deleted, the application shows sign-in. Dispatched creation/deletion requests invalidate the native and modern key caches when they settle, including failures or responses after departure; retired callbacks cannot navigate a replacement page. User writes similarly invalidate list/editor/profile and task/assignee contexts. Permission drafts survive background context refresh, and permission saves use the canonical ID from the selected record.

Sources are the pinned `UsersController`, `OpenApiController`, `GenericEntityApiController`, `ApiKeyService`, `UserfieldsService`, user/permission/key templates and scripts, and the specification's NoEdit/NoListing/admin enums. The production additions are `backend/src/grocy/api/v2/users_access.py` and `domains/users/presentation.py`, with the narrow compatibility grant correction in `core/auth.py`; `tests/integration/test_users_access_context.py` owns the new API proof.

The backend increment has 11 distinct selected passing cases: five users/access owner cases plus six existing permission, tracking and access siblings across `/tmp/grocy-native-key-grants-owner.log` and `/tmp/grocy-native-key-grants-access.log`. Expected failures were captured for the missing contexts, typed-path rejection of scientific IDs, and the incorrect Admin-only native deletion grant. The cases cover field/value ordering, safe profiles, inherited permission rows, self/create/read grants, authentication flags, own/all key visibility, a usable native-created key, raw query errors, native deletion of own/other/current keys, NoEdit/NoListing, unchanged modern restrictions and valid session fallback. Scoped Ruff, formatting and Ty checks passed. The access siblings used the temporary `nix shell nixpkgs#file` dependency; no runtime dependency or user profile installation changed.

At the users/access checkpoint, all 1,180 frontend tests passed in 94 files. The primary access owners comprise 32 user list/editor cases, five permission cases and 12 key cases; their focused sibling runs overlap the full total and must not be added to it. They cover persisted tables, safe interactive custom groups, native password/picture/custom-field staging, imported and raw selected IDs, read/write denial, stale contexts, overlapping saves, permission drafts, QR decoding, creation preloads, settled-write caches and sole-credential re-authentication. Recipe and user tables now share the existing native custom-field projections and rendered group controls. Frontend lint, formatting, application/node/test TypeScript checks, the production build, full backend Ruff/format/Ty checks and diff whitespace checks passed. Expected regression failures were captured for imported/hexadecimal identities, stale custom edits, mixed table snapshots and missing cache invalidation. The preceding 1,251-pass/1,137-skip backend run predates this increment; the selected backend cases above verify these changes. No new complete backend run, live PHP differential, real-browser/device or production-image result is claimed here. Remaining comparisons include further raw selected/query and malformed custom-field cases, callback overlaps, configured paths, translations, frames/pictures, keyboard behavior and the release gates below.

### Native user, stock and shopping preferences

`/usersettings`, `/stocksettings` and `/shoppinglistsettings` now provide their native controls; `/settings` remains an additional consolidated editor. Each valid Change independently writes one compatibility setting. Checkboxes send booleans; other controls retain their string values. The shared helper uses JavaScript loose equality, assigns the in-memory value before dispatch, and permits overlapping requests. Write failures log without a notice, rollback or automatic retry. The failed value remains the last seen value, so choosing that same value again does not retry it. These pages have no Save or Reset button. Their controls latch a fresh server snapshot and retain the current draft through background reads; dispatched writes mark affected caches stale when they settle, including after departure.

User settings provides Language with a blank Default option. The first raw `returnto` parameter controls OK; bare/empty values fall back to `/`, and array-style names are ignored. Return links stay within the configured application path. Stock and shopping OK links always return to `/stockoverview` and `/shoppinglist`. Router query provenance now preserves duplicate parameters, bare flags and encoded spaces for native readers, including after ordinary search-object spreading; deliberately changed structured parameters are encoded normally. Language changes dispatch an autosave immediately, while the current catalog remains fixed through background configuration/catalog reads until navigation. Leaving refreshes configuration and settings. An initial localization failure exposes Retry rather than leaving the page loading indefinitely; errors after a valid catalog was retained do not replace it.

Stock settings includes product presets, overview flags, purchase/consume defaults, quantity/price precision and automatic price separators. Location, opened-product, label-printer and price controls follow their configuration flags. Due-soon days must be at least one, precision is integral from zero through ten, default due days permit `-1`, and default amounts use the initial saved quantity precision. Quick consume disables its alternative default-amount control while retaining that value. Currency inputs apply the pinned separator rule on blur: digits-only `1234` becomes `12.34` at precision two, while explicit separators remain unchanged. The source's precision-zero slicing defect is retained: `123` becomes `123.123`.

Shopping settings provides the month-calendar, round-up, automatic below-minimum addition, target-list and automatic stock-booking controls. Initial target-list disabling follows PHP truthiness, while checkbox initialization and later clicks follow JavaScript `BoolVal`; stored strings such as `false` therefore retain their different effects. Unmatched raw selected IDs remain blank rather than silently selecting the first list. The authenticated choice contexts at `/api/v2/settings/stock-context` and `/api/v2/settings/shopping-context` return active stock catalogs and all shopping lists in SQLite `name COLLATE NOCASE` order. They preserve stored ID/name pairs without invented defaults and permit native authenticated readers without stock/shopping grants, including disabled-feature configurations. Existing compatibility setting writes and defaults remain authoritative.

The shopping page now displays the inline month calendar with week numbers and Today. Rounding uses `ceil` on the stock-unit amount before converting to the displayed unit. The converted display amount controls plural text; the native purchase URL carries the rounded stock-unit amount. It does not persist a rounded shopping amount or change the modern atomic booking amount. Additive `stock_amount` and `display_factor` metadata travel through the backend shopping/API and MCP contracts; frontend consumers accept their absence for older payloads. Per-row native stock booking uses a persistent purchase iframe, retains it when hidden, and follows the source's `AfterItemAdded` deletion and `Ready` completion stages. Per-row review fixes retain completion after Close, preserve retry after failed row removal, retire the dialog when its list is replaced, and invalidate committed stock changes independently of the later shopping-row deletion. Matching completion IDs accept native numeric or string payloads.

The native all-items workflow now captures product rows in their current DOM order, including done rows and excluding notes. Its captured sequence and one-based progress remain fixed while the user books or skips items. Purchase frames stay mounted when hidden; Ready messages advance the sequence, and Skip posts Ready to the host itself. Shopping-row deletion runs independently. The source's stale captured-row stall and trailing Ready → CloseLastModal reset behavior are retained. This workflow is separate from the additional modern atomic booking action.

Native purchase custom-field metadata writes now start independently rather than waiting for earlier fields. Completion of the last dirty field, including its file work, controls the next booking stage. A failed last metadata PUT remains silent and busy; subsequent file-operation failures use the generic error. Late acknowledgements do not replace a newer draft, and each booking builds fresh upload metadata. Departure aborts remaining owner work; settled stock-booking requests still mark possibly changed caches stale. File Clear and other native dirty-state quirks are not claimed corrected. Adjacent differences remain: the per-unit transaction GET is awaited here but independent in the source, whose failure logs to the console. Native printer-webhook failure shows one generic error (`grocy.js:466–495`); the current `printLabel` catch uses a toast. Printer failures must be compared by stage rather than all being classified as console-only.

### Native general calendar

`/calendar` now hosts the pinned FullCalendar 3.10.5 runtime in an isolated frame, with its licensed jQuery, Moment/locales, Bootstrap and Font Awesome assets. The additional Agenda/Export page remains at `/calendar-agenda`. Native month, agenda-week, agenda-day and list-week views, previous/Today/next navigation, week numbers, first-day configuration and `HH:mm` times use the source runtime. Date/view query parameters do not select the native view or rewrite its URL. Initial view selection and the first outer-window resize use the source's 768-pixel boundary; later resizes do not repeat that view change. Event dates preserve native offset wall times, explicit/inferred all-day behavior and invalid-event handling rather than the modern agenda's date conversion. Event clicks use their native destination links and per-user colors.

The authenticated `/api/v2/calendar/context` supplies events, user settings, configuration and locale without requiring a calendar/domain grant. The Calendar flag controls navigation visibility, while authenticated direct access remains available. Existing event and iCal wire contracts are unchanged. Color configuration exposes the five source feature-dependent inputs, including browser coercion of blank or invalid stored colors. Each Change independently autosaves through the shared preference helper. OK, Escape, backdrop and native close dismissals reload the unfiltered calendar without awaiting writes; dispatched writes still mark affected caches stale when they settle. Sharing fetches a fresh compatibility sharing link for every activation, displays its selectable URL and a real QR, and permits overlapping responses. Its static-backdrop alert focuses OK, has no close icon and closes without reloading the calendar. Sharing failures log without a notice; departed owners cannot open a late alert or reset a replacement page.

The isolated runtime uses the source's normal FullCalendar stylesheet for print as well as screen; no additional FullCalendar print stylesheet is enabled. The wrapper supplies host theme tokens and licensed solid icons. Runtime DOM checks verify theme-token updates, preserved user event colors, cleanup and the native selectors that hide five toolbar buttons for printing. Physical screen/print rendering remains unverified. Runtime and action tests are DOM cases, not a real-browser rendering or physical-device result.

The action owner passed ten cases plus 28 settings/API-key siblings (`/tmp/grocy-calendar-actions-final.log`, 38). They cover color flags/coercion, independent held writes, all dismissal paths, console-only failures, fresh and overlapping sharing responses, independent decoding of the rendered QR, initial OK focus, departure and settled-cache invalidation. Expected failures were captured for retained sharing alerts after native close and missing primary-button focus. The final renderer run passed seven actual-runtime cases and two retained agenda cases; combined with the ten action owners, all 19 cases passed (`/tmp/grocy-native-calendar-final.log`). This includes localization, theme changes and disposal, first outer resize, all four views, raw offset/all-day handling, ignored view/date queries, fresh color-dismissal reset under a configured prefix, and context/resource retry. Three backend context owners plus six siblings passed with one optional PHP comparison skipped (`/tmp/grocy-calendar-context-final.log`, nine passed). No live PHP differential result is claimed.

The development proxy now preserves the incoming Host (`changeOrigin: false`). A causal HTTP check first produced an event destination at the backend host `http://127.0.0.1:18851/tasks` instead of the web host on port 18852, then verified the corrected destination (`/tmp/root-calendar-host-before.log`, `/tmp/root-calendar-host-after.log`). The proxy setting was checked against the [Vite 8.3.5 proxy documentation](https://github.com/vitejs/vite/blob/v8.3.5/docs/config/server-options.md#serverproxy) and its installed implementation. The root Vite delivery run passed 100 checks (`/tmp/root-calendar-root-vite-resources.log`), and the final delivery run passed 101 resource/form responses each through Vite and nginx under `/house.hold/` (`/tmp/root-native-calendar-final-resources.log`). These cover the actual runtime scripts, styles and fonts, native/retained SPA routes, context access, manifests, auth redirects and HEAD behavior. All 1,281 frontend tests passed across 105 files (`/tmp/root-native-calendar-full-frontend.log`); frontend lint, formatting, application/node/test TypeScript checks, the prefixed production build, full backend Ruff/format/Ty checks, pinned resource hashes and diff whitespace checks passed. Temporary verification servers were stopped after the checks. ### Native Products master list

`/products` now provides the native master list; the preceding modern catalog remains at `/catalog`, with its existing selected modern `/products/{id}` editor. The native table preserves all ten base column indices plus configured custom columns, including feature-hidden columns and the initially hidden store, Grocycode and picture columns. Saved visibility, reordering, grouping and numeric minimum-stock sorting use the native table settings. Name opens Product overview; Add, Edit and Copy link to their selected native forms. Authenticated media readers fetch the source's 25-pixel Grocycodes, 64-pixel product pictures and 32-pixel custom images, and retire object URLs when their page is replaced.

The authenticated `/api/v2/products/context` applies PHP query-presence rules for disabled products and the in-stock/out-of-stock aggregate filters. It retains inactive referenced units, locations and stores, native catalog order, and custom-value row presence, including explicit nulls. The frontend preserves the source's initially All status selector, Clear and raw product-group quirks, and unmatched selections with `selectedIndex = -1`. Each true page reload takes a fresh controller snapshot; a held settings request from the preceding page cannot block the table or overwrite the context's authoritative settings. Table Reset preserves the source query scope, while successful Delete or Merge returns to bare `/products`, including when only a hash previously differed.

Delete confirms permanent removal of stock, journal and references, sends an empty-object DELETE rather than deactivating the product, and closes the confirmation before its response. Its failure logs without a notice. Merge offers the controller-filtered product choices, including self and any included inactive product, and keeps both required selections on failure. Requests remain independent and unlocked; merge failures use the native generic error with inert technical details. Cancel does not revoke a dispatched write. A page-level action scope retains callbacks when filtering removes a row, while departure or page replacement aborts later UI work. Settled requests still invalidate possibly changed product, stock and recipe caches. No frontend permission rule hides these native controls; existing backend mutation grants remain unchanged.

The native Products link and exact `/products` page remain available with Stock disabled, matching the unconditional Products navigation entry before the source's stock-only links. The additional `/catalog` and modern selected product editor retain their existing feature gates. The retained modern owner continues to refresh barcode/conversion messages without saving or discarding its draft. Two unchanged owner assertions initially failed because the host still matched the former modern `/products/` route; correcting that match to `/catalog` restored all 35 selected action/related/card cases (`/tmp/root-native-products-retained-host.log`).

The final focused frontend run passed 24 table owners, nine action owners and 28 retained related/preset/Grocycode siblings: 61 cases (`/tmp/grocy-native-products-final.log`). Baselines demonstrated the missing native table and the table remaining absent behind a held older settings read; the repaired boundary cancels that older read and seeds the controller settings. The action owner does not claim a separate preimplementation failure for each lifecycle case. Three backend context owners plus seven siblings passed (`/tmp/grocy-products-master-final.log`, ten). These cover the raw query, aggregate, reference/custom-value and authentication boundaries; no new PHP differential result is claimed. All 1,314 frontend tests passed across 107 files (`/tmp/root-native-products-full-frontend-final.log`). Frontend lint, formatting, application/node/test TypeScript checks, the production build under `/house.hold/`, full backend Ruff/format/Ty checks and diff whitespace checks passed. The first broad run exposed a product-group navigation fixture still supplying the former catalog shape and expecting product-name links; it now supplies the native context and checks native name buttons while retaining all active/group/Clear/bookmark assertions. The related table/action/reference run passed 72 cases before the complete rerun (`/tmp/root-native-products-crosspage-siblings.log`). Final delivery checks passed 116 resource/form responses each through Vite and nginx under the configured prefix, including the Products context, native/retained routes and 25px Grocycodes (`/tmp/root-native-products-final-resources.log`). Temporary verification servers were stopped after the checks. The preceding 1,281-test/105-file result remains the historical general-calendar checkpoint. No new complete backend run, real-browser rendering or printing, physical-device check, or release-image verification is claimed; remaining implementation and verification gaps are listed below.

### Native page access, navigation and entry routing

Native page controllers generally authenticate independently of feature flags and module grants. The pinned `routes.php`, `BaseController::renderPage` and `AuthMiddleware` establish this boundary; Users controllers retain their explicit read/create/edit checks. The frontend now identifies exact native route shapes before applying the additional modern workflows' feature gates. Page controls and navigation still use their individual source feature conditions. Native meal-plan context loading no longer requires Recipes and Mealplan to be enabled together.

Presentation readers for household tracking/journals and selected forms, stock entries/journals/forms, product form choices and descriptions, Grocycodes/paper labels and shopping contexts now use authentication without extra domain grants. Rich-editor and print-draft POST requests only render supplied content. Dataset, stock, file, user and physical-printer writes keep their existing permissions. A no-grant reader with the related features disabled can read saved data; anonymous requests and neighboring writes remain denied. Recipe Grocycodes already had this boundary and are covered without changing their handler.

The entry route now follows `SystemController::GetEntryPageRelative`: its nine recognized configured choices use native destinations, an omitted value defaults to Stock overview, and an unknown or disabled choice goes to About. Mealplan's entry decision checks its own flag, independently of Recipes. Menu visibility is separate from direct access; stock and shopping navigation use `/stockoverview` and `/shoppinglist`. Source permission classes leave denied links visible with disabled pointer styling. Their native destinations remain authenticated pages. Additional command-palette actions keep their own permission filtering.

`/shoppinglist` now renders its native page instead of redirecting to the additional `/shopping` workflow. Its raw `list` query uses PHP query registration and SQLite/PDO selection, independently of the Multiple lists flag. Numeric aliases and single positional array bindings retain their source row scope; unknown scalar selectors can produce an empty page rather than a modern resource 404. Displayed rows and initial Notes use the server selector. Hidden list controls can still target list one for writes, as in the source. The modern integer shopping resources retain their existing not-found rules.

The authenticated native context and GET/POST `/api/v2/shopping-lists/native-print` share that raw selection. Paper output retains displayed rows and optional list metadata, including the single-array case with rows but no matching metadata. Thermal printing continues to use the DOM control's list ID. A live Notes draft can be rendered without persisting either list. Embedded item links retain their raw server selector and return to the native shopping page or send the native parent messages; departed editors cannot navigate or message a replacement page. Clear reloads the same raw URL, while Add missing and Add due navigate to the DOM-selected list. Native list-change callbacks permit imported zero and negative IDs; the additional modern page retains its own restrictions. A pinned LessQL/PDO/helper probe compared 31 query cases and exposed the malformed `list[::1]` binding, which now returns the source error instead of selecting a list.

Two further source differences were corrected alongside this increment. Browser label transaction reads now run independently of purchase custom-field saves and receipts; late read failure is logged, and webhook failures expose native generic technical details without repeating the stock booking. Native product/user custom columns use PHP numeric whitespace and first-match rules, including numeric field-name aliases. Non-breaking spaces, BOM characters and hexadecimal-looking IDs do not alias another record. A pinned-helper/PDO probe confirms these identity and ordering rules (`/tmp/root-native-value-identity-reference.log`); its product/user owner and sibling run passed 57 cases, while the purchase/shopping run passed 53 cases. These totals overlap later integrated runs. Selected user-form hydration was subsequently compared and repaired separately; its exact SQL object-ID contract is recorded below.

The integrated frontend run passed all 1,349 tests in 108 files (`/tmp/root-native-access-full-frontend.log`, 125.81 seconds). Frontend lint, formatting, application/node/test TypeScript checks and the production build under `/house.hold/` passed. The route/entry owner and auth/settings siblings passed 257 cases in 17 files; native page/shopping owners and siblings passed 92 cases in eight files. These selected totals overlap the integrated run. Expected failures were captured for the former Tasks feature gate, meal-plan query suppression, purchase label blocking and product custom-value alias selection. This checkpoint still had ordinary native navigation and warm-settings freshness gaps. The subsequent document-lifetime increment below implements fresh top-level page preparation; nested child-document isolation remains separate work.

The complete Python suite passed 1,281 tests, with 1,137 skipped and three warnings, using `GROCY_USE_NATIVE_PHP=false` (`/tmp/root-native-access-full-backend.log`, 1,247.70 seconds). That run finished before the final native-print addition and exact PDO-name correction. Their three new regressions first failed on the missing print route and incorrect malformed binding; after the patch, all 29 native-access, shopping-list, purchase and paper owner/sibling cases passed (`/tmp/root-native-print-final-backend.log`). Backend Ruff, formatting and Ty passed on the final source. Final HTTP checks passed 127 resource/form responses each through Vite and nginx under `/house.hold/`, including raw native shopping contexts, print documents and anonymous failures (`/tmp/root-native-access-final-resources.log`). All temporary verification servers were stopped and their ports checked closed. These results do not establish interactive browser printing/rendering, physical devices, the complete live PHP differential suite or the release image.

### Native form authentication and document lifetime

Native `POST /login` now accepts URL-encoded and multipart forms independently of the modern JSON key-login endpoint. The form retains raw usernames, valid empty passwords, the `stay_logged_in=on` checkbox, native OK control and first-raw `invalid=true` error selection. Public login presentation exposes only the canonical login/logout URLs. Its locale ignores saved account preferences even when the browser has a valid session or connection key, matching the source named-login branch. Native form submission selects cookie authentication so a previously saved connection key cannot replace the newly authenticated user. Additional key-based sign-in and sign-out remain at `/signin` and `/signout`.

The Python login service reproduces the checked PHP form registration, duplicate/bracket field, NUL, nesting and default pair-count behavior; exact credentials; default/LDAP dispatch; invalid-login and success redirects; session-only issuance; 30-day versus permanent session expiry; the source cookie attributes and 2038 expiry; and imported password verification/rehash behavior. Session creation remains committed before a possible rehash error. Native `GET`/`HEAD /logout` authenticates normally, removes the raw cookie-selected session, retains API keys, sends no cookie-clearing header and redirects to the canonical root. Unsupported native providers keep the source Not implemented error. The modern JSON account/key API retains its independent contracts. Arbitrary reference PHP.ini body/file/part limits remain runtime configuration boundaries.

Source login probes are in `/tmp/grocy-login-source-probe.php` and `/tmp/grocy-login-source-results.jsonl`; they use disposable credentials. The initial missing implementation produced 29 failures, and the codec refinement produced four additional causal failures. The native/modern access, users, files and actual LDAP/TLS run passed 68 cases (`/tmp/grocy-native-login-final.log`). The complete backend run then passed 1,330 tests, with 1,129 skipped and four warnings, using Python without native PHP and with an actual OpenLDAP server (`/tmp/root-native-auth-full-backend.log`, 1,282.41 seconds). After that run, the seven public-context/anonymous-locale cases first failed and then passed as part of a 67-case final owner/sibling run (`/tmp/root-native-login-context-before.log`, `/tmp/root-native-auth-final-backend-focused.log`). These counts overlap; the full run preceded the final public-context and script-prefix patches.

Rewrite-disabled deployments follow the pinned configuration rule: include `index.php` in `GROCY_BASE_PATH` and the frontend `VITE_BASE_PATH`, while `GROCY_BASE_URL` identifies the installation root and `GROCY_DISABLE_URL_REWRITING` controls canonical URL generation. For example, use `/house.hold/index.php`, `/house.hold/index.php/` and `/house.hold` respectively. The middleware preserves the configured script mount, raw encoded resource suffix and modern JSON boundaries. It also accepts one leading script segment under a base-only mount when rewriting is disabled; embedded object names and a second script segment are not stripped. Frontend matching preserves the physical raw URL, query and manifest while generated page links follow its configured base. Matching an alias does not establish cookie authentication with a misconfigured frontend API base: the native cookie deliberately has no explicit Path, so its browser default must cover the configured API requests. Canonical login redirects retain the server's exact visible script URL. Vite and nginx route native POST/OPTIONS login, GET/HEAD logout, API calls, files and native resources under these prefixes while login GET remains the SPA.

The source Slim/SAPI probe is `/tmp/grocy-script-uri-probe.jsonl`. The frontend routing baseline produced three expected misses with six controls passing; the final route/query/metadata/auth/settings run passed 55 cases (`/tmp/grocy-index-php-routing-causal-before.log`, `/tmp/grocy-index-php-routing-final.log`). The backend routing regressions first produced four failures with the source-valid configured-script case passing. After the shared mount/CORS normalization, all 82 routing, native-auth, public-context, access, files, stream, system and paper cases passed in one final run, with one optional live PHP comparison skipped (`/tmp/grocy-front-controller-before.log`, `/tmp/grocy-front-controller-final-green.log`, 89.99 seconds). Actual LDAP/TLS ran in this scope. The tests cover configured and optional script mounts, encoded filenames, generated document links, literal/Unicode prefixes, HEAD/OPTIONS, native HTML/auth redirects and v2 JSON/no-store boundaries. A further protected-page redirect case exposed client URL generation shortening a canonical script URL (`/tmp/root-native-auth-canonical-redirect-before.log`); the redirect now enters the server-owned URL directly, and its 32-case auth/routing run passes. These are DOM/runtime and transport checks, not real-browser cookie or rendering verification.

Native top-level navigation now awaits fresh identity, grants, configuration, user settings and localization before mounting the destination. Domain queries from a retired document are canceled and removed so a new page reads current rows instead of inheriting a warm page snapshot. Products and Calendar use their authoritative full presentation contexts without duplicate context reads. The catalog stays fixed during partial URI changes and changes only when a new document is prepared. Source full-page reload actions now use an owner-scoped replacement capability, including table reset, relevant journal/entry filters and recovery, task show-done/undo, chore rescheduling, recipe selection/gallery/copy/delete and selected product Save-and-continue. A late callback cannot replace a newer document, and a child table reset cannot replace its parent editor or discard its draft. A failed full journal reload shows the failure without old rows.

The document freshness regressions first produced four failures (`/tmp/grocy-native-freshness-before.log`). The final core run passed 78 cases, including the unchanged Products and Calendar held-context assertions (`/tmp/grocy-native-freshness-owned-final.log`); callback and partial-navigation runs passed 36 and six cases respectively. Native meal-plan consumption still commits before a done-write failure; retry consumes again as in the source. A successful source full-page replacement does not retain the retired document's Undo toast. Existing genuine in-page undo coverage remains intact. Final integrated test fixtures provide the required permissions/settings/localization DTOs, reacquire replaced page elements and accept retired cache entries being absent or stale while retaining the navigation, mutation order, selection and late-callback assertions.

Selected User editor custom values now use the generic API's exact SQL TEXT object-ID selection after resolving the canonical user DTO ID, then the first PHP-loose field-name match. The context is a field-name map with explicit null for missing values. Overview custom columns retain their separate raw-row/loose-ID behavior. Alias, ordering, missing/default and first-match regressions failed before the repair; the final API and UI runs passed seven and 38 cases (`/tmp/grocy-user-selected-fields-api-final.log`, `/tmp/grocy-user-selected-fields-ui-final.log`).

The final integrated frontend run passed all 1,375 tests in 109 files (`/tmp/root-native-auth-full-frontend-final.log`, 129.68 seconds). Frontend lint, formatting, application/node/test TypeScript checks and both production builds (`/house.hold/` and `/house.hold/index.php/`) passed. Backend Ruff, formatting and Ty passed on the final source. Actual URL-encoded and multipart login, native cookie-only identity, OPTIONS and GET/HEAD logout passed through both Vite and nginx in both deployment configurations (`/tmp/root-native-auth-transport-final.log`, `/tmp/root-native-auth-transport-script-final.log`). Resource/context checks passed 129 responses per proxy per configuration (`/tmp/root-native-auth-resources-final.log`, `/tmp/root-native-auth-resources-script-final.log`). All temporary API, Vite and nginx listeners were stopped and their three ports checked closed. This checkpoint left Stock entries and Stock journal child-document isolation unfinished. The following increment addresses those outer frames. The native resolved-conversions child already uses an iframe; its additional modern catalog variant remains a separate shared-context case.
