### Native stock child documents and completion lifetimes

Product editor and Product overview Stock entries/Stock journal actions now open independent native documents at `?embedded&product={id}`, with the source `table` dialog type. Each child boots its own identity, grants, configuration, settings, query cache and catalog. The parent's unsaved product draft remains in its own document. The source product links are not hidden by the Stock feature flag; disabled anchors retain their href and keyboard behavior while the existing pointer-disabled styling applies. Source references are `views/productform.blade.php:27–34`, `views/components/productcard.blade.php:38–48` and `public/viewjs/components/productcard.js:78–81`.

Frames opened from a child are peers hosted by the highest ancestor marked `IsGrocy`, matching `public/js/grocy.js:789–845`. An unmarked external page containing Grocy does not become that host. Purchase, Consume, Inventory and Transfer retain their direct-parent completion messages, but their existing stock broadcasts now use this same topmost-Grocy boundary. Broadcast fanout remains recursive. No active-frame sender allowlist was introduced: the source accepts already queued messages without a modal-generation filter.

Native frame dialogs now portal to the document body, matching Bootbox's append order. This corrects CloseLastModal selecting an older parent dialog behind a newly opened frame. Closing a peer restores the previously focused control inside the earlier same-origin frame, rather than only focusing the iframe element. Embedded documents expose the native Close control and parent Escape message. The first raw `embedded` value determines whether the Close button exists; explicitly empty, duplicate, encoded and malformed values retain their distinct parsing rules. A local React dialog owns its Escape cancellation before the outer document can close.

Stock Entries now performs the booking POST, reads fresh product details for its receipt, dispatches the clicked-row refresh independently, and emits ProductChanged to the native host. Open disables the clicked action after the details callback. Undo broadcasts the affected product and shows its receipt. The Entries receiver refreshes existing matching rows, including native full reload when a hidden or missing row requires recovery. Stock overview remains the other source receiver; ProductCard has no broadcast listener. Row actions use the native cursor-only busy state rather than an added page-wide pending lock. Source references are `stockentries.js:75–151,315–342` and `stockoverview.js:411–420`.

Entries booking, Undo and row-refresh continuations now belong to the originating document. Departure aborts reads and prevents later detail stages, row updates, focus changes and notices. Dispatched writes mark affected caches stale even when their outcome is uncertain, without refetching or altering a replacement page. The shared entry-refresh reader accepts the document signal and checks it before secondary reads and display callbacks. Journal Undo has the same ownership fence and supports concurrent rows and native completion order. It deliberately sends no ProductChanged message and does not refetch parent tables: `stockjournal.js:109–136` updates the clicked/correlated rows and shows its own receipt only.

The Entries owner first produced six expected failures for the old shared dialog, stale-detail receipts, missing broadcasts and continued work after departure (`/tmp/grocy-stockentries-native-before.log`). Its final owner/sibling run passed 93 cases in five files, including 31 Entries cases (`/tmp/grocy-stockentries-native-final.log`). Journal's two departure cases and its pending-row lock regression failed before repair; 22 Journal and 12 Product card cases then passed (`/tmp/grocy-journal-lifecycle-before.log`, `/tmp/grocy-journal-overlap-before.log`, `/tmp/grocy-journal-siblings-final.log`). The topmost booking regression first failed against an unmarked external host and then passed in the 91-case Purchase/Consume/Inventory/Transfer run (`/tmp/grocy-booking-topmost-before.log`, `/tmp/grocy-booking-topmost-after.log`). These selected totals overlap the integrated run.

Host ordering, embedded dismissal and deep focus each have causal regressions (`/tmp/root-native-stock-frame-stack-before.log`, `/tmp/root-native-stock-frame-dismiss-before.log`, `/tmp/root-native-stock-deep-focus-before.log`). The final focus/modal/card sibling run passed 43 cases. A separate scratch copy without the Strict Mode once guard produced two failures for duplicated peer-frame commands; the live production files were not toggled (`/tmp/root-native-stock-dialog-duplicate-counterfactual.log`). The real shared-dialog owner passes both frame types and the configured visible-script prefix.

Real Chromium checks used an isolated named agent-browser session, a disposable database/account and the production nginx build under `/house.hold/`, with native PHP disabled. Native form login authenticated the fixture user. While an English product form retained its unsaved name, changing the saved locale and amount precision produced a German Entries child displaying `2.3 Pieces`. Changing settings again and confirming the child's table Reset reloaded only that child to English and `2.346 Pieces`; its raw URL and the parent draft stayed unchanged. Escape closed the local table-options dialog without closing the frame. A Journal opened from the child's Product card became a peer at the highest Grocy host; closing it retained the earlier frame/card and restored the exact Stock journal link inside the earlier document.

The same production app was also loaded inside an unmarked same-origin host fixture. Native frame commands and actual stock Open/Undo broadcasts remained inside Grocy; the external host received none. The stock entry returned to closed state after Undo. Successive Escape actions closed the current card and frame, restored the product form's Stock entries link and retained the unsaved draft. The saved product name stayed unchanged. The browser reported no application errors. Desktop at 1440×900 and a phone viewport at 390×844 were inspected; both kept document width within the viewport, and the wide table scrolled within the child. Captures are `/tmp/grocy-native-stock-child-desktop.png` and `/tmp/grocy-native-stock-child-mobile.png`. These checks cover Chromium and viewport emulation, not physical mobile hardware or cross-browser equivalence.

The final integrated frontend run passed all 1,396 tests in 110 files (`/tmp/root-native-stock-child-full-frontend.log`, 125.55 seconds). Full lint, formatting, application/node/test TypeScript checks and the `/house.hold/` production build passed. No backend production changes were made in this increment; the preceding backend full/focused results remain the applicable checkpoints. The browser session, API and nginx servers were closed, their ports checked clear, and the generated external-host HTML fixture removed from the build output.

At this outer-frame checkpoint, recursive row Edit/Transfer and the selected stock-entry save protocol were unfinished. The following increment implements those boundaries; the preceding browser and 1,396-test results remain historical evidence for the outer-frame checkpoint. They do not verify the selected form added below.

### Native selected stock entry and recursive booking frames

The native `/stockentry/{id}` route now uses a separate selected form, while StockLedger retains its additional working Undo and partial-retry workflow. Its authenticated `/api/v2/stock/entries/native-editor?row=RAW` reader binds the raw selected row identity without a positive-integer conversion; the existing typed modern endpoint remains unchanged. SQLite aliases such as `02`, `2e0` and `2.0`, imported zero/negative IDs, NUL suffixes and ASCII numeric whitespace follow the checked LessQL/PDO behavior. Hexadecimal, binary, bracket-like and unknown identities do not select another row; Unicode numeric whitespace is not trimmed. The native adapter returns a controlled 404 for an unmatched row instead of rendering the reference's unusable null-row form. Custom values initially belong to the selected row's `stock_id`, not its numeric row ID. Direct reads need authentication, without a Stock feature or edit grant; booking writes retain their existing authorization.

The route reproduces Slim's two decoding stages: `%2531` selects row `1`, while `%252531` leaves the controller argument `%31`. A slash decoded before matching does not become a selected row argument. Pinned PHP probes cover those route boundaries and raw row selection (`/tmp/grocy-stockentry-route-probe.jsonl`, `/tmp/grocy-stockentry-row-probe.jsonl`). The form independently reads the product's current quantity-unit name after its initial context loads; a held or failed unit read does not prevent editing or saving and cannot update a departed document.

Native Save puts the canonical row ID, then starts an optional label-data GET independently of custom-field completion. The label GET uses the returned stock ID and runs even without a configured webhook; a later webhook uses the configured native method/body, while its error notification does not delay the form's completion. Dirty custom fields dispatch in parallel under the returned transaction ID; only the last dirty field's metadata/file callback gates the completion sequence. Success posts topmost-Grocy `BroadcastMessage(ProductChanged)`, followed by direct-parent `ShowSuccessMessage`, `Ready` and `CloseLastModal`, and leaves the form busy. Primary errors log and unlock the draft. A failed final metadata write silently leaves the committed form busy; file errors retain the native generic-error behavior. Dispatched writes invalidate affected caches after settlement, including responses after document departure, while retired continuations cannot emit receipts or begin later stages.

The source's undeclared global `price` is preserved semantically: a first empty visible price sends a nonnumeric object, which the compatibility API treats as null; after a failed numeric save, clearing Price and retrying retains the prior formatted global value. Disabling price tracking sends the formatted hidden zero. The reference input object's dynamic jQuery event metadata is not reproduced byte for byte. The receipt also preserves the source's broken `result.id` access on an array, producing `UndoStockBookingEntry('undefined', rowId)`. On Stock entries, repeated Undo attempts reach the invalid booking endpoint and log the failure; a host without that native function logs the missing-function error. Neither path invents a successful Undo or refresh. The additional modern editor keeps its functional booking Undo.

Entries row Edit and Transfer, and Journal row Transfer, now open independent default-form iframes through the same topmost-Grocy host. Entries Transfer retains the source's raw product/location/stock prefills; Journal Transfer supplies only the product. Parent table filters and drafts remain in their own documents. Embedded Purchase, Consume, Inventory and Transfer now have one shared Close control and emit one parent dismissal per Escape; a local dialog still owns its own Escape first. At this checkpoint the complete native More menus were still missing; the following menu increment implements those actions and their source differences.

Sources are Grocy 4.5.0 commit `21aa5eab218c8466f90729154cb293fffbe10c21`: `StockController::StockEntryEditForm`, `views/stockentryform.blade.php`, `public/viewjs/stockentryform.js`, the stock row templates, shared `UserfieldsForm`, `grocy.js` and Slim routing. Causal regressions cover the missing raw reader, old save/receipt behavior, route decoding, independent unit reads and duplicate embedded dismissal. The final focused backend run passed seven cases (`/tmp/root-native-stock-entry-backend-final.log`); the selected form/unit and booking siblings passed 84 cases, route siblings passed 34, and shared host siblings passed 43 (`/tmp/grocy-native-stock-entry-unit-final.log`, `/tmp/root-native-stock-entry-route-final.log`, `/tmp/root-native-stock-entry-shared-final.log`). These totals overlap and must not be added together. Full backend Ruff/format checks covered 280 files and Ty passed; frontend lint, formatting, all three TypeScript projects and the prefixed production build passed. The first broad run exposed an older stock-settings fixture still mocking the typed editor endpoint. After updating that fixture without weakening its assertions, the final integrated frontend run passed all 1,432 tests in 111 files (`/tmp/root-native-stock-entry-full-frontend-final.log`, 126.67 seconds).

Real Chromium verification used the production nginx build under `/house.hold/`, an authenticated synthetic user and a disposable database. Entries Edit opened a separate row-ID form; saving amount `4.567` and a changed note closed only that form and refreshed the parent row through actual HTTP writes. Clicking its native Undo produced the expected `/bookings/undefined/undo` 500, retained the receipt and left the committed amount unchanged. No uncaught browser runtime errors were reported. This expected failed source action is not a successful Undo.

Over an unsaved product-name draft, the Entries frame opened Transfer as a peer in the highest Grocy host, with the source product/location/stock-ID prefills. One Escape in the Transfer body closed that frame alone and restored focus to its original link inside Entries. Reopening it and booking `1.123` units to Fridge again closed only Transfer; an actual API read confirmed `3.444` in Pantry and `1.123` in Fridge, while the product draft survived. The real HTTP bookmark `/stockentry/%2531?embedded` loaded canonical row `1`, retained its raw URL and used that URL in its manifest. The selected form was visually inspected at 1440×900 and 390×844; the phone document width stayed 390 pixels. Captures are `/tmp/grocy-native-stock-entry-desktop.png` and `/tmp/grocy-native-stock-entry-mobile.png`. The task-owned browser, API and nginx were closed and their ports checked clear.

These checks cover Chromium with viewport emulation. No new full backend, physical-device, physical-printer, full differential or release-image result is claimed for this bounded increment. The More-menu gap recorded at this checkpoint is addressed below. Further raw/control/callback cases, translated rendering and remaining stock-family verification stay open.

### Native stock row More menus and label actions

Stock entries and Stock journal now expose their complete native More menus through a shared menu rendered in the document body. Shopping, Purchase, Consume, Transfer and Inventory open independent default-form documents; Entries supplies its raw location and stock ID to Consume/Transfer, while Journal supplies only the product. Recipe search retains the source's direct product-name concatenation. Product overview uses the existing card; Entries/Journal and Summary links open peer table documents. Edit product performs normal navigation with the query-free return path captured when the current document was created. Entries Summary deliberately sends `product`, which the summary controller ignores; Journal sends `product_id` and filters correctly.

Feature flags control Shopping, Transfer, recipe search and label printing independently. Entries retains the source's absence of permission classes on these menu actions. Journal's shopping, booking and Edit-product links remain visible with their action-specific disabled/not-allowed permission state; read actions remain available. Backend mutations still enforce their grants. The menu escapes the table's scrolling area, fits the available viewport and restores focus to More on dismissal. Chromium exposed a first-open keyboard focus bug; focus now enters a visible menu item after positioning. Escape dismisses that menu before the embedded document's own dismissal handler runs.

Entries label printing always GETs `/api/stock/entry/{rowId}/printlabel`; the misleading native `data-stock-id` contains the numeric row ID. Journal GETs `/api/stock/products/{productId}/printlabel`. Each request is independent and can overlap other actions, without a busy cursor, pending lock, success notice, row refresh or broadcast. Label-data GET failures are silent. A configured client webhook receives the native JSON or form body with extra parameters overriding matching data keys, and its failure shows the native generic WebHook error. Row filtering does not retire an already dispatched print; leaving the owning document prevents its late webhook or notice. Downloads use the native Grocycode resource URLs with `download=true`; Entries' paper label remains a normal new-window link to `/stockentry/{rowId}/label`.

Sources are the pinned Grocy 4.5.0 commit `21aa5eab218c8466f90729154cb293fffbe10c21`, `views/stockentries.blade.php:150–245`, `views/stockjournal.blade.php:181–250`, their label-print handlers, `GrocycodeTrait`, `grocy.js` and the layout's `CurrentUrlRelative`. The corrected causal baseline produced two missing-menu failures (`/tmp/grocy-native-stock-row-menu-before-corrected.log`). Sixteen menu owner cases and the 84-case sibling run passed (`/tmp/grocy-native-stock-row-menu-final.log`); after the browser keyboard repair the 16 owner cases passed again (`/tmp/root-native-stock-row-menu-keyboard-fix-owner.log`). These selected runs overlap other totals.

Initial real Chromium checks used the production nginx build under `/house.hold/`. Entries row `1` and Journal product `4` delivered their label payloads to a synthetic printer endpoint returning 204, without a busy cursor or success notice. Entries' actual Summary displayed both Greek Yogurt and Native frame coffee, while Journal's `product_id=4` Summary displayed only coffee. One Escape inside the Journal menu kept the frame open and restored More focus. At a 390-pixel phone viewport the document width stayed 390 pixels and the menu fit within it. These checks do not establish physical printing, physical-mobile behavior or cross-browser equivalence.

### Native selected Product save and query completion

The selected `/product/{id}` route now explicitly uses the native Product save protocol. The additional Catalog and Product-card generic editors retain their sequential custom-field/retry behavior at their actual production callers. The synthetic stock-units-per-purchase-unit control belongs to the additional generic editor; it is omitted from the native form, whose save does not insert conversion CRUD. Native Save writes the product first, then dispatches dirty custom fields in parallel. The last dirty field's metadata and file callback alone gates the picture upload and completion; an earlier field may still be pending or may fail after completion. Native file controls retain the established initial-file deletion and live-selection rules. A failed final metadata write silently leaves the committed form busy. Custom-file errors show the generic error without inventing a completion or retry path.

Picture metadata fixes the native RandomString/CleanFileName name at submission, while the later upload reads the currently selected file bytes. Picture controls remain available outside the busy fieldset. Existing-picture deletion starts independently before the edit PUT; its failure unlocks the controls and shows the generic error, while the held PUT can still succeed and read the latest custom edits. Picture upload failure shows the generic error and unlocks the form. Create-primary failure also shows the generic error; edit-primary failure logs and unlocks without that notice. The source create-retry defect is preserved: retry after a picture failure sends another POST, but fields and completion retain the first created ID. Dispatched stages invalidate caches when they settle, including uncertain outcomes after departure; retired owners cannot start later writes, invoke completion hooks or navigate a replacement page.

Raw first-match `closeAfterCreation` presence, including an empty or duplicate value, closes the window before `returnto`. A defined flow appends literal `&product-name=` to the source return target instead of repairing its separator or normalizing existing bare/duplicate parameters. The final callback reads the live Name control, including a name changed after an independent picture-deletion error unlocked a held primary save. Canonical `/house.hold/index.php/` completion has real-route coverage. Related barcode/conversion deletion runs the same native save stages and then reloads the entire unchanged URI; that persistent reload intent takes precedence over close and return queries. Generic Catalog relation failures retain their existing retry proof. Copy continues to initialize custom fields from new-record defaults rather than copying source custom values or picture files; its previous retry assertion now checks the source's committed primary and silently busy final-metadata failure.

Sources are the same pinned commit's `public/viewjs/productform.js`, shared `UserfieldsForm`, `RandomString`, `CleanFileName` and `GetUriParam`. The initial owner baseline produced four failures for serialized fields and ignored close presence (`/tmp/grocy-native-product-save-before.log`). The 16 native owner cases and 64-case owner/sibling run passed (`/tmp/grocy-native-product-save-owner-final.log`, `/tmp/grocy-native-product-save-final.log`). A further held-primary/deletion-error regression failed when completion used the submitted name; after the live-value repair, all 36 selected cases passed (`/tmp/root-native-product-live-name-before.log`, `/tmp/root-native-product-live-name-after.log`). These totals overlap and must not be added together. The fixture's custom-field map type was corrected and test TypeScript passed; it did not change runtime assertions or behavior.

The final integrated run, after the live-Name correction and removal of the generic-only unit-factor control, passed all 1,463 tests in 113 files (`/tmp/root-native-stock-menu-product-full-frontend-complete.log`, 132.86 seconds). Full frontend lint, formatting and application/node/test TypeScript checks passed (`/tmp/root-native-stock-menu-product-check-verified.log`), as did the production build under `/house.hold/` (`/tmp/root-native-stock-menu-product-build-verified.log`). The preceding 1,432-test result belongs to the earlier selected stock-entry increment. No backend production code changed in this increment; its preceding full/focused checkpoints remain historical evidence, rather than a new complete backend run.

Further real Chromium checks used a synthetic user with no mutation grants. Journal's denied shopping, booking and Edit-product actions remained visible with computed `pointer-events: none`, while read, resource and printer actions stayed enabled. Focusing denied Purchase and pressing Enter still opened its authenticated native child, preserving the source's keyboard behavior; no denied booking write was performed. DOM owner cases independently cover Entries' absence of these permission restrictions and backend mutations retain their existing authorization.

An actual native Product edit changed product `4` to `Native menu coffee verified`, saved two real custom-field values and uploaded the repository's 2,091-byte PNG fixture. Subsequent API reads confirmed the name, both values and byte-for-byte picture contents. Completion returned to `/house.hold/stockentries?product=4&product-name=Native%20menu%20coffee%20verified`, retaining the configured prefix exactly once. A script-opened `/product/new?closeAfterCreation=&returnto=%2Fproducts` popup then created product `5`, saved both custom values and closed itself instead of following `returnto`; API reads confirmed that creation. The final native create form also omitted the generic unit-factor control. These browser checks use actual HTTP persistence; held ordering and stage-specific failures remain owner-test evidence.

The final menus were visually inspected at 1440×900 and 390×844; captures are `/tmp/grocy-native-stock-menu-desktop.png` and `/tmp/grocy-native-stock-menu-mobile.png`. The phone document width stayed 390 pixels and all menu actions remained reachable. The browser reported no uncaught application runtime errors. The task-owned browser, API, nginx and synthetic printer receiver were closed; ports 18861–18864 were checked clear. Synthetic records and uploads remain only in the disposable `/tmp/grocy-native-stock-menu-browser.db` and its temporary data directory. These are Chromium/viewport and synthetic-printer checks, not physical-device or physical-printer evidence.

At this save/menu checkpoint, native Product initial reads, copy timing and reference restrictions still needed their own implementation and comparison. The following increment supplies that context and loading protocol. The preceding save/menu counts and browser results remain evidence for that earlier checkpoint.

### Native Product initial context, pickers and independent copy

The authenticated `/api/v2/products/native-editor?row=RAW` now supplies the native Product controller's selected full row, canonical custom values, ordered reference rows, picker restrictions, saved conversions, user settings, configuration and locale. Literal `new` selects create mode; other raw identities use SQLite's native comparison without a positive-integer conversion. Checked numeric aliases, imported zero/negative IDs and NUL suffixes retain their canonical product ID; unmatched input returns a controlled 404 rather than the source's unusable null-row form. Slim's two decoding stages apply to the actual path, while the raw URL stays intact and mutations use the loaded canonical ID. Custom values are read by that canonical ID rather than the raw alias.

Reference choices follow `StockController::ProductEditForm`: create locations use BINARY name order, edit locations use NOCASE, active parents exclude the selected product and child products, and any existing child can disable the parent selector. Stock units include inactive units and become restricted to saved conversions once any stock history exists, including undone history. Other unit pickers retain their active/connected restrictions. The shared unit catalog is the union needed by those source controls and related tables; ordered picker IDs retain the restrictions. Saved conversion rows keep native cache order and raw factors. Definitions include only Product fields in their native SQL order. The modern typed form-options endpoint retains its existing behavior.

Native document preparation now consumes this context before mounting the form and seeds identity/configuration/settings/localization from the fresh document read. Older generic catalog, field, reference or settings replies cannot gate or replace that initial native form. Copy remains a separate, independently held raw lookup: its presence suppresses new-product presets even if the lookup fails, and failure logs without hiding the usable form or inventing a Retry gate. A later successful reply updates only the source's copied controls, preserving the current Name and unrelated draft state. Checkbox copies only set source-true values; source-false values do not clear a checkbox the user already checked. Custom values and picture files remain new-record defaults rather than being copied from the source product.

Copied selects distinguish a real blank option from no matching option. Null/empty optional values select the blank option and serialize an empty string; unmatched values can leave `selectedIndex=-1` and omit the field, including an unmatched label type when that control exists. The source's unit-default bug is preserved: Stock-unit changes assign the same option index to empty Purchase/Consume/Price selectors rather than matching unit IDs, so asymmetric lists can select another unit or leave no selection. Typing into Name also repeats that unit-change fanout. Generic management retains its separate ID-based behavior.

Parent prefill follows the source's initial name lookup, raw ID query and stored-ID priority. A stored parent ID of zero is PHP-empty and therefore does not override the query; a nonempty stored ID does. Initial and user selection events own their parent-rule GETs. A late copy changes the parent picker without starting that select-change rule read. Older parent replies can still complete out of order within the same document, matching the source; failures log and retain the current control state. Document departure retires those reads and prevents later control changes. Initial query values remain fixed for form initialization, while completion reads the current return/flow query and live Name. Native `U` concatenation uses the absolute application base before interpreting the destination, preserving its source behavior instead of accidentally treating a relative concatenation as another origin. The shopping return fixtures retain their literal Name assertions through a valid internal Cancel target.

The Description bridge now accepts a trusted parent paste command and executes the actual Summernote `pasteHTML` operation in its existing document. A late copy retains typed rich text and the current insertion position instead of replacing the draft or remounting the editor. Runtime regressions first produced two failures and then passed with the retained editor cases (`/tmp/grocy-description-paste-before.log`, `/tmp/grocy-description-paste-after.log`, six cases). The actual pinned copy/select and LessQL/PDO probes inform these rules; source references are Grocy 4.5.0 commit `21aa5eab218c8466f90729154cb293fffbe10c21`, `StockController::ProductEditForm:194–231`, `productform.js`, the ProductPicker/combobox scripts and shared field/number helpers.

The missing reader baseline failed all five cases; the implemented backend context and modern form/master siblings passed nine (`/tmp/grocy-native-product-editor-before.log`, `/tmp/grocy-native-product-editor-after.log`). The frontend loading owner passed 29 cases and its seven-file combined run passed 137 (`/tmp/grocy-native-product-loading-owner-final.log`, `/tmp/grocy-native-product-loading-final.log`). The shopping/navigation fixture migration and native-save siblings passed 42 (`/tmp/root-native-product-navigation-siblings.log`). These runs overlap the integrated frontend run: all 1,494 tests passed in 115 files (`/tmp/root-native-product-context-full-final.log`, 133.64 seconds). After the final dependency/format checks, all 31 loading and rich-paste owner cases passed again (`/tmp/root-native-product-context-post-static-owner.log`). Full frontend lint, formatting, application/node/test TypeScript checks and the prefixed production build passed (`/tmp/root-native-product-context-check-complete.log`, `/tmp/root-native-product-context-build-complete.log`). Backend Ruff/format checks covered 282 files and Ty passed. No new complete backend run is claimed for this bounded context increment.

Actual Chromium verification used the production nginx build and disposable API under `/house.hold/`. The raw `/product/%2534` URL loaded product `4`, remained unchanged in the browser, and saved through canonical product PUT `4`. For native copy, a local relay held the real source-product HTTP response without replacing its body. The typed draft Name and the existing Description iframe survived release; actual Summernote retained both typed Draft text and the copied strong roast markup. Saving then created product `6`; a subsequent API read confirmed Name `Typed draft copy verified`, both description fragments, parent `1`, and no copied picture. These are actual HTTP persistence and browser-runtime checks, not fixture-generated rows.

The resulting Product form and retained rich content were visually inspected at 1440×900 and 390×844; captures are `/tmp/grocy-native-product-loading-desktop.png` and `/tmp/grocy-native-product-loading-mobile.png`. The phone document width stayed 390 pixels. The browser reported no uncaught application runtime errors. The task-owned Chromium session, API, nginx and copy-response relay were closed; ports 18861–18865 were checked clear. Synthetic records and uploads remain only in `/tmp/grocy-native-product-loading-browser.db` and its temporary data directory. No generated host page or fixture was added to the repository's production output. These are Chromium and viewport checks, not physical-device or cross-browser evidence.

### Native Product feature controls, partial copy and code actions

The selected native Product form now uses the pinned template's control policy rather than the generic Catalog's disabled-feature defaults. Location and default consume location become addressable hidden inputs with initial value `1` when Location tracking is disabled. Store does the same when Price tracking is disabled. With due-date tracking enabled and Opened tracking disabled, after-open days become an addressable hidden `1`; with due-date tracking disabled, after-open days are absent. Due days and due type instead become hidden `1` when due dates are disabled. The three disabled Freezing inputs have value `0` and no IDs, so the source copy callback cannot change them. Disabled Recipes renders an addressable hidden `0`; setting its checked property does not change its serialized value. Other removed controls are omitted from saves. Price unit and Quick open amount remain visible independently of the Price and Opened flags, and the modern-only purchase conversion factor stays absent.

Hidden values retain strings on the wire and any changes made by presets or copy. Visible due-type and purchase-price controls use their native radio choices. Stored invalid values fall back to the first choice, and the native label select has only its three source options. Initial checkbox checks compare the imported value with `1`; copy retains the separate native BoolVal rules. Imported hexadecimal enum text follows PHP's numeric-string comparison rather than JavaScript's broader Number conversion. The additional modern Catalog controls and numeric disabled defaults retain their prior contracts.

Copy now executes source assignments in order and respects control addressability. With Price tracking disabled and a nonnull source store, the missing ShoppingLocationPicker interrupts the callback after parent, Description and location have changed. Later fields retain their current defaults or draft values. That expected TypeError is logged without replacing the usable form or inventing a toast. The partial result can still be saved through the native custom-field completion stages. With a null source store, copying continues: addressable hidden location, consume location and after-open days can change, while ID-less freezing inputs stay zero. True-only checkbox assignments retain their source behavior.

Saved native Products expose the ordinary `/product/{canonicalId}/grocycode?download=true` link and authenticated `?size=60` preview. The separate Grocycode row remains visible when Stock is disabled and while unrelated related-data reads are pending or fail. Label printing uses the native page-owned request sequence: every click independently GETs the canonical print-label endpoint and optionally executes the configured webhook. It does not lock the form, broadcast a refresh or display the modern success receipt. Read failures remain silent, webhook failures use the native generic error, and departure retires pending callbacks. Label-printer visibility is independent of mutation grants. Modern code actions retain their existing behavior.

The source basis is Grocy 4.5.0 commit `21aa5eab218c8466f90729154cb293fffbe10c21`, `views/productform.blade.php` and `productform.js` copy and print handlers. Nine actual Blade/jQuery/serializeJSON probes, plus an imported-hex template probe, checked feature combinations, hidden addressability, copy interruption and checkbox differences (`/tmp/grocy-native-product-controls-probe.json`, `/tmp/grocy-native-product-controls-probe-summary.json`, `/tmp/grocy-native-product-enum-hex.json`). Description probes record paste commands; the separate runtime regression executes actual Summernote.

The feature baseline produced five intended failures and the Product code baseline four. The imported-checkbox regression also failed before its fix. The final feature owner passed 18 cases, and the final feature/code/rich-paste run passed 24 (`/tmp/grocy-native-product-features-before.log`, `/tmp/grocy-native-product-code-before.log`, `/tmp/grocy-native-product-checkbox-before.log`, `/tmp/root-native-product-controls-owners-final.log`). All 1,516 frontend tests passed in 117 files in 139.03 seconds (`/tmp/root-native-product-controls-full-elevated.log`). The sandbox-stalled attempt was interrupted and is not counted as a pass. Final full frontend lint, formatting, application/node/test TypeScript checks and the `/house.hold/` production build passed (`/tmp/root-native-product-controls-check-verified.log`, `/tmp/root-native-product-controls-build-verified.log`). This frontend increment changes no backend source and claims no new backend suite run.

Actual Chromium verification used that production build, nginx and a disposable Python API with Stock, Location, Price, Opened, Freezing and Recipes disabled, due-date tracking enabled and a synthetic client label printer. Two native print clicks sent two real payloads to the local receiver while preserving the unfinished Name draft; the authenticated code preview rendered at 60 pixels. Copy with a null store created product `7`, preserving copied rich markup, hidden locations `2` and after-open days `19`, while freezing stayed zero. Copy with a nonnull store logged the expected interruption, retained the earlier parent/Description/location changes and left minimum/due days at zero and after-open days at `1`; completing the actual form created product `8`. Authenticated API reads confirmed both saved rows (`/tmp/grocy-native-product-controls-copy-saved.json`, `/tmp/grocy-native-product-controls-partial-saved.json`), and the partial-copy real request bodies were captured in `/tmp/grocy-native-product-controls-partial-writes.json`.

Captures are `/tmp/grocy-native-product-controls-desktop-verified.png` and `/tmp/grocy-native-product-controls-mobile-radio-verified.png`. No unhandled browser runtime error occurred; the expected copy TypeError was a console report. These are Chromium viewport and synthetic-printer checks, not physical-device or printer evidence. Fixture data remains under `/tmp/grocy-native-product-controls-browser.db` and its temporary data directory. Broader background configuration/preference changes, raw malformed responses, translated controls, callback overlaps and release-image verification remain open; this bounded increment does not complete Product or application parity.

The document-level settings stability gap identified here is implemented in the following increment.

### Native Product document metadata and rich-editor lifetime

The selected Product editor now keeps its initial native configuration and user settings for the document lifetime. Feature controls, amount precision and formatting, energy labels, currency display and input normalization, presets, and native label-printer configuration all read that snapshot throughout the Product subtree. Live shared configuration/settings queries continue independently; their replies cannot change the open form's constraints or remove its controls. Each native document replacement gets a fresh scope and authoritative metadata. The modern Catalog keeps its live shared settings behavior.

The boundary is the Product editor, including related rows, custom values and code actions. Shell quick settings keep their local live behavior. File selections, pending writes and business reads are not metadata snapshots. Native barcode, conversion, Entries and Journal frames are separate documents and load fresh metadata rather than inheriting a parent's old settings. Product save-refresh and child-change callbacks still replace the native document, adopting new settings at that point. Source references are Grocy 4.5.0 commit `21aa5eab218c8466f90729154cb293fffbe10c21`, `default.blade.php:104–122,509–621`, `productform.blade.php:194–197`, `numberpicker.blade.php:52–57`, `numberpicker.js:96–114`, and `productform.js:13–16,51–54,267–278,381–387`. Shared DataTables settings explicitly mutate local userdata and are not claimed to be universally immutable.

Real browser testing also exposed an independent editor lifetime problem: active-query refresh rebuilt the Description iframe even after Product metadata was fixed. Native Product Description documents now stay outside background refetches, retaining the same rich-editor frame and typed draft. A failed initial editor document can still be retried manually. Modern Description behavior is unchanged. This uses the installed TanStack Query static-data policy, checked against its current primary documentation and local implementation; native metadata queries likewise use genuine document-scoped query results without overwriting shared cache data.

A separate baseline with metadata already fixed but the original Description implementation replaced the iframe (`/tmp/grocy-native-product-rich-before.log`). The final three-case lifetime owner and four Product sibling files passed all 80 cases (`/tmp/grocy-native-product-settings-final.log`). The owner proves changed generic HTTP responses and shared cache values, stable native controls/formatting/print destination and parameters, continued rich draft messages through the same frame, native save and replacement adopting new metadata, manual editor Retry, and retained modern live updates. JSDOM messages are not claimed to execute the child editor runtime.

The shared native database-change handling gap identified at this checkpoint is implemented in the following increment. Broader raw values, custom files, translations, remaining domain workflows and release gates are still open; Product metadata stability does not complete application parity.

A separate disposable real-HTTP probe confirmed the prerequisite backend issue under `GROCY_DATABASE_WAL=true`: a successful Product PUT returned 204 and a following GET returned the changed name, but `/api/system/db-changed-time` remained `2026-11-07 06:29:01` before and after the write, over 15 seconds apart. The main database mtime stayed unchanged while the WAL mtime advanced. Evidence is `/tmp/grocy-native-db-changed-probe.json`, from `/tmp/grocy-native-db-changed-probe.db` with the pooled API still open. This is stronger than the preceding standalone SQLite probe. The following increment repairs application commit signaling while preserving authenticated-read timestamps. That task-owned probe API was stopped after capture.

### Native database-change polling and committed write signals

Native pages using the default layout now poll `/api/system/db-changed-time` once a minute and replace the whole native document only when the source conditions permit it. The initial read is omitted for user ID `-1`; periodic polling continues even when automatic reload is disabled. The additional modern workflows retain their five-second/visibility query refresh. The standalone native API browser is excluded because `openapiui.blade.php` does not include the default-layout database-change script. A new API-browser regression failed with the inherited poll and passed after that layout exception was applied (`/tmp/root-native-swagger-poll-before.log`, `/tmp/root-native-swagger-poll-after.log`, 18 API/native/modern/metadata cases).

The native idle counter advances once per second and resets only on window mousemove, mousedown, click, scroll and keypress. A strictly newer timestamp permits reload when idle time is at least 50 ticks, automatic reload is enabled, no `form.is-dirty` exists and the body is not in a fullscreen card. A newer timestamp is consumed even when those conditions block reload; equal or older periodic replies do not replay a blocked change. A late initial reply still assigns its baseline unconditionally, matching the source. Missing initial baselines retain Moment's comparison with the current time. Errors log without replacing the page or displaying an invented notification. Requests can overlap; timers and callbacks retire with their originating document, preserving the raw URL during an eligible replacement.

Dirty markers follow the source DOM rules rather than a generic unsaved-state model: input/textarea keyup, paste and change, and select click mark that control and its closest form. Markers stay set after a value is restored. Product picture selection is now outside the main form, as in the source, so it can stay live during save and does not itself block idle reload. The selected Recipe/User picture and Equipment manual boundaries, all seven native settings pages without forms, and custom values inside their owning forms were also checked against their templates; no further discrepancy was found in that bounded ownership audit.

The isolated rich editor forwards the five source activity events through an origin-, channel- and current-frame-checked bridge. Code-view textarea events mark the actual enclosing parent form; ordinary contenteditable edits do not, preserving the native Summernote dirty-marker quirk. Standalone Shopping Notes has no owning form and therefore does not block reload by marking an unrelated form. Dirty notifications do not imply idle activity when the original event was not one of the five source events. Trusted activity and dirty messages remain separate from ready/change/paste/content-save protocols. Runtime regressions execute the unchanged vendored jQuery and Summernote scripts: two missing-bridge failures plus two trust/retirement controls were retained, and the combined rich-editor/description/paste/Shopping/Product lifetime scope passed 17 cases (`/tmp/grocy-rich-editor-activity-before.log`, `/tmp/grocy-rich-editor-activity-final.log`).

SQLite application sessions now publish committed physical business writes through the existing main database-file timestamp even in WAL mode. The API response, server-time-zone formatting and one-second resolution are unchanged. Narrow authentication-write markers exclude session/key `last_used` updates from publication and preserve externally supplied file timestamps. Successful commits, rollbacks, explicit SQL commit paths, nested savepoint rollbacks and pooled connection reuse share one transaction boundary. Equal-value updates are not assumed to be physically empty: SQLite can rewrite indexed pages even when the stored value is unchanged. Actual physical file/WAL changes gate publication.

An asynchronous advisory lock bridges application commit and timestamp publication on Linux, preventing another application worker's queued authentication update from restoring a timestamp older than a business publication. SQLite remains the system of record; the timestamp is a derived refresh signal, not an independent business-data store. No schema, persisted counter or new dependency was added. This accepts a post-commit/pre-publication crash window: committed records remain durable, but a missed notification can persist until a later business write. Filesystem publication errors log without falsely reporting a committed booking as failed. Native wall-clock changes and second-resolution coalescing remain; unsupported locking platforms warn and require one process for the same guarantee. Direct external WAL writers do not participate in the application publication boundary. These limitations are not claims of crash-atomic notification or cross-platform multiworker proof.

Backend source and integration cases first produced one WAL HTTP regression failure with five retained passes (`/tmp/grocy-database-change-before.log`). The nested-rollback refinement produced two further causal failures (`/tmp/grocy-database-change-nested-before.log`). The final owner passed 12 cases, and the affected authentication/native-login/stock/import/system scope passed 94 with one skip (`/tmp/grocy-database-change-owner-final.log`, `/tmp/grocy-database-change-siblings-final.log`). These include an actual subprocess writer queued against HTTP authentication in both journal modes, durable publication failure/recovery, rollback accounting and unchanged read timestamps. Source behavior is pinned to Grocy 4.5.0 commit `21aa5eab218c8466f90729154cb293fffbe10c21`, `grocy_dbchangedhandling.js`, `grocy.js:498–504`, `default.blade.php`, `productform.blade.php:674,919` and standalone `openapiui.blade.php`.

The native polling baseline produced three intended failures: missing initial polling, missing select dirty markers and the picture input still inside the form (`/tmp/grocy-native-dbrefresh-before.log`). The final native owner passed 12 cases, with modern refresh and Product metadata siblings bringing its initial combined run to 16. A subsequent full run exposed a test synchronization race: the fake minute clock could advance before the real React Query observer notification committed a changed preference. Traces isolated that ordering, and awaiting the public notification turn repaired the test without changing production behavior, weakening overlap assertions or increasing timeouts. The seven-file run then passed 96 cases and three isolated repeats (`/tmp/grocy-native-dbrefresh-trace-before.log`, `/tmp/grocy-native-dbrefresh-sync-final.log`, `/tmp/grocy-native-dbrefresh-repeat-after.log`).

A keyboard-dirty Product form then retained its input node and unsaved text after an eligible minute poll while an authenticated API read showed the distinct external server value. The initial text-insertion-only automation did not emit the source's key events and is retained only as a failed exercise attempt; the final check verified `is-dirty` before the external mutation and observed the same dirty input after 72 seconds.

The prefixed production build and final frontend lint, formatting of 518 files and application/node/test TypeScript checks passed (`/tmp/root-native-dbrefresh-build-final.log`, `/tmp/root-native-dbrefresh-check-verified.log`). The Purchase label payload ownership gap identified at this checkpoint is implemented in the following increment. Native entry/menu printing retains its separate GET protocol, and label/custom-field/receipt independence remains valid.

### Native Purchase booking-snapshot printer payloads

Purchase browser printing now sends the native webhook payload directly from its saved booking snapshot. The product name comes from the fresh pre-booking product-details response, the Grocycode product identity from the submitted selection, and each stock ID from the booking or transaction response. Single-label mode makes no further label-data request. Per-unit mode reads the transaction once and sends one independent webhook for each returned row, in order, without deduplicating IDs or rereading individual entries.

When best-before tracking is enabled, every label uses the translated `DD` caption and the first booking's due date, including per-unit labels whose transaction rows contain other dates. With tracking disabled, the due-date property is absent unless configured extra parameters supply it. Native JavaScript concatenation is preserved for null or missing receipt dates. The submitted label type selects only single/per-unit modes; an unrelated current form value or unsupported positive value cannot substitute another mode. Configured parameters still override payload keys, and the existing JSON/form encoding and credential boundaries remain shared with other printer actions.

The payload sender reuses the existing webhook implementation without fetching mutable label data. This removes the extra GET failure dependency and the unrelated `details`/`stock_entry` fields that the entry-print endpoint supplies. That endpoint and native entry/menu/Product printing remain unchanged. Printing still starts beside custom-field saving and does not gate receipts, form reset or embedded completion. Held per-unit callbacks retain their original local snapshot after the form selects another product; transaction failures remain console-only and each failed webhook uses its own native generic error. These rules apply to the shared Purchase form and its additional Stock overview dialog.

Source evidence is Grocy 4.5.0 commit `21aa5eab218c8466f90729154cb293fffbe10c21`, `purchase.js:22–27,129–175` and `grocy.js` RunWebhook. Serialized form values and the local details callback are per-save snapshots; the mutable `CurrentProductDetails` global is not the printing source. The existing entry GET remains appropriate for explicit menu/entry print actions and was not generalized away.

Five intended regressions failed against the pre-fix application snapshot (`/tmp/grocy-native-purchase-print-before.log`). Three new single-label cases check exact direct JSON/translated-date, date-disabled omission and form/configured-override payloads while every entry-label read would fail. Existing held transaction cases now change the current product to fresh `Later tare coffee`, due date to 2035 and custom-field draft to the next lot; released labels still use saved product `7`, fresh pre-booking `Booked coffee` and first-booking date `2026-12-24`, ignoring differing 2040/2041 row dates. Receipts and saved custom fields finish before the held read. The final Purchase owner has 26 cases; Purchase/printing/Stock overview/Shopping preference siblings passed all 46 (`/tmp/grocy-native-purchase-print-final.log`). A separate retained Purchase/printing/Product-code/native-stock-menu run passed 53 (`/tmp/root-native-purchase-print-siblings.log`). These runs overlap; shared raw-byte serializer tests remain in their existing owner.

All 1,538 frontend tests passed in 120 files in 138.60 seconds (`/tmp/root-native-purchase-print-full.log`). Full frontend lint, formatting of 518 files, application/node/test TypeScript checks and the `/house.hold/` production build passed (`/tmp/root-native-purchase-print-check-final.log`, `/tmp/root-native-purchase-print-build.log`). No backend source changed in this payload increment, and no additional full backend run is claimed beyond the immediately preceding shared-session verification.

Actual Chromium verification used production nginx and a disposable Python API under `/house.hold/`, with browser printing directed to a local synthetic receiver. Native single-label and per-unit bookings returned 200 and displayed the real `Added 1 Piece` and `Added 2 Pieces` receipts. The receiver captured exactly three browser-origin JSON POSTs, each containing only product name, Grocycode, first-booking due date and the two known configured parameters. The per-unit transaction GET returned the same two stock IDs used in its labels; the 95-request browser capture contained no per-entry `printlabel` GET. SQLite independently confirmed the three saved log rows and stock increasing from `1` to `4`. No mocked booking response or generated receipt supplied that evidence.

Evidence is `/tmp/grocy-native-purchase-print-evidence.json`, `/tmp/grocy-native-purchase-print-browser.har` and `/tmp/grocy-native-purchase-printer.jsonl`; captures are `/tmp/grocy-native-purchase-single-label.png` and `/tmp/grocy-native-purchase-per-unit.png`. No JavaScript page error occurred. The named browser, disposable API, nginx and receiver were closed, and ports 18881/18883/18884 were independently checked clear. Fixture data remains in `/tmp/grocy-native-purchase-print-browser.db` and its temporary data directory. This proves browser-to-receiver delivery and actual saved bookings, not physical printing or label rendering.

The payload checks above do not establish printer-job lifetime parity. Printer callbacks belong to the document running `purchase.js`, which survives an ordinary form reset. Shopping's static purchase modal also retains its iframe and URL when hidden; its `hidden.bs.modal` handler only resets the shopping workflow counters and footer (`shoppinglist.blade.php:351–360`, `shoppinglist.js:275–281`). Pending work can therefore continue in that hidden child document until the frame navigates or is removed.

Native Stock overview uses a different modal path. Its Purchase link opens the generic `IframeModal`, which calls Bootbox without enabling reuse (`stockoverview.blade.php:251–253`, `grocy.js:840–848`). The loaded Bootbox defaults to `reusable:false` and removes the dialog, including its iframe, on `hidden.bs.modal` after the closing transition (`public/packages/bootbox/dist/bootbox.js:91,372–375,1119–1123`). Keeping the parent Stock overview page alive does not retain this child document. The React Purchase dialog's removal must consequently retire its child-owned work; it must not inherit the shopping modal's retained-frame behavior. Exact Bootstrap fade timing remains a separate comparison.

Webhook errors are also local to the purchasing document. `RunWebhook` invokes `ShowGenericError`, whose Toastr container uses that window's body; it does not forward an error to the parent (`grocy.js:398–418,466–493`, `public/packages/toastr/toastr.js:144–149,186`). Purchase success is explicitly forwarded in a different message. A hidden shopping iframe can therefore receive a local error without creating a parent-page error toast.

The reproducible probe is `/tmp/grocy-printer-document-lifetimes-probe.mjs`, with results and source SHA-256 hashes in `/tmp/grocy-printer-document-lifetimes-evidence.json` and execution output in `/tmp/grocy-printer-document-lifetimes-run.log`. It executes the pinned jQuery, Bootstrap, Bootbox and Toastr code with the unchanged Grocy handlers in JSDOM: the generic iframe is present at `hide` and removed at `hidden`; the shopping iframe and URL remain; and a child generic error creates only a child toast, with no parent message. This is source-runtime evidence for DOM retention and error routing, not Chromium request disposal. The following increment implements document-owned Purchase printing. Physical-printer checks and the broader compatibility gates remain open; exact payloads do not establish full Purchase or application parity.

### Native Purchase printer document lifetimes

Purchase printing now captures a document-owned abort signal and generic-error reporter before booking work begins. The authenticated root owns this capability for a direct Purchase page and for each real Shopping purchase iframe. Native pathname/revision changes and modern pathname changes retire the previous scope. Ordinary form reset, query/hash edits and hiding a retained Shopping iframe preserve it. Each identity transition creates a new owner, so returning to the same URL cannot revive an earlier job. Scope cleanup also handles authentication/loading replacement and React Strict Mode replay without reusing an aborted signal.

The destructive React Purchase dialog has a nested scope. Removing it retires its transaction reads and webhook requests even though the Stock overview page survives. This matches generic native modal destruction; it does not reproduce the Bootstrap interval between `hide` and `hidden`. The per-unit callback checks its captured scope before dispatching labels. Transaction failures remain console-only while the owner lives; late failures and generic errors are suppressed after retirement. A same-document delayed webhook failure still reports through that document's Toaster after the form resets. Aborting a request cannot undo a label already accepted by a receiver.

Ten routed owner cases cover native and modern Stock dialogs, held transaction success/failure, webhook transport retirement, departure and return to the same URL, explicit same-URL native reload, and retained reset/query/hash callbacks with exact snapshot payloads and delayed errors. Eight fail against the saved pre-fix application, while both same-document positive controls pass (`/tmp/grocy-purchase-print-lifetime-before.log`). The final owner and Purchase/printing/Stock overview/Shopping preference siblings pass all 56 cases across five files (`/tmp/grocy-purchase-print-lifetime-final.log`). The new fixture exercises the real router and form; it does not simulate a Shopping child document. That distinct browser boundary is checked separately.

Actual Chromium verification used production nginx and a disposable Python API under `/house.hold/`. A local proxy buffered genuine transaction responses and delayed genuine optional-field responses by 250 ms; it did not manufacture bookings or receipts. Four two-unit bookings returned 200, and four field writes returned 204. The independent SQLite check confirmed log IDs 8–15, eight saved field values and stock increasing from 1 to 9. The 386-request HAR contains no per-entry label-data GET.

| Browser boundary | Pending transaction result | New printer POSTs after release |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Direct Purchase reset | Continued in the same document | 2; a controlled 503 displayed the live native generic error after reset |
| Generic Stock overview dialog removal | Browser canceled the read after the API had generated its 200 response | 0 |
| Shopping dialog hide | Dialog closed, actual iframe remained connected, read remained pending | 2 |
| Later Shopping iframe removal through navigation | Browser canceled the separate held read after the API had generated its 200 response | 0; no stale webhook error appeared |

The independent persistence/HAR check is `/tmp/root-printer-lifetime-independent-verification.json`. Screenshots record the reset, hidden-frame and post-removal states; the transient error was verified directly in the browser DOM before expiring. No JavaScript page error occurred. The named browser, API, nginx, receiver and proxy were closed, and ports 18891/18893/18894/18895 were independently checked clear. These checks establish browser request ownership and receiver delivery, without physical-printer or exact Bootstrap fade-timing claims.

### Native Barcode file-save recovery

The native Barcode editor now uses the existing native file controls and reads the live selected File when a delayed metadata/delete stage reaches upload. Each submission fixes a fresh filename and metadata from the selection at that moment. The later upload uses the then-current bytes under that fixed filename, preserving the source race when a failed primary barcode write unlocks the form and the user selects a replacement. Completing an older field write leaves the current input, label and dirty state intact. An unchanged replacement can therefore be submitted again, with a fresh filename on every attempt. Stored-file Delete also preserves the selected replacement while removing the original stored file.

This follows `productbarcodeform.js:24–52` and `viewjs/components/userfieldsform.js:15–31,53–108` from the same pinned release. Primary and custom-field writes remain independent. Plain metadata failures remain silent; subsequent delete/upload failures use the native generic error. The Barcode helper no longer applies a generic saved-field callback that overwrote newer drafts and cleared native dirty state. Other forms retain their separate field-saving protocols.

Two regressions fail against the saved pre-edit source: uploading stale bytes after a primary failure, and Delete clearing a selected replacement. The final file-recovery case performs two failed primary saves and a later successful retry, reads the actual request File bodies, checks the first fixed filename and later distinct retry names, and verifies the replacement remains selected. All 19 Barcode owner cases and 62 owner/sibling cases across four files pass (`/tmp/grocy-barcode-files-owner-after.log`, `/tmp/grocy-barcode-files-siblings-final.log`). The causal logs are `/tmp/grocy-barcode-files-before.log` and the final selected recheck `/tmp/grocy-barcode-files-before-final.log`; source details are `/tmp/grocy-barcode-files-source-notes.txt`. These are routed DOM/transport-fixture checks, without a claim of database file persistence. File chooser Clear/cancel and real-browser required-file validation remain unverified for this bounded change.

The combined final frontend passed all 1,550 tests in 121 files in 141.74 seconds (`/tmp/root-printer-lifetime-barcode-full.log`). Full lint, formatting of 520 files, application/node/test TypeScript checks, diff whitespace checks and the final `/house.hold/` production build passed (`/tmp/root-printer-lifetime-barcode-check.log`, `/tmp/root-printer-lifetime-barcode-build.log`). No backend production code changed, and no additional full backend run is claimed. The following increment addresses Inventory printing's awaited transaction read and mutable entry-label GETs. Broader compatibility/release gates remain open; these two repairs do not establish full Grocy parity.

### Native Inventory printing and estimate responses

Inventory browser printing now follows the native saved-booking protocol. Single mode posts its payload directly, and per-unit mode starts an independent transaction read before the post-booking details, custom fields and receipt stages continue. Each returned row produces a webhook in response order, including duplicate stock IDs. The payload retains the fresh pre-save product name, submitted product identity and first booking's due date even after receipt details refresh or the next product is selected. Every per-unit label uses that first date rather than its transaction row's date. Disabled date tracking omits the property. Only submitted label types 1 and 2 start printing; configured overrides, serializers and server-print suppression retain their shared behavior. This booking flow no longer requests individual entry-label data.

Printing eligibility reads the latest completed amount estimate when the booking succeeds, as native `data-estimated-booking-amount` does. It is not recalculated from the fresh save-time stock read or returned booking delta. A response arriving while Save is busy can change the decision. Missing initial estimates remain `NaN`, and the stored estimate survives product changes and ordinary reset until another response replaces it. Superseded estimate responses apply in completion order. Their own product's tare weight and stock unit control their displayed hint and Due-date requirement; the current selected product still owns the main input's minimum and quantity-unit control. Raw comparisons select the added/removed branch, independently of the rounded displayed magnitude.

Transaction lookup failure logs to the live document's console and does not block a saved count, receipt or reset. Webhook failure uses the document-local generic error. Printing captures the existing document task capability; the destructive Inventory dialog adds its own nested scope. Reset/new selection retains an old label snapshot, while dialog destruction or document departure cancels dependent reads/requests and suppresses later dispatch/errors. The eligibility decision is consumed once per booking, including a skipped decision, so the existing secondary-field retry cannot start a previously skipped print. Form-owned requests also abort on retirement, and booking settlement marks affected caches stale without starting replacement-page reads.

Sources are pinned `inventory.js:20–21,80–179,440–478`, `productamountpicker.js:95–100`, and the shared webhook/document ownership rules recorded above. The saved pre-fix application fails 17 cases and passes 20 cases (`/tmp/grocy-inventory-printing-before.log`). Some retirement regressions first encounter the old blocking transaction stage, so that baseline alone is not evidence of an independently reproduced stale-dispatch defect. A separate pre-derived-UI snapshot reproduces the late Coffee estimate being hidden after switching to a tare product (`/tmp/grocy-inventory-estimate-ui-before.log`). Its fixed regression checks the response's distinct unit and required Due date while the selected product retains minimum 3 and unit Piece. The final Inventory owner has 38 cases; its Purchase, Purchase-lifetime, serializer and Stock overview siblings pass all 83 across five files (`/tmp/grocy-inventory-estimate-ui-final.log`). These routed transport fixtures do not claim database persistence or physical printing.

Actual Chromium verification used production nginx and the live Python API under `/house.hold/`, with a disposable database, local printer receiver and proxy buffering genuine API replies. Four Inventory POSTs returned 200 and persisted stock counts 2, 4, 6 and 8 from an initial 1. Four metadata PUTs returned 204 and saved values for seven actual stock IDs. Count 8 completed through Stock overview's native Inventory dialog; removal canceled its held lookup after the API had generated a 200 response, and releasing it sent no label or stale error.

The following increment migrates Inventory's native custom-field callbacks, save-error stages and the shared Purchase/Inventory Clear behavior. ### Native Inventory save stages and shared stock-field reset

Inventory now launches dirty custom fields in native DOM order after the receipt-details read succeeds. Only the last dirty field's metadata and file callbacks advance completion; an earlier held or failed request cannot block that last callback. Metadata failures remain silent. File delete/upload failures show the native generic error, and a failed final field leaves completion waiting. A new explicit saving state controls the form independently of pending cache refreshes. This preserves native initial/booking error unlocks, final-field waiting and direct completion without the former committed-booking Retry extension.

| Stage | Native behavior now implemented |
| --- | --- |
| Initial details or Inventory POST failure | Remove cursor, unlock controls and log to the console; no inline error or Retry |
| Receipt-details failure after booking | Remove cursor only; retain the current disabled state, start no fields and emit no receipt/completion |
| Earlier metadata failure | Silent; the last dirty field can still finish the form |
| Final metadata failure | Silent; completion remains waiting |
| File failure | Generic error; only failure of the last dirty field prevents completion |
| Independent barcode-assignment failure | Unlock controls and show the generic error while receipt/field callbacks continue |

Every Inventory submission now owns its own booking, label decision and receipt. After a barcode failure unlocks the form, another Save performs a fresh details read and Inventory POST. The native global edit target still follows the latest completed booking: an older held receipt callback reads current field values and saves them to that latest target. The natural-user recovery regression checks two distinct counts, barcode attempts and label jobs, and both receipt callbacks writing the current second draft to transaction 2. This replaces the earlier test's synthetic change to a disabled control. Embedded completion sends ProductChanged, ShowSuccessMessage and CloseLastModal in order, with native single-quoted Undo markup. Real embedded Inventory and Purchase documents no longer create an additional child-local success toast; inline React dialogs still report through their parent document.

The shared `UserfieldsEditor` capability exposes live snapshots and asynchronous native Clear without remounting controls. Inventory reads its snapshot only after receipt details arrive. Both forms generate fresh file metadata for each save, then read the live selected File at upload time. Clear fetches the current definitions and applies their response order to current controls, including drafts entered while the GET was pending. It empties ordinary values and unchecks checkboxes without clearing dirty markers or file selection. The next booking consequently resubmits cleared dirty text and can upload the retained selection under a fresh filename.

Native file/image Clear blanks the visible label before reading the source's free `value` binding; link Clear reads free `data`. Missing bindings stop later fields, with the same partial visible reset. Actual binding resolution matters: window properties, global lexical bindings and named elements can change the result. The isolated source/global probes `/tmp/grocy-inventory-fields-clear-globals-probe.mjs` and `/tmp/grocy-inventory-fields-clear-globals-evidence.json` parse 68 static scripts for top-level declarations and exercise binding presence/absence. A later caller audit corrected their scope: `DateTimePicker.Clear` assigns undeclared `value = ""` at runtime before Inventory's field Clear and before Purchase's field Clear with date tracking enabled. A declaration-only scan missed this normal global creation. At that checkpoint the React caller omitted it and the successful file Clear's additional Delete handler. The native date/file reset increment below implements their ordinary property-backed workflow; broader custom lexical bindings remain outside that proof. Isolated missing-binding controls and upload-byte checks did not establish the complete caller sequence.

Inventory's initial stage regressions fail nine cases against saved pre-edit source (`/tmp/grocy-inventory-stages-before.log`). A separate saved pre-recovery source fails the natural barcode-unlock/second-booking case (`/tmp/grocy-inventory-barcode-overlap-before.log`). The shared editor's pre-edit source fails five Clear cases while its no-fields control passes (`/tmp/grocy-native-userfields-clear-before.log`). Purchase's actual caller separately reproduces premature reset (`/tmp/root-purchase-native-clear-before.log`) and the extra embedded success toast (`/tmp/root-purchase-child-success-before.log`). The final combined owner/sibling run passes 96 cases across six files (`/tmp/root-native-fields-focused-final.log`); the shared editor/Barcode/custom-field/Shopping sibling run passes 54 (`/tmp/grocy-native-userfields-clear-siblings-final.log`). Those totals overlap.

A genuine earlier metadata response was held while the last field completed the count-2 receipt/reset; releasing it preserved the newer draft. Counts 3 and 4 resubmitted cleared text and retained the same 82-byte selected file, producing distinct stored filenames with identical SHA-256 hashes. Controlled transport failures after real server commits distinguished final metadata's disabled/busy state from receipt-read failure's disabled state with cursor removed and no field writes. ### Native Purchase recovery and raw document history

Purchase now owns each submission's primary read, booking, label job and receipt separately. A failed initial read or add request unlocks the form, removes the busy cursor and logs without an inline error. Barcode-creation failure unlocks and reports the native generic error while the successful booking's field callbacks continue; another Save performs a new read and add. Failure of a used barcode's `last_price` update is silent and does not unlock the form. Both secondary requests now use the form owner's abort signal and mark affected caches stale on settlement. Inventory's barcode request has the same retirement guard. Direct Purchase completion releases controls before waiting for stock/card refresh, while the existing parallel last-field completion rules remain intact.

| Native stage | Values read at that stage |
| --- | --- |
| Save click | Serialized product, stock/display amounts, raw price, note, label, unit and store; disabled controls retain native omission rules |
| Initial product read completes | Current unit factor and price radio, current date/store/location pickers, captured raw price/display amount and the fresh product's tare weight |
| Booking completes | Current used-barcode identity and price, live raw `flow`/`barcode`, then embedded/direct branch selection and live field values |
| Last dirty field completes | Live first raw `listitemid`; direct completion compares the booked due date with the selection-global next due date and displays a warning tone |

Each Purchase starts its field requests synchronously after assigning its own transaction, so overlapping submissions retain their own field target. This differs from Inventory's separate receipt-read interval and latest-global-target rule. Receipt and printer product data remain submission snapshots. The earlier-due warning deliberately reads the mutable selection context instead, uses Moment comparison and has its own warning presentation.

The new document capability reads first raw parameters, preserving bare flags, duplicates, literal plus signs and source URI-decoding behavior. Successful barcode creation replaces the entire URI with the configured canonical Purchase URL, including in an embedded document. FinishFlow performs the three native RemoveUriParam passes in order; their URLSearchParams/decodeURIComponent transformations preserve remaining duplicate parameters, expose encoded delimiters as the source does, drop the hash and retain a final empty `?`. Same-path native menu navigation now targets the actual anchor URI before replacing the document, including navigation from `/purchase?` to `/purchase`.

Native shell presentation is latched for the document lifetime. Removing `embedded` through history replacement consequently preserves the actual form and its disabled state. Starting a barcode-assignment workflow performs a native document replacement for a physical Purchase page. An inline Stock dialog owns an independent logical Purchase URI and revision: it retires the old task scope, discards the old draft and reloads catalog/settings without replacing its parent Stock page. Catalog bootstrap is separate from the longer document task scope, so short field-tree replacement cannot retire a valid delayed print job. Full independent iframe metadata/context isolation for every inline dialog remains a separate boundary.

The query/history owner's original five-case baseline fails four cases with one inline isolation control passing (`/tmp/grocy-purchase-document-before-final.log`). A separate pre-bootstrap snapshot fails the fresh catalog and empty-query-marker cases (`/tmp/root-purchase-bootstrap-before.log`). The combined final Purchase/document owner passes 46 (`/tmp/root-purchase-recovery-owner-current.log`); the natural navigation/lifetime/authentication/refresh siblings pass 91 across five files (`/tmp/root-purchase-native-nav-focused.log`). These totals overlap other focused runs. Pinned executable query-transform evidence is `/tmp/grocy-purchase-document-source-evidence.json` with its adjacent probe and notes.

A controlled barcode-creation 503 after a real server 200 unlocked the first form while its last field response was held; a natural second Save made a new booking. Its held initial read then used captured amount/note with live due date `2031-04-05` and Pantry location after the older callback reset the form. The real earlier-due notice had warning severity. A controlled used-barcode price failure after a real 204 remained silent and kept controls busy until the field callback completed.
