Its later genuine field callback read `raw first` from the first duplicate live `listitemid` and emitted BroadcastMessage, AfterItemAdded, ShowSuccessMessage, Ready and CloseLastModal in order, with a parent-only receipt and no child success toast. The 195-request HAR and real barcode/stock rows are recorded in `/tmp/grocy-purchase-recovery-browser-evidence.json`, `/tmp/grocy-purchase-recovery-browser.har` and `/tmp/grocy-purchase-recovery-proxy.jsonl`. Independent SQLite/HAR checks are `/tmp/root-purchase-recovery-independent-verification.json`. The explicit files root remained under `/tmp` with no uploads. Labels were disabled for this bounded browser check; prior label delivery/lifetime proof remains separate, and no physical-printer or live PHP differential claim is made.

### Native Consume request lifetime and recovery

Consume and Mark as opened now bind the save-time product read and stock POST to the form's lifetime. Leaving during a held read prevents a later stock write. Leaving during a committed or uncertain POST cancels response delivery and suppresses later receipts, parent messages, resets and close callbacks. Settlement still marks affected stock, product, recipe, shopping, calendar and product-card caches stale without starting reads in a replacement document. The independent barcode-creation request has the same lifetime guard and stale-cache settlement. Existing selection and location read cancellation remains separate.

| Native failure stage | Form and feedback behavior |
| --- | --- |
| Save-time details GET, for either action | Unlock all native controls, remove the busy cursor, log the failure; preserve the draft without an inline or generic error |
| Consume POST | Native generic save error with technical details, unlock and console log |
| Mark as opened POST | Unlock and console log without a generic error |
| Independent barcode creation | Native generic save error and unlock, including a retained embedded form; successful consumption completion remains independent |

An explicit saving state now follows these native callbacks instead of the mutation's pending cache refresh. Direct Consume therefore releases controls, displays its receipt and resets before waiting for stock/card refresh. A newer draft survives that refresh. Embedded Consume sends ProductChanged, ShowSuccessMessage and CloseLastModal in source order, with single-quoted Undo markup and no extra child-local success toast. Inline dialogs continue to report through their parent document.

Mark as opened retains its distinct completion: even embedded, it stays open, emits a local receipt and optional move notice, and keeps recipe, quantity unit, location and amount ceiling. It does not actively refresh the product card. The move notice now has the native informational severity, using the existing cobalt palette. Quick-amount reset reads the live selected unit factor. Consume has no native custom-field Save/Clear workflow.

Consume's stock payload remains captured before the initial details read. Mark as opened deliberately retains the source's shared `jsonData` behavior: after an older Consume barcode failure unlocks a pending Open, a second Open can replace the first one's eventual wire body. Each receipt still describes its own captured amount. The natural overlap case exercises this through enabled user controls and verifies two Open writes of amount 2 with separate receipts for captured amounts 2 and 1.

The final Consume owner passes all 48 cases, retaining the previous 40 and adding eight (`/tmp/grocy-consume-recovery-after.log`). Its saved pre-edit source fails 12 and passes 36 (`/tmp/grocy-consume-recovery-before.log`): native feedback stages, late writes after held-read retirement, late committed-write effects and natural barcode recovery. The overlap baseline stops at the former barcode feedback failure; the later shared-body assertion and held-refresh unlock are verified in the final application without a separate pre-edit failure claim for those later stages. Scoped lint, formatting, test TypeScript and whitespace checks pass (`/tmp/grocy-consume-recovery-types.log`). Raw-query/bootstrap timing and broader document isolation remain separate parity work.

Actual Chromium verification used the final production build, live Python API and `/house.hold/` prefix. Both actions' held save-time details reads were canceled by real About navigation, with no late stock POST. A controlled initial-read 503 unlocked without a notification or write; a real Consume commit delivered as a controlled 503 produced the generic technical-details error, while a real Open commit delivered as a controlled 503 only logged and unlocked. A successful direct Consume showed its receipt/reset and enabled controls before the downstream product refresh was released. In an actual iframe, Open retained identical Document/Form nodes, a local success and informational move notice without parent close. A later Consume had no child-local receipt and sent exactly BroadcastMessage, ShowSuccessMessage with the real transaction's single-quoted Undo, then CloseLastModal.

The 511-request HAR contains five genuine stock writes: three Consume and two Open, each requested amount 1. The copied disposable fixture includes child product 4 under parent 1 with matching stock units; the requests allow substitution. They persisted five distinct transactions across seven log rows, including two split parent/child transactions. Recorded family stock decreased from 20.567 to 17.567, and the two Open transactions each total 1. Independent SQLite/HAR/proxy assertions verify the actual per-transaction totals, wire bodies, controlled response statuses and final stock (`/tmp/root-consume-recovery-independent-verification.json`). Browser evidence, the HAR and delivery gates are `/tmp/grocy-consume-recovery-browser-evidence.json`, `/tmp/grocy-consume-recovery-browser.har` and `/tmp/grocy-consume-recovery-proxy.jsonl`.

There were no uncaught JavaScript errors; three console errors correspond to the intentional transport failures. This is not a zero-failed-HTTP-request claim: the API-key browser fixture's manifest requests omitted credentials, and its temporary urllib proxy followed the expected login redirect inside the API, producing incidental 405 responses instead of forwarding the original 302. Current production nginx and backend GET/HEAD manifest routing are present; the evidence records the proxy-specific redirect cause. One initial About asset request also overlapped the informational-tone rebuild; fresh final-build documents were used thereafter, including both iframe checks. No live PHP differential or physical-device claim is made. Browser and all owned services closed, ports 18931/18933/18935 were independently checked clear, and the explicit files root remained empty under `/tmp`.

No backend source changed or additional full backend run is claimed.

### Native Consume raw documents and independent callbacks

Purchase and Consume now share a stock-document capability while retaining their public adapters. It separates initial control values, PHP-style initial embedded markup, live browser query reads and physical versus inline child history. Initial values use lazy first-literal-key reads. Live GetUriParam reads preserve bare boolean flags, literal plus signs, decoded values and ignored unescaped extra equals segments. Encoded PHP query keys can render an embedded shell and suppress scan mode without triggering Consume's literal-key JavaScript bootstrap. Ordinary history edits retain the existing document and layout.

Consume now captures its stock payload before the save-time details read, then reads live raw `flow`/`barcode` and its embedded completion branch when the stock POST succeeds. Barcode success replaces the complete URI with the configured canonical Consume URL, including for embedded documents, without replacing the form. Direct FinishFlow updates the currently selected product's local barcode search data and performs the three native RemoveUriParam transforms. Remaining duplicate parameters and encoded delimiters follow the source transforms; the hash disappears and a final empty question mark is retained. Barcode assignment replaces a physical native document or starts a new independent inline child revision, retiring its old requests, resetting its draft and refreshing its catalog/settings while preserving the parent Stock URI.

Embedded initialization now starts the requested location's entries read before its checkbox transitions and product initialization. The initial select contains the document's locations, so unmatched, bare and empty location values do not force a nonexistent option. Product defaults can subsequently replace the visible location. A late initial response still appends its own matching entries, changes the shared sum and toggles matching stock IDs; its separate current-product details callback updates the amount limit. The held-response owner verifies visible Pantry with late Shelf options and a limit changing from 1.00 to 1.50 in the saved box unit, rather than incorrectly making the query location win.

Product, location and barcode reads now belong to the document without generation-based cancellation. An older product-details response can publish its quantity choices and start its captured product's locations request after another product was selected. Those later location callbacks request entries for the current picker, and each entries callback starts its fresh details read for that current picker. Unit options remain independent from the mutable details global. Location options retain response order and callback-local default labels. Repeated notifications of one React text commit are deduplicated at the picker adapter; independent asynchronous responses still run. Native product/location read failures log without the former inline Retry extension; catalog/bootstrap loading errors retain their separate read-retry controls.

The source probe executes the complete pinned Consume script, amount-picker script, extensions and jQuery in a reduced DOM with queued API/component boundaries (`/tmp/grocy-consume-document-source-probe.mjs`, `/tmp/grocy-consume-document-source-evidence.json`). It records both initial/default response orders, unmatched query locations and cross-product callbacks. It also verifies the exact amount-picker class placement: its grouped change includes the quantity-unit select, so scan mode refreshes the limit from the current sum before validating and issuing its separate save-details read. This preserves valid first scans and the invalid-scan warning/error sound. The probe uses actual form validity with matching numeric step; it is neither a full native browser nor a live PHP comparison.

The Consume owner passes 56 cases, retaining the previous 48 and adding eight; the combined Consume/document owner passes 64 (`/tmp/grocy-consume-document-owner-after.log`, `/tmp/grocy-consume-document-owner-final.log`). Its saved pre-edit source fails seven and passes 49 (`/tmp/grocy-consume-document-owner-before.log`): console-only read failures, the missing startup phase, live raw callback reads and older different-product callbacks. Captured-body and existing positive controls still pass that baseline. The separate document owner fails seven of eight cases against its saved pre-edit source, with inline parent-query isolation passing as a control (`/tmp/grocy-consume-document-before-final.log`). Its final eight cases and the existing Purchase document/print-lifetime siblings pass all 24 (`/tmp/grocy-consume-document-after.log`). These totals overlap. Fixtures now wait for actual unit/location/entry state and scheduled focus rather than treating an enabled control as proof that all native asynchronous callbacks finished. Original scan amounts, sounds and limits remain unchanged. Scoped lint, formatting and app/test TypeScript checks passed.

Actual Chromium verification under `/house.hold/` used flat disposable products with one stock unit and no parent substitution. A held initial location-1 entries response arrived after default location 2's 8-unit result: the 4-unit row appended and its requested stock ID became checked/selected while the visible location stayed 2. The limit remained 8 until its separately held fresh details response arrived, then became 12. An actual iframe's stock POST retained its submitted amount 1, location 2 and unchecked flags while live history changed to a first bare barcode followed by a valued duplicate. Completion sent barcode JSON `true`, the source's three parent messages and no child-local receipt. Releasing the barcode response canonicalized the child URL, removed query/hash and retained the same Document, Form, embedded layout and disabled controls. A separate genuine direct booking ended at `/house.hold/consume?payload=a&flow=still-here&keep=one&keep=two&flag=`, proving the three transforms expose an encoded delimiter between passes, retain duplicate unknown values, normalize a bare flag and remove the hash. Independent SQLite/HAR/proxy assertions verify bodies, barcode type/storage, stock and delivery gates (`/tmp/root-consume-document-independent-verification.json`). There were no HTTP failures, console messages or uncaught JavaScript errors in this bounded browser run. ### Native date reset and file Clear/Delete sequence

Inventory now publishes the date picker's empty `value` before requesting stock-field Clear, including with date tracking disabled: the native Inventory template retains its hidden due picker. Purchase publishes it only when date tracking is enabled. A fresh Purchase document without date tracking consequently retains the source's missing-binding stop at the file; an existing same-document property can instead supply its value. Date input editing does not itself publish this binding: those keyup handlers use a local variable.

The binding capability surrounds the form's bootstrap subtree. A physical native document publishes the actual global property, reads it live when definitions arrive and restores its prior descriptor when the document retires. Logical child forms keep their own publication and cannot inherit a physical parent's owned date reset. Ordinary form reset and delayed Clear preserve the scope, while Purchase assignment revisions create a fresh child scope. Existing genuine free-binding lookup remains the fallback when nothing was published. This covers the tested property-backed workflow; arbitrary global lexical declarations, exotic property descriptors and complete custom-script isolation for inline documents remain separate boundaries.

Successful file Clear blanks its label, exposes the native public Show URL with the empty filename and applies the corresponding native thumbnail URL. It retains the selected File, dirty state and required attribute, then continues with later definitions. Its installed Delete behavior persists across subsequent file selections. Delete hides Show and marks the old filename as defined but empty without clearing the FileList or adding a new dirty marker by itself. Link Clear still resolves its separate free `data` binding; date reset supplies no link data and can leave a later text field unchanged after that distinct error.

The next selected-file save writes new metadata, then attempts the compatibility DELETE for the defined empty old filename before uploading. Its actual 404 reports the native generic error and prevents the upload. An earlier file's failure can coexist with a last text field's completed receipt/reset; a final file failure leaves the form busy without a committed-booking Retry extension. If the input's live FileList is empty, stale cached upload data is ignored: metadata is an empty object unless a truthy old filename requests an empty field value, and no file upload runs. This is an empty-input contract, not a claim about every browser's chooser Cancel behavior.

The combined source probe runs the pinned DateTimePicker and UserfieldsForm scripts with actual jQuery, extensions, moment, Bootstrap and Tempus Dominus initialization/reinitialization; no date-widget stub is used (`/tmp/grocy-native-date-clear-source-probe.mjs`, `/tmp/grocy-native-date-clear-source-evidence.json`, `/tmp/grocy-native-date-clear-source-log`). It invokes their methods in the independently established caller order in constructed component markup, with queued XHR and a real JSDOM File. It verifies visible/hidden date cases, pre-existing property overwrite, held definitions, continued text reset, the independent link error and metadata→empty-name DELETE→no-upload/final-error sequence. It does not render Blade or execute the complete native booking page, and its surrounding validation callback does not prove browser validity. A separate disposable Python TestClient check records authenticated compatibility DELETE 404, anonymous 401 and modern v2 DELETE 400. PHP's nonempty filename route was source-read; no live PHP HTTP comparison is claimed.

### Native Inventory document, staged reads and estimate state

Inventory now owns its raw initial document parameters and its later URI reads separately. Initial product/name controls use the native first-parameter rules; the product-change bootstrap runs only for the two known flow literals or literal JavaScript `embedded` presence. A plain product bookmark or unknown flow keeps the selected control without fetching its form details or showing the initially hidden Product card. PHP-style embedded markup remains fixed independently of later raw JavaScript query reads. The shared stock-document adapter now also serves Inventory, including canonical barcode-success history replacement, sequential FinishFlow cleanup, physical document reload on Assign and isolated logical documents for inline dialogs. Reload refreshes catalog/settings, drafts, native bindings and document-owned tasks without rewriting an inline dialog's parent URI.

Save captures product, converted count, raw price, note, label and barcode metadata's serialized store at the click. After the primary product GET, it reads the current conversion factor for that captured price and the live date, purchased date, store and location controls. Booking success reads the current raw flow/barcode for its independent barcode request. The receipt read then chooses the embedded/direct field-completion branch; later query changes while the last custom field is pending do not change that choice. Existing last-dirty-field completion, latest Inventory field target, independent receipt/printer snapshots, ordinary date/file Clear and owner retirement remain in place.

Selection details and barcode defaults now settle independently. A late barcode reply can change amount, unit and note while a primary Save GET is pending; it does not recapture the submitted count or note. Selection/read failures follow the native console-only path, and selection reads do not lock the form. A completed EndUiBusy restores the quantity-unit control, including a single-option control. The purchased-date component follows PHP truthiness while its Save branch follows JavaScript truthiness: string `false` renders and submits the picker; boolean false omits it; string `0` omits the component but enters the script branch, producing the actual undefined-component error before POST and retaining the busy state until document retirement.

Each amount edit captures its hidden stock-unit count before the estimate GET. Responses remain effective after later edits, selection or a completed reset. Returned stock/tare/unit values determine the stored signed estimate without replacing the selection's current product details. Displayed add/remove text and required due/location fields are stateful: raw comparisons select their branch even when the signed estimate rounds to zero. Equal non-tare stock and under-tare values hide existing text while retaining requirements; neutral tare gross count can redisplay the previous text. A late response after reset can consequently show an estimate and require date/location while product and amount remain empty. Unit-only and barcode-only updates do not manufacture an amount-keyup estimate. Browser label dispatch continues to use the stored signed estimate and the submission's printing snapshots.

The isolated source probe executes the complete pinned Inventory script and actual amount/date widgets with pinned jQuery, Moment, Bootstrap and Tempus Dominus. Its queued API/component boundaries, reduced DOM, validation limitations and exact observations are documented in `/tmp/grocy-inventory-timing-source-notes.md`; executable and evidence are `/tmp/grocy-inventory-timing-source-probe.mjs` and `/tmp/grocy-inventory-timing-source-evidence.json`. It proves source callback behavior within those boundaries, not PHP rendering, HTTP persistence or physical hardware.

The final document owner has 14 cases. Eleven fail for the intended missing behavior against saved pre-edit source and three controls pass (`/tmp/grocy-inventory-document-before.log`); the original 13-case baseline is preserved separately. Inventory's 59 timing/estimate cases retain the prior 48 with source-backed corrections and add eleven; their saved-source baseline fails 14 and passes 45 (`/tmp/grocy-inventory-timing-before.log`). The current document owner, Purchase/Consume document siblings and Purchase printer-lifetime owner pass all 38 cases (`/tmp/grocy-inventory-document-after.log`). The combined Inventory timing/estimate and document owners pass 73 cases (`/tmp/grocy-inventory-timing-after.log`). These totals overlap. The inline Assign and physical reload/held-printer scenarios are separate because the native unknown-product menu cannot be opened while the failed barcode flow remains in the URI; no fixture bypasses that source reachability rule. The equal-stock regression replaces the displayed count directly: clearing first would create a genuine intermediate zero estimate and change the requirements before the equal reply. The selection-error fixture targets the form's reader after diagnosing the independent Product card request consuming its initial transport failure; no production-only test seam was added.

Actual Chromium verification under `/house.hold/` confirms rounded-zero add/remove requirements and the retained under-tare, neutral-gross and equal-stock text. Two genuine Inventory bookings returned 200 and persisted separate correction transactions, each changing a flat product's stock from 4 to 5. One held the original count-7 estimate JSON until after a real count-5 booking, receipt and reset; releasing the remaining unchanged bytes redisplayed add-3 information with blank product/count and required date/location. The test proxy flushed one original byte with explicitly transport-only no-store/no-buffering headers before holding the remainder; this is controlled delivery of a genuine response, not fabricated stock or native server-latency proof. The other held barcode metadata and then the primary details read: the barcode response changed live display to 8, hidden count to 4, unit factor to 2 and note to `late-barcode-note`. The actual booking still sent captured count `5.` and empty note, while captured price 3 used the live factor and became `6.00`. SQLite stores the blank note as NULL. No successful typed-note edit is claimed for that browser case.

The actual settings API initially converted a saved false override to string `0`, which reproduced the hidden purchased picker, undefined `GetValue` error, disabled Saving form and no booking POST. Deleting that override restored an actual boolean false default and enabled both successful cases. The reference `UsersService::GetUserSettings` merges raw saved values over typed defaults; `BaseController` supplies that same result to the layout's direct JSON encoding. Three earlier positive-save attempts were contaminated by that override. Their initial cache-lock diagnosis is explicitly withdrawn; they establish neither a Chromium limitation nor an application caching defect. One discarded attempt returned a proxy timeout 504. The corrected positive HAR has 50 requests, two successful Inventory POSTs and no HTTP failures; the raw-0 HAR has 82 requests and no Inventory POST. Browser observations, discarded-trial classifications and exact errors are in `/tmp/grocy-inventory-timing-browser-evidence.json`; HARs and controlled delivery are `/tmp/grocy-inventory-timing-positive.har`, `/tmp/grocy-inventory-timing-raw0.har` and `/tmp/grocy-inventory-timing-proxy.jsonl`.

Two uncaught non-finite-gradient errors occurred in the unchanged price-history trendline vendor after same-day price points. The dataset/configuration and pinned fitter arithmetic are consistent with a shared source edge; that classification is an inference, not a native-browser reproduction or established React regression. No chart fix or zero-JavaScript-error claim is made. The Inventory saves and receipts completed. Independent SQLite, all five HARs (308 requests), real request bodies/statuses and callback-delivery ordering pass verification (`/tmp/root-inventory-timing-independent-verification.json`). Browser and owned services closed; ports 18961/18963/18965 were independently checked clear, and the explicit temporary files root is empty. No live PHP differential or physical-device claim is made.

All 1,660 frontend tests pass in 125 files in 153.41 seconds (`/tmp/root-inventory-timing-full.log`). Full lint, formatting of 530 files, application/node/test TypeScript checks, the final `/house.hold/` production build and final diff whitespace checks pass (`/tmp/root-inventory-timing-check-final.log`, `/tmp/root-inventory-timing-build-final.log`). A final source review found no further concrete defect within this increment's scope. No backend source changed or additional full backend run is claimed. The next confirmed ordinary Transfer defect is its successful booking waiting for optional barcode metadata; a read-only source/caller brief also records its distinct bootstrap, captured-body/live-receipt and document-lifetime requirements (`/tmp/grocy-transfer-next-gap.md`). Wider query/script-error, custom binding, translation, configuration, browser/device and release gates, including native comparison of the observed chart edge, remain open. Full Grocy parity remains unproven.

### Native Transfer document and callback lifecycle

Transfer now owns raw initial document parameters separately from later URI reads, through the shared stock-document adapter. Only literal JavaScript `embedded` presence replays the initial picker change: a plain product bookmark, either known flow alone, or an encoded embedded key retains the initial selected control without loading its form details or showing the initially hidden Product card. Embedded location/stock initialization preserves the native From/checkbox event order. Inline dialogs use their own logical URI and fresh catalog/settings on Assign; physical Assign reloads the native document and retires its old owner. Canonical barcode-success history replacement and sequential FinishFlow cleanup remain shared with the other stock documents. Unknown-product actions read the live flow after direct FinishFlow, independently of the original hint awaiting metadata success.

The main Save captures product, hidden converted amount, From, To and optional stock identity before its product GET. Those body fields stay captured even when an earlier barcode error unlocks the controls during a later booking. After booking, raw flow/barcode/embedded, selected From/To text and freezer flags are read live. Barcode creation runs independently of receipt, parent messages, direct EndUiBusy, FinishFlow, reset and cache refresh. Its later success replaces the whole URI with the canonical Transfer URL; its failure independently unlocks and shows the native technical-details generic error. Primary GET/POST failures only log and unlock, preserving the draft for a new Save. Embedded child completion emits parent messages without a child success toast. Direct controls and inline closure no longer wait for cache refresh. Frozen/Thawed use info and the BoolVal-based should-not-freeze notice uses warning. EndUiBusy also restores the quantity-unit and unchecked specific-item controls; direct reset explicitly disables the specific selector again.

Details, locations, barcode defaults, stock-code source and entries now have independent callbacks without per-selection generation cancellation. The locations response changes From as it appends options and then reads the current raw location query; those changes can dispatch entries requests for the currently selected product. Each From event captures its source/forced identity, while its later entries callback appends/deduplicates against the live options and uses the current unit factor. A blank specific-item callback instead filters by the current From at response time. Stock-code source selection changes the visible From without generating an entries request. Older selection and entries responses remain effective until actual document retirement. All primary and secondary requests use that owner's abort signal; booking settlement marks caches stale even when the page retires.

Hidden converted amount is state rather than a render-time division. Actual first Reload can clear the numeric display and leave hidden `NaN`; setting the stock unit alone does not recalculate it. A later Reload can calculate using its first conversion before selecting the displayed stock unit. The unit-change and barcode group events retain their different max/conversion behavior. Missing `stock_default_transfer_amount` has no native configured default and leaves initial/reset display blank. Present values pass through actual numeric-input sanitation and native quantity precision normalization. On a specific-item success, reset's uncheck dispatches an entries read before clearing product/unit/source; its late reply can restore old options and a `NaN` max after the form is otherwise reset.

The isolated pinned Transfer/amount-widget runtime probe records these stages, both source/stock-code callback orders, raw query changes, live-factor entry limits, metadata overlap, parent messages and console-only failures (`/tmp/grocy-transfer-source-probe.mjs`, `/tmp/grocy-transfer-source-evidence.json`, `/tmp/grocy-transfer-source-notes.md`). It uses pinned jQuery/extensions and declares its reduced DOM, component/API/message doubles and validation/busy limitations. Separate execution of the actual pinned `RefreshLocaleNumberInput` with pinned jQuery and real numeric DOM setters confirms absent, fractional, leading-zero and invalid defaults (`/tmp/root-transfer-default-source-probe.mjs`, `/tmp/root-transfer-default-source-evidence.json`). These are source-runtime observations, not complete native browser or live PHP persistence proof.

The original 17 Transfer owner cases fail eleven and pass six against saved pre-edit source (`/tmp/grocy-transfer-owner-before.log`). The document owner's eleven cases fail nine and pass two (`/tmp/grocy-transfer-document-before.log`). Document plus Purchase/Consume/Inventory siblings pass 39 (`/tmp/grocy-transfer-document-after.log`). The added missing/fractional-default and late-reset-read cases separately fail three and pass the earlier 17 against the saved intermediate application (`/tmp/grocy-transfer-defaults-before.log`). The final Transfer owner has 20 passing cases; owner/document/action siblings pass all 41 (`/tmp/grocy-transfer-defaults-final.log`). These totals overlap. Tests explicitly exercise source-reachable metadata failure unlocking a later booking; they do not force disabled controls. Fixture corrections commit an unchanged prefilled product with Enter, replace stale references after actual Assign reload and target form reads in an embedded page rather than depending on the changed fetch cache policy.

Actual Chromium under `/house.hold/` verifies the source-reachable overlap: transfer A completes its receipt/reset before its held metadata response; delivering that genuine API-200 response as controlled HTTP 503 unlocks pending transfer B. The user then changes From/To to Fridge/Transfer freezer. B still posts captured amount `1.` and Pantry-to-Fridge IDs, while its receipt and Frozen/warning notices use those live controls. No stock moves to the freezer. An actual iframe emits ProductChanged, ShowSuccessMessage and CloseLastModal before metadata completion, with no child receipt; metadata success strips the whole query/hash to canonical Transfer while preserving the same Document/Form and disabled Saving state. A genuine optional barcode GET delivered as controlled 503 leaves already-loaded details, locations and units usable and only logs. Actual About navigation while a save-details GET is held aborts it and produces no later product-11 transfer POST.

The HAR records 455 requests and five genuine transfer transactions with ten movement rows. The first two writes belong to an abandoned overlap attempt whose held second response exceeded the QA proxy's 60-second timeout; they remain real persisted transactions and are not counted as successful overlap UI proof. The repeated overlap passed after the owned proxy timeout was extended. Four transfer POSTs delivered 200 and the discarded one delivered 504 after its real API commit. Three controlled 503 deliveries cover two metadata errors and the optional barcode read; metadata rows consequently remain committed. Independent verification matches all five posted bodies to SQLite source/destination rows, checks conservation and exact final location amounts, confirms zero freezer stock and no product-11 booking, and correlates callback ordering (`/tmp/root-transfer-independent-verification.json`). The original product returns to Pantry 2/Fridge 8; the iframe product finishes Pantry 3/Fridge 7; the departed product stays 2/8.

Browser evidence and delivery logs are `/tmp/grocy-transfer-browser-evidence.json`, `/tmp/grocy-transfer-browser.har` and `/tmp/grocy-transfer-proxy.jsonl`. No uncaught JavaScript errors occurred. Manifest resources show HAR status 0 with backend GET 302 authentication redirects under this API-key-only browser setup; no clean-resource or cookie-session claim is made. Browser cases use an explicitly saved and GET-verified string-1 transfer default; absent/default-rounding behavior has source-runtime and routed-owner proof, without an additional browser-default claim. Price tracking was disabled, so this run does not reverify the earlier chart edge. Browser and all owned services closed; ports 18971/18973/18975 were independently checked clear, and the explicit temporary files root is empty. No live PHP differential or physical-device claim is made.

All 1,682 frontend tests pass in 126 files in 152.87 seconds (`/tmp/root-transfer-full.log`). Full lint, formatting of 532 files, application/node/test TypeScript checks, the final `/house.hold/` production build and diff whitespace checks pass (`/tmp/root-transfer-check-final.log`, `/tmp/root-transfer-build-final.log`). Final source review's missing/fractional defaults and specific-reset event gaps were repaired and causally verified. No backend source changed or additional full backend run is claimed. The next confirmed stock gaps are Consume/Inventory's stale post-FinishFlow lookup gates and quantity normalization at source-defined phases (`/tmp/grocy-stock-flow-next-gap.md`); the brief corrects an initially overstated Inventory estimate claim after inspecting the current hidden-count rounding. These checks cover the bounded Transfer implementation; further malformed-response/query/transport, translation, configuration, browser/device and release comparisons remain open. Empty successful booking receipts remain a malformed-response edge rather than an ordinary source API result: both implementations reject a positive transfer without transaction rows. Full Grocy parity remains unproven.

### Native stock quantity refresh and live workflow gates

Consume and Inventory now gate unknown-product actions with their current document's raw flow presence, independently of the original hints and later barcode success. Normal direct completion removes flow through FinishFlow and permits the next unknown product even while metadata is pending, or when the new-product flow never dispatches metadata. Consume Open deliberately retains its flow and its disabled lookup workflow. Purchase's lookup gate also uses its live document rather than the prior completion latch; initial hints remain separate.

The shared `nativeQuantityValue` follows a real numeric input's value sanitation and the pinned formatter's English, ungrouped Intl quantity normalization. It is called at source-defined phases rather than on every edit. Inventory normalizes selected stock before conversion and estimate, so precision-2 stock `9.996` displays valid `10` and `2.675` displays/converts `2.68`. Its barcode callback converts and validates before formatting the display; hidden amount and conversion help retain their captured raw amount/factor, with no invented estimate request. Transfer's corresponding barcode group updates hidden conversion/help and the total-stock maximum before formatting its display. With barcode `2.675` in the stock unit, the visible value is `2.68` while hidden remains `2.67`; with `0.675` and factor `0.5`, display is `0.68`, hidden/help remain `1.35`, and max follows total stock times the selected factor. Transfer's already-verified initial/reset normalization uses the same helper.

Consume normalizes selected defaults and quick-amount products before conversion, and normal/Open reset values at their own source phases. Initial page assignment stays raw. A quick normal reset has already removed units and can therefore leave blank display/hidden NaN; Open retains its unit and its converted quick amount. Barcode formatting occurs after capturing the group conversion. Scan reads that synchronous capture rather than a later render, allowing native display `2.68` with a real `2.67` booking. A programmatic-only barcode refresh after focus does not manufacture a blur conversion. Genuine user edits while focused still mark the later native change-on-blur path, preserving the earlier stock-code focus behavior.

Purchase now separates independent product details and barcode defaults, uses its document owner's signal and allows older callbacks to apply. A held or failed optional barcode no longer withholds product defaults, controls or the earlier shopping auto-submit stage. Selection/read failure follows the source's console-only path. Initial/default display normalizes at startup; the details callback validates and may serialize Shopping auto-submit before later normalization, then performs the final keyup conversion. Thus an overprecision shopping prefill can become visibly valid without retrying the earlier failed auto-submit attempt. Barcode conversion precedes display normalization and ScanModeSubmit(false); the display/wire split is retained. Direct Purchase reset intentionally restores the raw default without a new formatter call. The Product card starts hidden until the actual picker event, and EndUiBusy does not disable an invalid empty form's OK control; submission still validates it.

Purchase price hints separately retain their amount basis at the source refresh phase. A barcode's first hint can use its raw pre-format quantity; a later user price or radio edit refreshes from the live formatted display without changing the captured quantity. The routed case changes total price to `53.6` after barcode `2.675` displays `2.68`: hint becomes `20.00`, while booking amount stays `2.67`. This increment normalizes main quantity controls, not every currency/custom input touched by the source's global formatter.

The full pinned Purchase/Consume/Inventory scripts execute with actual amount/number widgets, unaltered locale formatter and native form/busy helpers in constructed page-content DOM (`/tmp/grocy-stock-quantity-source-probe.mjs`, `/tmp/grocy-stock-quantity-source-evidence.json`, `/tmp/grocy-stock-quantity-source-notes.md`). Eighteen assertions cover the normal, barcode, scan, quick-reset, auto-submit and flow stages; component, API, sound and parent boundaries and reduced-markup limits are declared. Separate actual-script Inventory and Transfer probes record precise hidden/help/max values (`/tmp/grocy-inventory-quantity-source-evidence.json`, `/tmp/grocy-transfer-quantity-source-evidence.json`). These are independent source runtime observations, not complete PHP/browser or persistence proof.

Inventory's five new cases fail against its saved source and its 64 owner plus document/action siblings pass 96 (`/tmp/grocy-inventory-quantity-before.log`, `/tmp/grocy-inventory-quantity-after.log`). Consume retains 56 and adds eight: saved source fails seven and passes 57, including the Open negative control; current owner has 64 and siblings pass 82 (`/tmp/grocy-consume-flow-quantity-before.log`, `/tmp/grocy-consume-flow-quantity-final.log`). The focused intermediate blur regression is separately causal (`/tmp/grocy-consume-barcode-blur-before.log`). Purchase's 49 cases fail nine and pass 40 against saved pre-edit source, including source-backed migration of eager-card and inline Retry expectations; current owner/document/printing/Transfer siblings pass 85 (`/tmp/root-purchase-quantity-baseline-final.log`, `/tmp/root-purchase-quantity-final.log`). Its added hint case separately fails the intermediate source and passes after repair (`/tmp/grocy-purchase-hint-before.log`, `/tmp/grocy-purchase-hint-after.log`), bringing the owner to 50. Transfer's two added cases fail saved source; its 22 owner plus document/action siblings pass 51 (`/tmp/grocy-transfer-quantity-before.log`, `/tmp/grocy-transfer-quantity-after.log`). These totals overlap and do not imply full family parity.

Actual Chromium under `/house.hold/` verifies seven bounded workflows, with eight genuine 200 booking requests and eight transaction/log rows. Inventory's normalized stock `9.996` books count `10` (correction `0.004`); its barcode then shows `2.68` while booking count `2.67` (correction `-7.33`). The selected-10 wait, request, receipt and ledger are retained; the named selected-state JSON was captured after Enter/reset and is not a stable pre-save display artifact. Enabled barcode scan consumes captured `2.67`. Purchase's details and controls remain usable while barcode metadata is held, then display `2.68` books captured `2.67` and direct reset restores invalid raw `2.675`. Its valid shopping prefill auto-saves; a corrected overprecision prefill becomes valid after formatting but does not auto-submit. Open books `2.68`, retains its flow and blocks the unknown workflow.

An earlier barcode-under-shopping-flow attempt was source-blocked without a GET/POST and was not counted as a phase-barrier success; the before-optional-barcode shopping boundary remains declared source-probe evidence, not that natural browser path. The verifier records that mapping and uses stable log/transaction identities rather than falsely treating the historical receipt's stock ID as immutable. ### Native stock price, currency refresh and hint snapshots

Inventory now captures its actual hidden Price when price tracking is disabled, rather than replacing it with zero. The pinned Inventory script assigns product last price into the hidden control and unconditionally converts the captured value with the live factor at Save. Purchase deliberately differs: its primary booking price remains zero with tracking off, but its independent used-barcode update reads the live hidden Price without forcing zero. Hidden price controls lack currency classes and retain values such as `1.23456`; they must not be rounded merely because visible currency fields are refreshed.

`nativeCurrencyValue` shares numeric-input sanitation with the quantity helper, then applies English, ungrouped Intl formatting with minimum and maximum decimals both set to input-price precision. Main Price is normalized only at the source's global-refresh phases. Purchase's shopping validation/auto-submit remains before that refresh; barcode group conversion, hint refresh and validation precede formatting, while ScanModeSubmit(false) follows it. A barcode can therefore show Amount `2.68`, hidden amount `2.67` and Price `2.68`, then book amount `2.67` at total-derived price `1.00`. Inventory normalizes after its initial selected-price write and after its later quantity/global phases, preserving conversion snapshots and avoiding new estimate requests. Typed prices remain raw until a source-defined formatter call; the separate optional auto-decimal blur rule is unchanged.

Hints now preserve the relevant source event snapshots. Purchase captures both the pre-format price and amount, so price `2.6751` and amount `2.675` can retain hint `1.0000` after both visible fields become `2.68`. A later actual Price keyup or radio change refreshes using current values, producing `2.0000` for Price `5.36` and Amount `2.68`. Inventory has no Price-only hint handler: its generic Price keyup validates, while selection, amount-group/QU events and estimate responses refresh the hint. Its pre-format hint can consequently remain `2.4691` while visible Price becomes `1.23`, and remain unchanged through a later Price edit until a genuine unit or estimate refresh. This corrects an initially overgeneralized Purchase-to-Inventory hint assumption.

The source's formatter also visits currency Userfields, independently of the main Price feature flag. `UserfieldsEditor.refreshCurrencyValues` updates only number-currency values in its owning document, preserving the complete edit and dirty/file flags and skipping unchanged or retired controls. Both stock callers invoke it at the same global-refresh phases. Fee `9.1234` becomes `9.12` at price precision two, and the actual later metadata save sends `9.12`; decimal and integral fields remain unchanged. The source currency Userfield's own step remains four decimals. Updating the controlled model avoids a DOM-only change being lost at rerender or saving a stale FieldEdit. Logical children do not format a parent's fields, and the new method does not manufacture user change events or new dirty flags.

Its reduced DOM, queued API and component/Userfields-save boundaries are declared; auxiliary Fee DOM observations do not themselves prove dirty-state or persistence behavior.

### Inventory validity follows native quantity events

### Native Recipe editor code actions

The selected Recipe editor now uses the native `/recipe/{id}/grocycode?size=60` authenticated preview and a plain native Download anchor. The feature-gated print anchor uses the existing native label-print owner. Each click independently requests a label while the unfinished recipe draft and other actions remain usable. Returned snapshots dispatch their own printer payload with the configured extra data, including when label reads complete out of order. ### Native Recipe table actions and print ownership

The Recipe table now retains Edit beside one native More menu. Its item order is Add to meal plan, Delete, Copy, a separator, native Grocycode Download and feature-gated label printing. Download is an ordinary `/recipe/{id}/grocycode?download=true` anchor with the configured router/script prefix exactly once. The table no longer sends these actions through the additional Grocycode popover. Gallery and reader controls retain their separate additional-workflow behavior.

The print owner belongs to the overview document, rather than a filtered row or open menu. Each click starts its own label read; filtering the row out does not abort those reads or change their captured recipe identity. Native Table options Reset replaces the document and retires its requests, preventing an old label response from starting a printer POST. Printing uses the existing native helper's silent label-read failures, generic webhook errors and absence of successful-print receipts. No row action is locked by a pending print.

The menu renders outside the scrolling table, uses the existing native surface tokens and stays within the viewport. Arrow keys, Home/End, Escape, pointer dismissal and native Tab-exit dismissal operate on the menu; a Tab keyup inside the menu retains it, while leaving the menu and trigger dismisses it without stealing focus. The native meal-plan item remains present even when meal-plan navigation is disabled, matching the unconditional controller collections, template modal and delegated handler. Its explicit `nativeMenuItem` capability reuses the existing dialog; additional gallery/reader actions still apply their feature rules.

Existing Copy/Delete/filter/reset assertions are retained through the menu; reader fixtures use the native table entry point before continuing their retained gallery/dialog checks. The Tab-exit assertion is folded into the menu owner. Removing only that dismissal from the otherwise current application fails the intended menu-still-open assertion (`/tmp/root-recipe-native-table-tab-before.log`); that intermediate preimage is explicitly reconstructed, not a claimed chronological capture.

### Native table meal-plan save stages

The Recipe meal-plan provider now retains an explicit opening mode. A native table opening dispatches an immutable body for each Save without a pending field lock or progress label. Each successful request immediately reports success and closes a current native opening, without waiting for background query reads. Relevant meal-plan and recipe caches become stale without automatic refetch; settlement still invalidates them after a sent write's document has retired, since transport cancellation cannot promise server rollback.

The task owner belongs to the native document. Cancel closes the presentation but preserves a pending job; reopening selects the newly clicked recipe/local today while retaining Servings and Section. An older success hides that current native reopening, matching the source's single static modal. A failure after Cancel reports the page-level native generic save error with technical details. Actual Reset/navigation retires the task signal and suppresses late UI effects. A current additional gallery/reader opening deliberately remains open when an earlier native job succeeds; those additional workflows retain their earlier pending lock, inline error and refresh behavior.

Five new routed owners cover independent body snapshots and completion without unrelated reads, hidden-modal generic errors, Cancel/reopen, document transport/cache settlement, and the additional-opening policy. The final overview owner passes 30 cases (`/tmp/grocy-recipe-native-mealplan-mode-after.log`); the initial 29-case owner and editor/reader siblings pass 77 before the last added mode variant (`/tmp/grocy-recipe-native-mealplan-after.log`). The saved-source baseline has two behavioral failures, one independent transport-abortion failure and a passing native Cancel/reopen control (`/tmp/grocy-recipe-native-mealplan-before.log`). Replacement UI/cache assertions already pass the older transport baseline; no fabricated stale-toast bug is claimed. The additional-opening variant independently fails because the old global close removes the additional draft (`/tmp/grocy-recipe-native-mealplan-mode-before.log`).

The 13-check source oracle executes the unmodified opening/Save/Enter tail of pinned `recipes.js`, native XHR Post, validation/generic-error helpers, jQuery/serializeJSON and the actual Bootstrap modal bundle (`/tmp/grocy-recipe-mealplan-source-evidence.json`, `/tmp/grocy-recipe-mealplan-source.log`). It verifies pending usability, independent serializations, immediate success/hide, retained values, actual Bootstrap Cancel, older success hiding a reopened modal, a hidden-modal failure, technical details, the visible-combobox guard and Enter. Reduced DOM, queued HTTP, notification display and date/recipe-picker helpers are declared doubles. At that checkpoint the application retained the source's global combobox-visible guard, but its Recipe select/date input remained simpler than native picker widgets. The subsequent native picker/date increment below addresses those controls; that earlier save-stage oracle did not prove their equivalence.

Actual Chromium verifies three bounded workflows with Recipe enabled and meal-plan navigation disabled. The first real POST commits servings `1.00` with its response held; editing to `2.50` and saving again commits another row and immediately closes the modal while the first reply remains pending. No overview/section/config/settings read becomes a completion prerequisite. A third real save captures original recipe 3/servings `3.25`; Cancel/reopen selects recipe 4 with retained Servings/Section, then the older success hides that current native opening while persisting recipe 3. An independently created no-grant account can read the native page/action but receives a genuine backend 403 for servings `9.75`. Cancel before delivering that response still produces the native generic error and technical details. Its raw HTML response states the missing RECIPES_MEALPLAN permission; no denied row is inserted.

The 150-request HAR and proxy are independently matched to actual actor permissions, exact bodies, created IDs and SQLite's three stored snapshots (`/tmp/grocy-recipe-mealplan-browser-evidence.json`, `/tmp/root-native-recipe-mealplan-independent-verification.json`). The selected positive section was explicitly chosen; reserved-section source ordering is not misclassified as a missing positive-only filter. These browser cases do not exercise picker equivalence, additional-opening interop or Reset retirement; their separate routed controls remain declared. There are no uncaught JavaScript errors. Two manifest requests have HAR status 0. The no-grant phase also exposed four denied additional-shell nutrition-count GETs, separate from the intentional save denial; that observed permission-query issue is addressed separately below, rather than omitted from network results.

### Native shared Table Reset request stages

Reset now removes both cached preference keys immediately and unconditionally dispatches group and state DELETEs with the native empty-object body. They settle independently: only state success can replace the document, so a held or failed group deletion cannot block it. A child table without ownership of its parent's native document retains the local layout-refresh fallback. A state failure leaves the visible living layout intact, with automatic preference persistence suppressed until a subsequent layout action; it does not silently restore the removed preferences.

The Options dialog closes when Reset opens confirmation, and confirmation closes immediately on Yes. Reset adds no pending lock, inline error or Retry control. Deletion failures use the native page generic error only when the actual HTTP status text is nonempty and the original owner is alive. ApiError now retains optional response status-text metadata for that production check without changing existing callers. Empty status text/network failures remain silent at this boundary. Late callbacks cannot reload or report into a replacement, while sent-write settlement still marks user settings stale. Ordinary layout PUT behavior remains unchanged by this increment.

Four new routed Reset cases fail saved source at the missing unconditional group dispatch (`/tmp/grocy-table-reset-snapshot-before-final.log`). The stock-entry/journal owner plus localization, embedded-host and settings siblings pass 116 cases (`/tmp/grocy-table-reset-siblings-final.log`). Earlier assertions requiring stacked Options/confirmation, waiting for group before state, or an inline Reset retry were narrowly migrated to native behavior; foreign-sender, focus, late actor/cache and retained layout/error details remain covered. The ten-check source oracle executes original table Reset/state-save and setting-delete helpers, including held requests, group/state failures, immediate cache removal, empty status text and the native reload invocation (`/tmp/grocy-table-reset-source-evidence.json`, `/tmp/grocy-table-reset-source-notes.md`). Its queued transport/Bootbox/DataTable boundaries and JSDOM's unsupported real navigation are explicit.

Actual Chromium verifies independent real group/state DELETEs with body `{}` while both original 204 responses are held. Confirmation closes immediately, the table/layout remain alive and Reset/OK remain usable. Releasing state alone replaces the table and retires the held group transport before its response is delivered. The negative case deliberately converts an original API 204 state response into delivered 403 `Forbidden`; this is a disclosed transport fault, not a genuine backend rejection. It preserves the same hidden-Note/Location-group layout, reports the native generic deletion error and technical details, and adds no inline Retry/busy lock. A normal Reset retry then replaces it with default columns/groups.

Independent verification matches the 105-request HAR, actual status text, original/delivered fault distinction, response order, layout snapshots and the final actor-1 SQLite settings (`/tmp/grocy-table-reset-browser-evidence.json`, `/tmp/root-native-table-reset-independent-verification.json`). The new document legitimately saves its default state after deletion; unrelated users retain their earlier settings. Stock's seven rows, thirteen log rows and twelve products are unchanged. Three manifest status-0 requests, the retired group request and the one controlled 403 are declared network outcomes. There are no uncaught JavaScript errors. Browser sessions and all owned services for both increments are closed; ports 19021/19023/19025/19031/19033/19035 are independently clear (`/tmp/root-native-mealplan-reset-ports.txt`). The Reset temporary file root is empty, and the meal-plan run did not create its configured file root.

The integrated meal-plan/Reset gate passes all 1,740 frontend tests in 126 files in 156.91 seconds (`/tmp/root-native-mealplan-reset-full-final.log`). Full lint, formatting of 534 files, application/node/test TypeScript and the `/house.hold/` production build pass (`/tmp/root-native-mealplan-reset-check-final.log`, `/tmp/root-native-mealplan-reset-build.log`). No backend source changed or additional full backend run is claimed.

At this checkpoint ordinary table-setting PUTs remained a separate confirmed gap: native updates memory before independent requests, leaves OK/Reset usable and logs failures without rollback or Retry UI, while the earlier shared save mutation differed (`/tmp/grocy-native-table-save-next-gap.md`). The subsequent ordinary-save increment below addresses that policy. Native Recipe combobox/Grocycode input and date shorthand were still incomplete at that checkpoint; the save-stage increment alone did not implement those widgets or establish full Grocy parity.

### Native ordinary table-setting saves

The shared table hook now remembers each changed preference before dispatching its independent PUT. Group changes save before drawing, so a held layout request cannot block grouping. Success has no state callback: older responses cannot backdate remembered values or produce an unsolicited replay. Global and column searches stay stripped from saved state; comparisons still exclude time and child rows. Ordinary PUT failure logs to the console, keeps the local choice and remembered value, and does not add a pending lock, visible error or Retry. OK and Reset remain usable while writes are held. Settings-read errors retain their separate recovery control.

Closing Options preserves the table's requests. Actual document replacement retires its signal and suppresses late error/cache writes; settlement still marks settings stale because an already sent request may have committed. Existing independent Reset DELETE and reload behavior remains intact. The Stock overview snapshot no longer waits for the removed ordinary-save pending state.

The table-entry, summary and Stock overview owners pass 78 cases (`/tmp/grocy-table-save-after.log`). Saved source fails four selected checks: pending controls, reversed replies causing both stale cache and a real extra state PUT, grouping blocked by a held layout save, and the former visible summary Retry policy (`/tmp/grocy-table-save-before.log`). The embedded lifetime case already passes saved source and remains a positive control. These tests do not claim that the former implementation visibly rolled back its column draft.

The 16-check source oracle executes the complete pinned common DataTables script and actual SaveUserSetting/API callbacks with pinned jQuery. Declared DataTable, Bootbox and queued XHR doubles expose memory-before-send, independent requests, group-before-draw, reverse completion, unchanged failed-save suppression, stripped searches, canonical comparison and Reset usability (`/tmp/grocy-table-save-source-evidence.json`, `/tmp/grocy-table-save-source-notes.md`). It does not model database commits or real unload.

The first integrated run passed 1,744 tests and failed two Purchase print-lifetime cases before any purchase was submitted. A focused 25-repeat loop reproduced the failure. Temporary focus/input diagnostics showed Batch receiving focus, then the existing initial requestAnimationFrame moving focus to Amount while typing, leaving Amount invalid and Batch empty. The fixture now waits for that initial Amount focus in both dialog and direct branches; the direct branch already had this wait. No production code, timeouts or lifetime assertions changed. The same 25-repeat loop and all ten owner cases pass afterward, and diagnostics are removed (`/tmp/grocy-purchase-print-lifetime-repeat-before.log`, `/tmp/grocy-purchase-print-lifetime-repeat-after.log`, `/tmp/grocy-purchase-print-lifetime-focus-diagnostic.log`, `/tmp/grocy-purchase-print-lifetime-owner-after.log`).

Chromium exercises two bounded flows. Two real state PUTs and one group PUT commit before their responses are held; releasing B, group, then A preserves the newest layout without an extra save. Options can close and reopen while requests remain held. A third real state PUT commits successfully before an explicit transport fault changes its delivered 204 to 503. The layout remains, the failure is console-only, and a real group-collapse draw does not retry the unchanged value. This is a controlled delivery failure, not a backend rejection. A normal Reset then successfully deletes both preferences and replaces the table with defaults. The 89-request HAR, proxy observations and SQLite snapshots preserve the distinction between commit order and response-release order (`/tmp/grocy-table-save-browser.har`, `/tmp/grocy-table-save-browser-evidence.json`). A separate 58-check verifier correlates raw traffic, exact bodies, DOM captures, actor-1 write credentials and final actor-scoped SQLite settings; foreign users' preferences remain unchanged (`/tmp/grocy-table-save-independent-verification.json`). Intermediate flat SQLite snapshots match the authenticated writes byte-for-byte but do not themselves include actor IDs. Browser proof covers Options close; embedded destruction remains routed proof. Two manifest non-deliveries and the controlled 503 are declared; no uncaught JavaScript error is observed. Stock, stock log and products remain unchanged. Browser sessions and owned services are closed, and ports 19041, 19043 and 19045 are independently clear.

The final integrated run passes all 1,746 frontend tests in 126 files in 156.85 seconds (`/tmp/root-table-save-full-final.log`). Full lint, formatting of 534 files, application/node/test TypeScript and the `/house.hold/` production build pass (`/tmp/root-table-save-check-final.log`, `/tmp/root-table-save-build.log`). Whitespace checks pass. No backend source changed or additional backend run is claimed.

Native Recipe picker/date behavior was the next implementation gap at this checkpoint. A read-only follow-up executes the actual pinned combobox, Moment, Bootstrap, TempusDominus, picker component scripts and full recipes.js in native order: 35 checks across nine fresh realms establish typed/code selection, Enter/menu ordering and first-open/reopen date-cache behavior (`/tmp/grocy-native-recipe-picker-date-source-evidence.json`, `/tmp/grocy-native-recipe-picker-date-source-notes.md`). Queued XHR, reduced DOM, notifications and a fixed local clock are explicit boundaries; this was source evidence for the following implementation, not application or browser parity by itself. Wider family, configuration, translation, device and release gates remain open. Full Grocy parity remains unproven.

### Native Recipe meal-plan picker and date controls

Native table meal-plan openings now use a searchable Recipe combobox and text date widget. Ordinary name lookup clears the selected ID on keyup; typed exact text requires choosing an option. Enter/Tab choose on keyup, while a later valid Enter keydown can save. Actual shown suggestions supply the global Save guard. Name ranking, duplicate-name last-ID selection and exact typed Grocycode blur resolution reuse the shared picker; unknown codes clear the selection. Additional reader/gallery openings retain their select/date controls and their separate save policy.

The native modal remains mounted after its first opening. Hiding releases the modal body lock and restores focus while keeping control state and pending blur/scanner timers alive; actual document retirement clears them. Rapid Cancel/reopen therefore retains the real pending suggestion menu until its existing dismissal callback runs. The narrow optional visibility argument to useModalDialog preserves every existing caller's default behavior. Document-owned independent save snapshots, error notifications, old-success hiding and additional-mode interoperation retain their earlier owner coverage.

Every opening reinitializes the date cache from the prior displayed text, then writes local today without committing it into that cache. This preserves the source's distinction between first open, an unedited reopening and a previously edited day. Shorthand such as `+7d` expands from local today. A rejected date can restore an earlier valid-looking value while retaining the strict custom error; a subsequent valid keyup or calendar hide revalidates it. Native Save reads the live date input, including blur normalization. Configured week start is passed as source-equivalent text, and the week-number flag follows its scalar truth rules. BookingDateInput itself is unchanged.

Native camera results and a document CustomEvent adapter for `Grocy.BarcodeScanned` use the actual focus/blur resolver. The adapter accepts `{barcode, target?}` detail with the native accepted target values; it does not claim to support jQuery's extra positional event arguments. Scanner callbacks use current control values, survive hiding and do not autosubmit. The source's FormFocusDelay is 500 ms during the document's first second, then zero; the earlier research assumption that this depended on viewport was corrected by reading the actual common-script assignment/timer. A hidden-control focusout fallback supplies the source's programmatic blur event where a browser cannot focus a hidden dialog. These are integration boundaries, not physical camera evidence.

The four new routed control cases fail saved pre-edit source at missing native combobox/date behavior (`/tmp/grocy-native-recipe-picker-date-before.log`). The overview, reader, editor and date-widget siblings pass 118 cases; the real-menu/valid-form rapid-reopen guard also passes five repeats (`/tmp/grocy-native-recipe-picker-date-after.log`, `/tmp/grocy-native-recipe-picker-date-menu-stress.log`). The code case includes wrong-target exclusion, a raw-code interval retaining the existing valid selection, delayed resolution without automatic submission and an explicit save of the resolved ID; it does not assert a permanent 500 ms desktop delay. Native fixture migrations retain exact posted IDs and additional select assertions. An initial partial-key test used separate user-event keyboard sessions for keydown/release; one setup session now emits both actual phases without changing their selection/no-POST assertions. No runtime code was changed to accommodate that harness mistake.

### Native Task save stages, permissions and optional Due

Selected `/task/{id}` forms now opt into a document-owned native save coordinator. Each attempt writes the primary task POST/PUT first, then starts dirty custom fields independently in rendered order using the canonical created/selected ID. The last dirty field controls completion; earlier requests do not gate it. No dirty fields complete immediately. Metadata failure remains silent and busy because this source caller supplies no outer error callback. A file's metadata success gates its old-file deletion and live-file upload; file errors report the native generic error while retaining busy state. Primary failure alone unlocks the form and reports generic details; retrying a failed create makes a fresh POST. Native controls do not offer the generic partial-save Retry workflow.

All native form inputs/buttons and the body busy cursor remain locked through successful completion or metadata/file failure. Dirty acknowledgements are deliberately ignored, matching retained source markers. Native file selection uses current picker bytes and a freshly generated save filename. The shared field editor's new dirty-event policy is enabled only by Task: unchanged text keyups/paste/change count as dirty, ordinary preset-list value change alone stays clean until its source click, checklist changes remain dirty, and link-with-title display edits pack the hidden-equivalent JSON on keyup. Generic consumers retain their prior on-change behavior. The unchanged generic task-category recovery owner remains a policy control, not proof of complete native Task-category parity.

The form remains editable without MASTER_DATA_EDIT so the mutation API can return its actual denial. Backend grants are unchanged. A document/form signal is captured before the primary request; departure suppresses later UI, completion and file stages. Every dispatched primary/field settlement marks relevant Task and calendar caches stale, including settlement after departure. Completion does not wait for active background reads or all earlier fields. Embedded completion sends the exact `{Message: "Reload", Payload: null}` bag; current raw embedded-parameter presence determines the outcome. Existing fixed destinations, Save & add another and default/selected values remain covered.

The native optional Due control now initializes its accepted cache from the original selected date and parses on blur. A complete invalid text paste plus keyup remains optional-valid; change/blur restores blank on create or the prior selected date on edit. Click Save reads that normalized live input. Enter keydown before change/blur captures raw invalid text without inventing a required custom error. These are separate source behaviors; typing a long invalid word character by character can instead trigger the existing four-character MMDD shortcut and advance focus. BookingDateInput itself is unchanged. Further optional-date/raw-value, assignee, translated validation, configuration and focus comparisons remain open.

Twelve selected cases fail saved pre-edit source; the retained generic category recovery and raw optional-Due Enter path already pass as two positive controls (`/tmp/grocy-native-task-save-before.log`). The owner covers create/edit dirty DOM order independent of edit order, unchanged Arrow-key dirty input versus untouched omission, canonical bodies/IDs, last callback, silent final metadata failure, real file stages/bytes, no-grant fixture403 recovery, three departure stages/cache settlement and Due click-versus-Enter. One additional routed preset/link owner closes the late dirty-policy proof gap: saved source sends unwanted metadata for change-only edits; current source sends only the primary write until the real click/keyup events, then exact packed values. Its final siblings pass 56 (`/tmp/grocy-native-task-events-before.log`, `/tmp/grocy-native-task-events-after.log`). Scoped test/app TypeScript, lint and formatting pass. Test setup was corrected to respect blank native create-text defaults and whole-text date paste; no production date algorithm changed to accommodate fixture typing.

### Native reference-form saves and primary text serialization

Locations, product groups, stores and task categories now use the native household save coordinator. Each keeps its own object/userfield namespace and primary fields; no Task payload, add-another action or clear/reset behavior is introduced. The primary POST/PUT precedes independent dirty-field writes in rendered order, and the last dirty field owns completion. Metadata failure stays silently busy without partial Retry; file errors report the native generic error while staying busy; primary errors alone unlock a fresh primary attempt. Settled writes mark their actual entity and dependent option/list caches stale without waiting for unrelated active reads. Document retirement suppresses later file stages, errors and completion.

The three conditional routes read live first literal embedded-parameter presence when completion occurs, send exact `{Message: "Reload", Payload: null}`, and otherwise navigate to their fixed native list. Product groups retain unconditional `{Message: "CloseLastModal", Payload: null}`, including standalone pages; no list fallback is invented. Task-category controls now remain editable before a genuine API denial, and imported non1 Active values submit unchecked0. Stock-reference controls were already editable. Existing freezer feature-on checked and feature-off hidden0 behavior remains.

All actual HouseholdObjectEditor consumers now use the native policy, so its unused generic saved-ID/recovery branch and optional permission/body-reader switches are removed. Additional EntityManager/saveUserfields callers retain their separate recovery behavior. Primary bodies read actual named controls in the primary fieldset: single-line inputs use DOM values after CR/LF stripping, main textareas serialize CRLF, and custom multiline metadata retains LF from its own field handler. Task optional Due still reads its live DOM value, preserving blur-restored click submissions and raw Enter-before-blur submissions; Battery single-line Description receives no textarea conversion. NumberPicker and BookingDateInput accept optional names for this real form serialization boundary without changing other callers.

The four complete pinned form scripts, common UserfieldsForm, actual helpers and pinned jQuery serializer execute in18 isolated realms with57 assertions and57 helper requests (`/tmp/grocy-native-reference-family-source-evidence.json`). Root reruns the oracle successfully (`/tmp/root-native-reference-source.log`). Three expected JSDOM unsupported-navigation diagnostics establish only that source list-navigation branches execute, not real unload. The oracle uses template-shaped DOM and queued transports; its controlled403 inputs are not backend permission evidence.

### Native Chore saves, assignment continuation and code actions

The selected Chore editor now uses the native household writer with its own scheduling, assignment and optional stock fields. It sends fresh primary POST/PUT, then independent dirty metadata in rendered order; only the last dirty field starts the assignment-calculation POST. Primary error shows the native generic error, clears cursor and unlocks all form controls, including source-observed previously disabled product, assignment and history-start controls. Assignment error logs to console and clears only the cursor; every form control remains disabled because source EndUiBusy has no form ID. ### Native Chore and Battery tracking save stages

The native `/choretracking` and `/batterytracking` forms now use the source tracking sequence: capture record ID and Chore Skip at the click, fetch fresh details, read live time and Done by at that response, then create an execution or charge cycle. Primary success reads the live custom controls and starts their independent writes in rendered order. Only the last dirty field's own metadata/file chain gates the local receipt and main-picker reset. Earlier fields can still settle or fail afterward. These two pages do not send embedded parent completion messages.

Tracking deliberately never invokes common Userfields Load or Clear. Custom dates remain blank even with a definition default of now; required checkboxes remain plain required controls after checking and unchecking. Successful counts retain dirty controls and selected Files. Fresh file trash has no loaded-file handler, so it leaves that selection intact. Every subsequent count creates its own filename metadata and uploads the retained File again. Other editors keep their existing Load/default and sequential-save policies.

GET and primary POST failures log and unlock for a fresh details read and primary attempt. A last metadata failure stays silent and busy; a file-operation failure shows the native generic error and stays busy. There is no committed-row Retry interface. Save remains available without a write grant, allowing the backend to reject the actual request. Undo retains the real execution/charge ID and native POST endpoint, logs failures, and emits only its local success text.

The Chore selection object read is independent and unversioned. Its response installs the date format and manual-Skip CSS even if a newer selection has already responded. Date format survives clearing the main picker. Manual Skip uses pointer-disabled styling rather than a disabled attribute, preserving the source's separate keyboard/script behavior. At this checkpoint main controls still used the existing datalist and browser date adapters. The main-date increment below supplies the actual Tempus/date-only reset behavior; complete combobox/scanner behavior and same-ID selection events remain separate work.

Details, primary writes, custom-field chains and Undo belong to document and form lifetimes. Every sent write marks actual card, overview, journal, calendar and affected stock/recipe/shopping readers stale when it settles, including uncertain replies after retirement. Native completion does not await active reader refetches; the existing generic invalidation helper remains available to other household workflows.
