### Native selected-editor asynchronous custom-field loading

EquipmentEditor, HouseholdObjectEditor, QuantityUnitObjectForm and UserobjectEditor now render their selected primary form before the custom-value GET finishes. These four implementations serve ten native route families: Equipment; Battery, Task, Task category, Location, Product group and Store; Quantity unit and Conversion; and Userobject. Primary-object/context/definition bootstrap still supplies the data previously rendered by PHP. Invalid primary objects retain their normal error behavior and never become create forms. A failed custom-value read logs once and leaves the same usable form; it no longer replaces the editor with an ErrorBlock and Retry button.

Selected custom controls start blank with ordinary required checkboxes, then apply the first fresh Load response once per document. A warm cached value does not consume that callback while its fresh request is pending. Later query refetches do not overwrite the initialized draft. Only returned keys apply: loaded text overwrites a local edit while keeping its dirty marker; checkbox0 preserves a live check and removes required, while omitted checkbox keys stay required; falsy checklist/file/link values preserve local state. Truthy files install stored-file bindings while retaining any live selected File. Household and Quantity reuse the existing object-or-PHP-array value parser. Other save, manual, plural-form and native completion coordinators remain intact.

Read signals combine the query and native document lifetimes, and the shared value reader rejects an already-retired response before parsing it. Load callbacks also retain their originating document owner. Same-provider Strict Mode setup replay can acquire its replacement signal; an actually replaced document cannot apply or log an old Load. Equipment's fresh definition latch and Userobject's fresh namespace-context latch remain independent of late custom-value loading.

Fresh execution of the unchanged pinned common script and all ten complete host scripts passes 64 checks (`/tmp/native-editor-load-oracle.json`; root rerun `/tmp/native-editor-load-oracle-root.log`). The probe uses the actual vendored jQuery 3.7.1 in JSDOM with a reduced template-like DOM and queued API callbacks. Ordinary text/checkbox dirty classes are seeded directly; packed-link keyup executes the actual source handler. Plugin rendering, validation and timers are facades; this is not PHP/Blade rendering, full browser-plugin execution or a host Save oracle. It verifies selected Load, not the separate create-definition callback.

That execution also settles raw callback progression. Partial packed links clear missing visible parts because the actual one-argument val(undefined) is a setter here. Packed JSON primitives/arrays clear both parts, retain the exact hidden payload and continue later keys; packed null or malformed JSON stops after already-applied keys. Malformed packed File data likewise stops later keys: both base64 components must decode, although an explicit empty display component is valid. The shared handle now preserves those boundaries instead of rejecting every non-object link or silently accepting malformed file encoding. Generic file-display fallback parsing remains separate.

### Remaining native selected loaders and Barcode one-shot initialization

Strict Mode setup replay gets a fresh local owner. No definitions means no value request.

### Native create-editor asynchronous custom-field initialization

All 19 inventoried create-capable native hosts now begin with blank custom values and run the independent filtered-definition Load callback. This includes all six HouseholdObjectEditor routes and Purchase/Inventory; shared wrappers represent multiple routes in the initialization inventory. The exact host set is Product, Chore, Battery, Task, Task category, Location, Product group, Store, Equipment, Quantity unit, Quantity-unit conversion, Recipe, Shopping list, Shopping item, User, Barcode, Userobject, Purchase and Inventory. The initial create/selected branch is captured for the document lifetime; obtaining a created or booking identity does not start a selected read. Purchase and Inventory keep their separate Clear requests and existing Save/receipt coordinators. The two tracking hosts still never call Load or Clear, Stock entry remains selected-only, and additional generic editors retain synchronous defaults.

The create callback applies returned definitions to currently rendered payload controls in response order. Only date/datetime `now` defaults assign callback-time values; configured ordinary text defaults are ignored. A returned required checkbox becomes indeterminate while preserving its actual checked state. Its acknowledgement listener is installed at callback time, so earlier edits do not acknowledge it; a later natural change removes required and retains the browser's indeterminate state. Dirty markers and live File selections survive initialization. Definitions absent from the response do not acquire callback policy, and new names do not create controls. No stored-file or original/delete bindings are installed on create.

Per-control handling also preserves less common DOM effects. A scripted checkbox change can retain indeterminate; a response date definition targeting a rendered single preset can leave no selected option, whose native metadata value is null. Both the common writer and Barcode's separate writer retain that null. Packed-link payload assignment does not rewrite its visible title/link parts. Definition parsing happens per row, allowing earlier controls to change before a later malformed row throws. The parser still requires an outer JSON array and typed string names; this is a recorded raw-response boundary, not a claim that the real API emits malformed definitions.

### Native custom Date/Datetime text, locale and picker runtime

All 22 inventoried native custom-field hosts now use text Date/Datetime controls and the pinned DateTimePicker2/Tempus runtime. The 19 create hosts above, selected-only Stock entry, and both tracking hosts share the capability. Tracking still never calls common Load/Clear. Generic EntityManager, ShoppingItemForm and StockEntryDialog keep their existing HTML date controls and adapters. Native snapshots and both metadata writers preserve raw text, including localized digits, spaces, seconds and optional invalid strings; they do not run the old datetime-local T/seconds conversion. Create callback-time now uses the document's actual Moment locale. Selected Load and common Clear remain silent text assignments that retain existing custom validity and widget cache.

The runtime is pinned to Grocy `21aa5eab218c8466f90729154cb293fffbe10c21`: jQuery 3.7.1, Moment 2.30.1 with locales, Bootstrap 4.6.2 and Tempus package 5.39.2, whose unchanged source banner says 5.39.0. Static ESM factories give each form private jQuery/Moment, selector scope and event ownership without eval, shared global assignments, dynamic script loaders or runtime network dependencies. Four npm archives were SHA512-verified against the pinned lock. Raw source/license hashes, provenance, reproducible generation, and MIT texts are retained in `frontend/public/vendor/native-userfield-date/`. Generation verifies twelve pinned inputs and sixteen runtime assets; SHA256SUMS also includes the asset manifest. Only obsolete standalone source-map directives are omitted from generated wrappers; executable source bytes and raw input files are preserved. Scoped styling and the existing SVG icon system supply the popup presentation without global Bootstrap CSS.

Actual source behavior includes collection-level effects. Purchase/Inventory's configured purchased-date control and native Stock entry's stored purchased date now participate before custom controls in the same form. The source's first main DateTimePicker2 supplies defaultDate to every member: when the purchased-date setting is enabled, custom Date and Datetime can already contain the main date and midnight before their Load callback. Stock's stored main date has the same effect. Thus earlier blanket blank-initial-date descriptions apply to forms without that first main initializer; they do not establish the enabled purchased-date configuration. Main values and widget models survive common custom Clear. Main Due-date DateTimePicker1 controls and their existing Clear/script bindings remain separate.

The unchanged script retains MMDD/YYYYMMDD/end-of-month/relative offsets, arrow modifiers, calendar/month/year/decade and hour/minute/second actions, source blur behavior, and the first-control validation quirk. A relative offset can write the entire DateTimePicker2 collection; a datetime-origin keyup can validate the first date or main purchased control against the datetime format. Popup close emits input/change/keypress/keyup for all collection members and can dirty untouched siblings. Load/Clear do not recompute contextual help or reset plugin cache. Only the first contextual time receives the first input's value, including source ASCII Never/Unknown and localized Today comparisons. Purchase alone triggers its main initial helper. The input deliberately retains `datetimepicker2-input`, rather than adding the plugin's different delegated `datetimepicker-input` class that would introduce extra normalization.

Configured week numbers use the source's PHP-compatible truthiness, including string `0`. The source layout's quoted first-day setting updates only the owning private Moment locale. Calendar popups expose named dialogs, localized day names, keyboard actions and disabled states. Cleanup mutes source handlers before destroying widgets, including individual conditional participants, so hide cannot dirty a retired draft; form retirement removes private delegated/resize handlers and popups. DOM identity, rather than a repeated input ID, controls participant reinitialization. An independent implementation review found and verified repairs for individual-popup cleanup and string-zero week configuration (`/tmp/native-userfield-date-coordinator-review.md`, independent review messages).

The final date owner passes 39 cases, together with 34 preserved create cases. Its original 37 cases against preserved pre-edit production fail 36 and pass the actual generic-editor positive (`/tmp/native-userfield-dates-final.log`, `/tmp/native-userfield-dates-before.log`). Four Tamil create/selected Date/Datetime routes exercise held initial requests, same controls, dirty retention and exact metadata writes. Other cases cover required seconds, optional raw values, source mixed-field validity, actual translated calendar/retirement, compact 22-host capability wiring, all three main-first default/relative interactions, shortcuts and generic HTML/wire behavior. ### Native Chore and Battery record pickers

The native tracking record controls now use a shared HouseholdRecordPicker instead of HTML datalists. Typing a prefix and leaving the input retains uncommitted text without selecting the first partial name. The actual source ranking is prefix, case-sensitive substring, then folded substring, retaining input order within each group and the last-ID map for identical display names. Explicit pointer or Enter/Tab keyup commits the active option. Open-menu keydown prevents a premature booking; selected, closed-menu Enter reaches the parent's input-only checkValidity and primary button click. Invalid Enter does not report/focus invalid fields. Textarea Enter remains separate.

Input/change-only assignment and ordinary keyup are distinct. The former can retain the prior selected ID; ordinary keyup clears source/serialized IDs before lookup. Plain unchanged selected-name blur does not refresh the card or reset time. Valid raw Grocycode blur emits an explicit selected-change event, including the same ID: Chore starts its independent object read before card refresh and applies date format/current time at that callback; Battery refreshes its card without changing time. Raw code kind and exact ID spelling follow the source, including wrong-kind/unknown clearing and the case-sensitive grcy prefix. Existing active-only SQLite name ordering, independent earlier Chore selection replies, source Date1 behavior, custom-field stages and Undo remain intact.

The source select ID, hidden serialized target and plugin selection cache are separate. Actual successful booking code blanks source ID/text without clearing the cached selection or previous hidden target. First toggle then clears cache/target and stays closed; second opens choices. A production ResetAfterBooking/GetValue capability preserves this unconditional raw reset and the source distinction between current-ID receipt reads and serialized-ID booking snapshots. Tracking still never calls common Userfields.Load/Clear; dirty fields and Files retain their existing policy.

The picker supports the source document barcode protocol and existing CameraScanner decoder. Matching target, omitted target, literal string `"undefined"` and null are accepted; other targets are ignored. Delayed focus/blur reads current live text rather than a captured barcode. Programmatic source events still operate during a real pending booking: disabled controls remain disabled, while a document scan or dispatched keyup can change the live selection without changing the booking's captured target. The original 500ms startup focus delay becomes zero after the first second; scheduled work retains its captured delay. Native time focus selects its text. Document retirement removes listeners/timers and suppresses obsolete effects.

Two source quirks required explicit review repairs. A focusout event does not itself run the direct blur handler; resolution belongs after actual focus and blur, with one fallback for controls that cannot receive native focus. Scroll-safety mousedown on the UL detaches both plugin and host code-blur listeners; hiding restores only plugin blur, so later code blurs remain inert for that document. A visible menu is retained during busy state, and its source option selection remains active even while form controls are disabled. Toggle/camera/input controls retain native disabled behavior. Named combobox/listbox options, highlighting and existing picker styling/icons supply the application UI without new vendor or global style dependencies.

### Native Chore and Battery tracking Cards

Native tracking now uses an independent Card reader and shared NativeTrackingCard view. Each invocation makes its own literal raw-ID request, including repeated IDs and the empty trailing path. The displayed Card has its own observed query-cache entry, separate from current picker selection. Replies apply in completion order: an earlier selected record can overwrite a later record's Card while that later record remains the booking target. Successful booking refresh reads the live source ID before reset. Only document/component retirement suppresses obsolete requests and callbacks; a subsequent selection does not cancel an earlier Card read. Existing booking snapshots, metadata, custom fields, receipt and Undo remain intact.

Genuine HTTP failures log and retain the previous entire Card rather than replacing it with a retry panel. Successful-response decoder/callback failures reach the browser's global error channel through its own microtask. Successful non-JSON responses use that same channel. Typed schema failures produce ZodError rather than the original unchecked TypeError; this explicit implementation boundary does not claim identical exception classes. The source's empty-ID request is preserved rather than silently skipped or replaced with the booking ID. Prefix invalidation marks the actually displayed historical cache stale after uncertain writes, without an unused legacy numeric-ID cache.

Initial overview headings and named blank fields are visible, including pointer-disabled but keyboard-focusable `href="#"` actions. After a response, names, count, timestamps and links use that response's record. Edit remains a plain enabled native link independent of the parent's mutation grant; the backend remains authoritative. Journal opens the embedded native page using the bare `?embedded&chore=id` or Battery equivalent. Chore Description applies the exact pinned `nl2br` expression and then sanitizes the complete result with DOMPurify; allowed rich HTML remains, while active script/attributes are deliberately excluded. Battery Used in stays plain text. No additional formatted-content request is needed.

Raw datetime attributes and contextual relative text survive date-only display. Null last-time uses an empty raw timestamp and a separate translated Never label. This fixes the inherited bug where a long Tamil Never label was parsed/truncated as a date and emitted a Moment warning. Initial blanks remain distinct from post-response zero/Unknown/Never. The inherited `toLocaleString` count formatting also differed from actual source execution: late asynchronous Card counts remain raw after generic initialization. Both Chore `1234` and Battery `5678` now display without grouping.

### Strict native API trailing paths under configured mounts

Legacy API paths now retain strict native slash routing rather than acquiring FastAPI's automatic alternate-slash alias. The middleware first preserves every actual full or method-partial route match, including the existing Python file path converter. Only an unmatched native path that would otherwise redirect to an alternate slash shape is sent through the existing compatibility error policy. Native HEAD still uses the existing GET/body-stripping wrapper; OPTIONS keeps its prior shortcut. Health and modern v2 retain their existing redirect behavior. This is one production-file change adding 24/removing one line, without changing nginx, frontend application code, global router configuration, accepted front-controller aliases or authentication.

The original trailing Chore/Battery collection and detail paths do not resolve to the corresponding slashless endpoints. The pinned OPTIONS wildcard makes their other methods routing errors with allowed OPTIONS. Root errors use JSON 405 and `Method not allowed. Must be one of: OPTIONS`. The source ExceptionController tests the full URI for `/api/`; a configured URI prefix instead selects its errors/500 render boundary and status 500. Python preserves its existing minimal HTML error presentation. No byte-for-byte original error-page rendering is claimed, and no redirect is added to normalize these malformed native URLs.

This distinction is executed against the actual pinned archive routes, Slim 4.14/FastRoute 1.3, PSR URI factory, RoutingMiddleware, ExceptionController API branch and outer CorsMiddleware using ephemeral PHP 8.4.26. The probe records 288 observations over four mount shapes and passes 37 assertions (`/tmp/native-prefix-php-probe.json`, `/tmp/native-prefix-php-probe-checks.json`). There is no PHP SAPI, authentication/locale middleware, actual template HTML, database mutation or full differential-suite claim. Source file/barcode variables use default single segments, not a path wildcard; the Python converter's retained slash behavior is an existing implementation boundary, not newly established source parity.

### Native file route segments and encoded identities

The four native API file decorators now use a nonempty default filename segment, matching the pinned source rather than consuming arbitrary extra path text. Literal or singly encoded base64 slashes fail during routing. Doubly encoded slashes remain legitimate data: the first whole-URI decode leaves `%2F` within a single segment; captured-argument decoding then restores `/` as part of the base64 filename. Shared normalization and both decoding passes remain unchanged. A redundant raw-path guard is also removed: its literal search for `/api/files/` wrongly rejected source-valid encoded static route text such as `/api/%66iles/` and `/%61pi/files/`, even after the real router had matched the correct endpoint. Only files.py changes in production, adding five/removing nine lines; the file service, named download route, v2, authentication, proxy config and frontend are unchanged.

Malformed native file routes now use the existing strict method policy: root JSON 405 with only OPTIONS allowed; URI-prefixed method failures use the source's 500 error-render branch. A valid matched route with a missing or invalid file remains a different stage, returning 404. In particular, prefixed matched missing files use HTML 404, not JSON and not the routing 500 branch. Native HEAD retains status/headers with an empty body; OPTIONS still returns 200. Existing ordinary tokens, literal/singly/doubly encoded padding, named-file links and modern routes retain their policies. The source's second argument decode occurs in RoutingResults.getRouteArguments; Route.getArguments exposes that already prepared result.

### Native table custom-file links, thumbnails and image dialogs

Native custom-column renderers now use the complete stored packed binding for named-file links. Files are ordinary `_blank` anchors; images are named 32-by-32 best-fit thumbnails with the source title, alt and lazy attributes, inside `show-as-dialog-link` anchors. Primary clicks, including Control-click, open the existing topmost native form dialog at the named picture URL. Nonprimary click handling and normal file navigation follow the source. Empty, null and missing values stay blank; string `0` remains plain text. The default modern component retains its authenticated internal-name API/blob workflow.

A separate native table display decoder follows PHP's permissive base64 and UTF-8 HTML behavior, reading only the second packed token. It handles invalid internal metadata independently of the display label, ignored suffixes, noisy/padded display data, invalid UTF-8, BOM, normalized CRs and contextual NUL handling. The editor/global modern decoder is unchanged. Image group toggle labels use the same native attribute display. The original Blade renderer supplies fifteen concrete cases; the current helper matches all twenty-six nonempty text/attribute comparisons, with zero/empty guarded separately.

Table metadata now retains the complete named anchor HTML and actual configured full URL. File search sees the encoded internal identity and configured URL; image titles and alt text do not become searchable. Sorting and RowGroup identity use full cell HTML, including the source image whitespace. The actual pinned DataTables 1.13.11, Chinese-string plugin 1.13.6 and RowGroup 1.5.1 execute thirteen source checks; twenty-six independent current-helper assertions match normalized HTML, search, order identity and guards. These source probes use real Blade and locked runtime code but explicitly facade modal/topmost/state and omit layout and real network behavior.

### Native Equipment download links and retained previews

Equipment's cookie-session view now uses the source's real `target="_blank"` Download anchors and direct PDF-declared embeds. Non-401 identity errors render before configuration/localization helpers or domain pages mount.

### Native account controls and authenticated logout

Resolved browser transport now supplies the native account controls, whether selected by native Login or discovered automatically. The menu uses the actual username and a user icon or the source-style named user-picture URL with UTF-8 PHP base64 and 32-pixel best-fit options. Nonsquare photos keep their natural aspect ratio. Ordinary Logout and user-editor links retain configured public roots/front controllers and browser modifier behavior. The inline rail disclosure supports arrows, Escape/focus restoration, outside dismissal and Tab-away; opening scrolls its links into view on short screens.

PHP-truthy `DISABLE_AUTH` hides the entire native account control. Exact dev/demo/prerelease modes with false disabled-auth remain visible and offer Change password. Normal built-in LDAP/proxy authentication instead offers Edit user. Backend user-form/overview flags now apply the same bypass rule, so the existing native editor enables its password controls for those bypass-mode links. No edit-self/admin grants are added. Explicit Connection and key-fallback modes retain their additional modern Sign out action. Embedded URI handling stays separate; the original `embedded.txt` distribution mode remains unsupported rather than being inferred from a query parameter.

An actual cookie may authenticate a browser without the frontend cookie-selection flag. Native logout removes that session but retains its stale cookie; a new document must not turn its failed browser identity into the owner of an implicit development key. The public V2 login context now reports only a strict `browser_session_supplied` boolean, parsed from the same raw Cookie headers as native auth. Initial automatic identity failure checks that readable context even when the original native redirect is opaque. A supplied cookie pins browser transport; missing, malformed or unavailable metadata fails closed. A known cookie-free key-only deployment retains its original fallback. The signal discloses no credential or actor and does not itself authenticate anyone; legacy identity/logout responses and credential precedence remain intact.

The strict routed owners also found a transient protected-Outlet mount during Login navigation. Auth-page allowance now follows the committed route matches, preventing a new Login URL from exposing the previous Equipment page. Native cookie and unknown-hint cases retain their no-key/no-grants/no-Equipment guards. Exact old-source and old-resolver comparisons, real native session workflows and mode-specific API contexts provide separate causal evidence; their overlapping results are not added together.

The first actual image run exposed a second lifecycle defect that fast mocks missed: one modern Sign out click sent two POSTs and revoked both its disposable control key and the independent bootstrap key. ### Native Chore Reschedule callback lifecycle

Reschedule now owns one persistent form for the native page. Each explicit row selection starts an independent details read; only a successful callback fills the actual Date1/UserPicker controls and opens the modal. Genuine read failure stays silent, without a loading/error/retry modal. Cancel or Escape hides the retained controls without aborting their requests. Older live reads can refill and reopen that same form; repeated IDs are not deduplicated. Real page/table retirement suppresses late callbacks.

Save and Reset remain editable and independently submit while earlier writes are pending. Save validates the native form; Reset bypasses validation and sends null overrides without clearing the draft. Each PUT captures its click-time date, user and target ID. Its successful callback recalculates the **live selected ID**, including the source quirk where A's saved override can be followed by B's calculation before B's details reply. The calculation body uses the source's string ID. Accepted calculation requests the existing full native document reload, even when the form has been hidden; the existing non-document fallback is retained.

The three request stages have distinct policies: details HTTP failure is silent; PUT failure shows the generic source notice and logs its raw response; calculation failure logs the original request error and leaves the form/draft intact without a reload. No additional inline ErrorBlock, automatic retry or busy lock is introduced. A successful PUT remains a partial side effect when calculation fails.

The two native write helpers opt into `requestNativeJson`: only 200/204 succeeds, every 200 body is parsed irrespective of Content-Type, and malformed or empty successful JSON cannot advance to calculation/reload. Existing default API callers and the chore editor's calculation helper keep their behavior. Successful decode/application exceptions use a captured-owner browser error callback; typed adapter exception text/classes are not claimed identical to PHP/jQuery. Valid JSON200 and ordinary204 writes have retained routed positive coverage.

### Native Chore and Battery journal Undo callbacks

Each Undo click now starts an independent native POST with the literal empty-object body. Other rows remain usable while a request is pending. A successful callback mutates its captured row, uses the browser callback clock for the displayed timestamp and shows the native success receipt. Records initially loaded as undone remain disabled in both journals.

The journal-only transport uses the existing strict native JSON policy: only 200/204 succeeds, every 200 body is parsed, and malformed successful JSON reaches the guarded browser error channel before a display update or receipt. Shared modern tracking callers keep their existing helper. Settled sent writes mark related caches stale without an active journal GET, draw or parent refresh. Page/table retirement suppresses obsolete UI and logs without aborting an already dispatched write or losing its later cache invalidation.

The table retains its initial search, ordering and grouping values. Relative-date refresh visits only currently attached, visible cells of the clicked row. A hidden name column receives no new marker, while the Battery action can still become disabled; hidden date cells retain their earlier text when shown again. Local search can detach a row without retiring its request: an accepted callback still mutes and strikes that row, but its new marker has no relative text and its old date text remains cached until a later explicit refresh. Column reorder restores cached cell descendants and controls while retaining row muting. An older button callback after reorder may refresh the live row's dates and show its receipt, but cannot add a marker or disable the replacement button, or erase a newer row's accepted state.

### Native Tasks Delete confirmation and row lifecycle

Delete now captures the selected task and dismisses only its confirmation immediately on Yes. No sends nothing. Each later confirmation/request is independent; an older completion cannot dismiss or answer a newer dialog. Native DELETE sends the literal empty object through a strict native-only reader: only 200/204 succeeds, every 200 is parsed, and malformed successful JSON stops through the captured-owner browser error channel. Shared modern object deletion remains unchanged. Genuine request failure logs the original error quietly, leaving rows, filters, statistics and unrelated action busy state intact without a Delete error panel, retry or rollback.

The Delete entrypoint is visible for every row, as in the pinned template, while the backend still enforces `MASTER_DATA_EDIT`. The denied-reader owner now exercises the real 403 instead of asserting that Delete is absent. Add/Edit and Complete/Undo keep their separate grants and existing behavior. Current accepted Delete retains its existing cache invalidation; failed or retired sent writes mark relevant readers stale without active refetch as an explicit uncertain-write freshness policy. Real page/table retirement suppresses obsolete UI and logs without aborting a sent write.

Successful Delete binds to the live row at callback time and starts the source’s 500 ms fade, shortened for reduced motion/print. Removal waits for actual `animationend`; there is no timer/no-event fallback. Completion queries the current row ID again and removes it in the current table draw. A row filtered out before the reply receives no listener. An animation ending on a detached row consumes its listener but cannot remove an absent live row. Pending logical row listeners survive React row recreation; genuine reload/unmount retires them. Cached data is not deleted or refetched: an explicit later draw may resurrect the row. A keyed null-rendering slot keeps a deleted row's group heading present until that draw, including an otherwise empty singleton group.

The original tasks.js/core Delete/real Bootbox/Bootstrap/DataTables probe passes **35 checks across seven cases**. A separate actual RowGroup supplement passes **five checks in one case**, confirming that the same orphan heading remains after animation-end row removal and that a subsequent real draw rebuilds groups and resurrects cached data. Queued XHR and controlled animation events establish source callback/selector ownership, not physical browser event emission or SQL persistence (`/tmp/native-task-delete-source-report.md`, `/tmp/native-task-delete-source-group-report.md`). The initial Bootbox harness used a nonexistent DOM data attribute rather than its actual button classes; that diagnostic remains preserved.

The final Tasks page, Task editor and Household journal run passes **73 tests across three owners** in 9.92 seconds, including 25 Tasks cases. Exact original source fails seven intended guards with 18 controls passing. The A/B owner reaches the actual older-response dismissal bug before duplicate body guards; its natural No closes a still-open old confirmation only on the original implementation. Backend403 and genuine missing-row400 are enforced by the fixture, and unrelated Complete busy ownership remains protected. Retired failed-write stale invalidation is explicitly a freshness-policy regression rather than a measured source cache defect.

A narrow pre-group component variant fails the singleton-heading guard after already reaching literal body, immediate dismissal, accepted fixture deletion, row retention until animationEnd and physical selected-row removal. The existing primary owner now protects the same connected group heading and cached redraw resurrection without adding a duplicate owner. Both original/current and narrow variants run in isolated preserved source copies, with no live production swap. Final scoped lint, formatting and test TypeScript pass; early runs/reviews before the heading correction remain separate evidence (`/tmp/native-task-delete-test-handoff.md`).

The actual release browser creates no records. Two separate normal setup creates supply disposable Tasks A/B with date-only overdue dates; the original task stays untouched. No on A sends nothing. One external DELETE removes B; its cached row's Yes sends genuine DELETE 400 with literal body{}, logs once and retains rows/statistics/filters/document. A's Yes sends genuine empty 204 with body{}; its trusted animationend reports `elapsedTime` 0.5 s and the captured row disconnects. Both confirmation DOM removals are measured before their authentic responseEnd timestamps. The page keeps its two initial overview/statistics reads, with no Complete/Undo/stock operation, reload, relay or mutation replay (`/tmp/native-task-delete-qa-report.md`).

Actual screenshot phases stay distinct. Full confirmation has settled 1,440/390/320 captures; the accepted two-row state has desktop evidence. Its initial geometry guard incorrectly included a CSS-hidden Filter button; the diagnostic is preserved. A broad optional Clear locator then clicked the overdue counter instead, producing a separate read-only draw with two cached overdue rows. The first exact-name Clear subsequently restored all three cached rows and has final three-width captures. These final mobile captures are redraw-state evidence, not accepted-two-row mobile evidence. Root inspects seven images; visible prompts, actions and counters fit, while lower mobile rows may require vertical scrolling.

The HAR records **65 requests: 63 GETs and exactly two DELETEs**,400 then204. Setup creates and the external deletion are recorded separately. Failed 400 leaves the post-external database unchanged; accepted 204 removes only its selected fixture. The owned profile, containers, network and helpers close; images/data remain, and no engine command follows pause cleanup.

The independent current-run audit passes **25 substantive groups with zero errors**, opening ten raw SQLite backups and reconstructing all 44 tables and 183 schema records. ### Native system pages and page metadata

`/about` now provides the native System info and Changelog tabs. Only the first raw `tab=changelog` selects the latter initially; clicking tabs does not rewrite the URL. Release notes sort by integer release number, and releases at or above `newest_release_number - 4` start expanded. Each release retains its own collapse state across tab changes. The system table shows the pinned Grocy version/date, database, SQLite, OS and client values. PHP Version remains blank; an additional Python Version row reports the actual runtime. The authenticated `/api/v2/system/about` context provides these values and prepared changelog HTML without requiring domain grants. Direct, embedded and configured-prefix frontend routes have DOM coverage. Malformed tab escapes retain the default System info view, which the source has already rendered before its script fails.

The committed changelog is generated from Grocy 4.5.0 with its exact pinned Parsedown implementation through `scripts/generate-system-resources.py`; generation records source/output hashes and attribution. Runtime `nh3` sanitization prepares the displayed HTML while retaining standard Markdown headings, links and code blocks. PHP is needed only when regenerating that resource, not in deployed images. About has 12 owner cases and six localization/description siblings passing in their combined 18-case run; backend authentication, actual bundled releases and safe HTML are covered separately through the real API.

`/barcodescannertesting` now provides the native expected-barcode and scan fields, local Hit/Miss counters and newest-first scan history. Raw prefills, exact whitespace/case matching, keyup-dependent control enabling, Enter/blur duplicates and the initial delayed-to-immediate clear behavior follow the pinned script. Keyboard diagnostics perform no stock booking and remain available when browser camera scanning is disabled. The camera path uses the existing scanner and releases its stream on completion/departure. Nine diagnostic owner cases and six shared camera cases pass; these use DOM/media fixtures and do not establish physical camera or torch behavior.

Authenticated pages now maintain their native document title and a credentialed manifest link for the current page. Unicode titles and the raw current URL, including duplicate parameters and bare flags, are encoded for `/manifest`; route changes update the metadata, and login removes it. The backend manifest preserves native query registration, permissive base64 decoding, title/start-URL separator behavior, icon/theme/display fields and malformed-input behavior. GET and HEAD work under literal configured prefixes, with authentication redirects for unauthenticated native-resource requests and matching HEAD length/empty-body behavior. Shared navigation, styles, head handling and development proxy routing now include the native system resources.

`/api` now provides the native standalone REST API browser using the unchanged pinned Swagger UI 5.20.2 bundles, standalone preset and CSS. An isolated iframe owns its styles, runtime and abort scope. The native deep links, authorization, operation expansion, Try it out and response display are available, while the source-hidden topbar, server controls and model pane remain hidden and the external validator is disabled. The connection key is sent to the specification request through an origin/source-checked message; operation requests use Swagger’s explicit Authorize value. Mutation settlement and iframe retirement mark parent queries stale, including uncertain writes during departure. Four owner cases execute the actual bundled runtime in an isolated JSDOM realm with platform/static-file/fetch transports; they cover authenticated specification loading, authorization and GET responses, a JSON POST followed by reading its persisted fixture state, parent deep links/cache invalidation, denied specification rendering and held-request departure. The combined key/auth sibling run passed 27 cases. These runtime DOM cases are supplemented by the disposable real-browser verification recorded with the native reload increment above.

Nine backend system-page/manifest cases passed (`/tmp/root-native-system-api-final.log`). The page-metadata owner plus 16 native-settings and four feature siblings passed 21 cases (`/tmp/root-system-metadata-siblings.log`). These selected results overlap other frontend totals. The integrated preference/system run passed 74 cases in eight files, and the final system/backend sibling run passed 12 cases (`/tmp/root-native-system-backend-siblings-final.log`). The metadata regression first failed without the authenticated metadata owner because no credentialed manifest existed (`/tmp/root-native-metadata-before.log`). Frontend lint, formatting, all three TypeScript projects, the prefixed production build, full backend Ruff/format/Ty checks, resource hashes and diff whitespace checks passed. Final delivery checks passed 71 responses each through Vite and nginx (`/tmp/root-native-preferences-system-final-resources.log`). The nginx check initially found that its automatic `/api` → `/api/` redirect bypassed the browser; an exact SPA location repaired that behavior. All 1,250 frontend tests passed across 102 files (`/tmp/root-native-preferences-system-full-frontend.log`). The historical 1,251-pass/1,137-skip backend run predates these additions; no new complete backend run is claimed. These DOM and API cases do not establish real-browser/device, live PHP differential or production-image verification. Remaining system-page query/error/locale comparisons and the broader release gates remain open.

Run the normal tests without PHP:

```bash
cd backend
GROCY_USE_NATIVE_PHP=false uv run pytest
```

With the API and web server running, check public resource links:

```bash
uv run --project backend python scripts/check-web-resources.py \
  --api-url http://127.0.0.1:8000 \
  --web-url http://127.0.0.1:8080
```

Include the configured path prefix in each URL. Add another `--web-url` to check a development server. The command sends only GET and HEAD requests. It uses `GROCY_TEST_API_KEY`, with the local `dev-key` default. Set `GROCY_TEST_SESSION_COOKIE` to also check a session cookie.

Run the separate legacy comparisons from the repository root:

```bash
GROCY_USE_NATIVE_PHP=false uv run --project backend python scripts/check-reference.py
```

The comparison command downloads Grocy 4.5.0 and checks its SHA-256 checksum. It creates a temporary database, starts the legacy server, compares public responses and saved state, and then stops the server.

The reference server requires PHP 8.1 through 8.4 with `bcmath`, `ctype`, `curl`, `dom`, `fileinfo`, `gd`, `iconv`, `intl`, `libxml`, `mbstring`, `pdo_sqlite`, `simplexml`, and `zip`.

The Python comparison environment must also load libgd; the runner rejects a missing library before starting tests. Its owned PHP server explicitly disables development assertions and records the effective settings alongside inherited interpreter settings. Standalone PHP diagnostics retain their own interpreter configuration.

Use `--timezone UTC` or `--timezone Europe/Bucharest` to set the test time zone. Use `--php /path/to/php` to select the reference executable. Use `--release /path/to/grocy_4.5.0.zip` for a local release archive. Use `--dry-run` to check the setup.

Use repeated `--feature-flag NAME=VALUE` options to set the same flags on both applications. The disabled-feature CI check selects its own cases with `PYTEST_ADDOPTS='-k disabled_feature_flags'`.

For an existing reference server, set `GROCY_REFERENCE_URL`, `GROCY_REFERENCE_API_KEY`, and `GROCY_REFERENCE_DISPOSABLE=1`. Its database must be safe to change. Set `GROCY_REFERENCE_DATABASE` when a case needs direct test setup. For a remote Python test server, use the corresponding `GROCY_BACKEND_URL`, `GROCY_BACKEND_API_KEY`, and `GROCY_BACKEND_DISPOSABLE=1` values.

## Remaining parity checklist

This checklist uses the pinned Grocy 4.5.0 `routes.php` before its `/api` group. The initial inventory contained 84 non-API route operations, including separate GET and POST login operations. Of these, 24 had directly matching frontend path shapes. Parameter names and trailing slashes were normalized for that comparison. These are route-reachability counts, not completed features or proof of equivalent behavior.

Seven further operations already have Python resource handlers: `/files/{group}/{fileName}`, `/product/{id}/grocycode`, `/recipe/{id}/grocycode`, `/chore/{id}/grocycode`, `/battery/{id}/grocycode`, `/stockentry/{id}/grocycode`, and `/stockentry/{id}/label`. They are implemented in `backend/src/grocy/api/compatibility/files.py` and `grocycode.py`, with production and development proxy rules. They must not be counted as missing frontend pages. Their authentication, headers, content, base paths, and error behavior still require the applicable resource checks.

The initial subtraction leaves 53 operations without directly matching frontend routes or those resource handlers. This is not a count of missing capabilities: modern routes and generic editors provide alternatives, and some matching paths have different meanings. At that snapshot, `/locations` showed stock by location rather than the native locations master list; the stock master-data increment restores the list and moves the additional tool to `/stockbylocation`. At the initial snapshot, `/chores` and `/batteries` presented overview-style actions; the household master-data increment now restores those lists and provides separate native overview routes. Work added after this snapshot does not change the initial inventory counts. Mark an item complete only after validating its native behavior.

### Behavior and release gates

- [ ] For every native operation above and every already matched path, record the reference controller/template, implementation, tested scenarios, and remaining exceptions. Exercise direct navigation, refresh, invalid IDs, authentication, permissions, configured base paths and disabled features. A registered route or a redirect to an unselected generic editor is insufficient evidence.
- [ ] Compare native query parameters, new-record conventions, embedded forms, `returnto` behavior, modal messages, parent/child refresh and callback lifetimes. Verify cancellation and overlapping requests, including callbacks already inside asynchronous work when the originating dialog closes.
- [ ] Finish form and table behavior across domains: defaults, dependencies, custom controls, partial writes and retries, file/picture save order, code previews, field errors, native generic-error notifications, richer receipts and undo, saved columns/filters/order, reset behavior, and translated web text. Preserve observed reference defects where required by the compatibility boundary.
- [ ] Check all supported configuration and preference effects with enabled and disabled features, supported locales and time zones. Include number/date/unit plural formatting, custom recipe columns, and rendered reports and print layouts. Separate stored-setting coverage from visible behavior coverage.
- [x] Run the current checkpoint's normal Python tests with `GROCY_USE_NATIVE_PHP=false`, frontend checks and meaningful workflow integration tests; run the full original-reference UTC/Bucharest suites and separate disabled-feature profiles with selection, skips and runtime recorded above. These results and the 86 API operations/33 listable entities do not prove complete web behavior.
- [ ] Exercise the workflows in a real browser at desktop and mobile sizes, including keyboard/focus behavior, native navigation, embedded frames, dialogs, printing and rendered reports. DOM-only integration checks do not establish browser/device behavior or visual equivalence.
- [x] Verify the measured production-image runtime, resource, native form/session and representative browser workflows at root, /house.hold and literal /house.v1+demo paths, including Python/no-PHP and GD/libmagic checks. The scope and capture limitations are recorded above.
- [ ] Verify physical camera, torch and printer behavior, and extend production-image workflow checks across the remaining native families/configurations. Report checks that could not be run rather than treating them as passes.

## Boundaries

The compatibility API must retain observed Grocy 4.5.0 behavior, including partial writes and known defects. The v2 API keeps its documented application rules. For example, the shopping purchase extension uses an atomic write for each item.

PHP version text, PHP interpreter warnings, and execution of arbitrary PHP code are properties of the reference runtime. They must not make PHP a release dependency. Equivalent product features belong in Python.

The PHP template cache and its first-request redirects are checked only on the reference runtime.

Python checks environment configuration at process startup. It rejects the checked invalid settings before it starts the HTTP server. PHP reloads `config.php` for each request and can return an HTML configuration error. The tests check the same value rules at each runtime's configuration boundary.
